Workflow State Machines

Quote status lifecycle (decided)

The authoritative quote status enum (distinct from the 4-stage progress tracker below, which is just a Draft-phase indicator):

                 ┌─────────── On-Hold ───────────┐   (manual pause; returns to prior status)
                 ▼                               │
   Draft ───finalize/send───▶ Sent ──────────────┘
     │                          │
     │ (decline whole RFQ)      ├── customer accepts online / Mark as Won ──▶ Won ──▶ (Build Order, internal)
     ▼                          ├── Mark as Lost (+ optional note) ─────────▶ Lost
  No-Quote (terminal)           ├── expiration date passes ─────────────────▶ Expired (SOFT: still viewable/acceptable)
                                └── Cancel ──────────────────────────────────▶ Cancelled
   Sent ──Create Revision──▶ Rev B (Draft, supersedes Rev A which locks as "Superseded")
   Lost / Expired / Cancelled ──Reopen──▶ Draft (in place)        Trash = soft-delete (recoverable), any status
StatusMeaning · entry · allowed transitions
DraftBeing built (the 4-stage tracker runs here). Editable. → Sent (finalize), No-Quote (decline RFQ), On-Hold, Cancelled, Trash.
On-HoldManual pause (from Draft or Sent); reversible — returns to the prior status. Editable only if it came from Draft.
SentFinalized & sent to customer. Locked (read-only) — changes require Create Revision. → Won, Lost, Expired, Cancelled, On-Hold; Resend (re-send same rev, stays Sent).
WonCustomer accepted (online acceptance or internal Mark as Won). Order is built internally via Build Order (a subset of line items + a chosen qty break each — partial orders allowed). Won does not require an order to exist.
LostNot awarded. Optional free-text reason note (no fixed list in v1).
ExpiredSoft — auto-set when the expiration date passes, but the quote stays viewable/acceptable (informational label; acceptance/checkout NOT blocked). Buyer "request requote" governed by the Requotes setting.
CancelledAbandoned by the shop; kept for audit.
No-QuoteFormally declining the entire RFQ (terminal). Distinct from a line-item No-Quote.
  • Revisions: editing a Sent quote → "Create Revision" clones it to Rev B (Draft); Rev A locks as "Superseded"; only the latest revision is active; all revisions kept in a revision history (Rev A/B/C…). ACTIONS → Create revision
  • Reopen: a closed quote (Lost / Expired / Cancelled) can be reopened in place → Draft (no new revision); re-editable, can be re-sent.
  • Win/loss is quote-level, but a Won quote can convert a subset of line items (each at a chosen qty break) into an order; line items not ordered remain on the won quote. (Line items separately carry their own No-Quote + review-step states — see below.)
  • Send preconditions (default — confirm): a contact is assigned and ≥1 line item is priced (non-no-quote). No-quoted line items send as "No Quote" (unpriced) alongside priced ones.
  • Trash vs Cancelled: Trash is a recoverable soft-delete (the "Trash" saved view) available from any status; Cancelled is a lifecycle status (abandoned but intentionally kept). Hard delete is Admin/owner only (§ Authorization).
  • ACTIONS-menu transitions (Demo H): Mark as sent, Resend, Mark as lost, Create revision, Cancel, Copy (duplicate to a new quote #, unlinked).
Defaults flagged for confirmation: On-Hold mechanics; revision labeling (Rev A/B/C vs v1/v2); send preconditions; whether On-Hold is reachable from Sent. Everything else here is per your decisions.

Quote-level workflow (4-stage tracker) — a Draft-phase progress indicator

A horizontal stepper with timestamps, shown while a quote is in Draft (it is not the status enum above). SS quote general / workflow

RFQ Received ──▶ Quote Started ──▶ Outstanding Work ──▶ Quote Sent
 (timestamp)     (timestamp)        (live count)          (on send)
   done            done        "7 Incomplete Quote Items"     --
  • RFQ Received — set when the quote is created (e.g. from email). Timestamp editable.
  • Quote Started — set when an estimator begins work.
  • Outstanding Work — derived/live: shows "N Incomplete Quote Items" and "No Open Tasks"; computed from line items not yet fully reviewed.
  • Quote Sent — set when the quote is finalized/sent (the FINALIZE QUOTE ▸ action).

Line-item status

Not Started ──▶ In Progress (%) ──▶ Complete

The dashboard renders status as a label with optional percentage and a thin progress bar ("In Progress (50%)"). Each of the five review stages on a line item carries its own independent Not Started → In Progress → Complete status and an assignee.

Quote saved-view filters (derived states)

Saved views are server-side filters over Quote/LineItem state — they imply queryable fields. SS quotes list

Quotes views

  • All Quotes
  • My Quotes
  • Due This Week
  • In-Progress
  • Outstanding
  • Unassigned
  • VIP Expiring Soon
  • Won
  • Trash
  • Workflow: Material Pricing Needed
  • Workflow: Ready for Executive Review

Line-item views

  • <User>'s Quotes (per salesperson)
  • My line items
  • My line items due this week
  • Tariff View: Material
  • Workflow: On-Hold Line Items
Saved views are user-customizable filtered tables. Build a generic view engine: a view = {entity, filter predicate, visible columns, sort}. Ship the above as system defaults; allow users to create their own.

Roles

folded from USER-STORIES-AND-WORKFLOWS.md Roles, end-to-end choreography, the remaining state machines, and the per-feature user stories with acceptance criteria (that sub-spec is archived). Acceptance binds to Acceptance Criteria + SEED-AND-FIXTURES.md goldens.
  • Salesperson — owns the account relationship; receives RFQs, assigns, sends quotes.
  • Estimator (incl. Component Estimator) — builds quotes, runs interrogation/Kalk, sets pricing.
  • Purchasing / Buyer — sources purchased components + outside services (Vendor RFQ).
  • Shop-floor / Manager — reviews manufacturability, approves before send.
  • Admin — Configure (processes, ops, materials, rules, users, integrations).
  • External buyer — the customer on the Digital Quote (checkout).   Vendor — external supplier on the Vendor RFQ portal.

End-to-end choreography (RFQ → order)

Intake — RFQ arrives via Smart RFQ form or email ({slug}@rfq.tolera.eu). Lens parses contact/part/qty; creates Quote (Draft), attaches files, runs interrogation + extraction. Salesperson assigns an estimator (Task/@mention).
Setup — Estimator confirms part fields (Lens suggestions via AI Governor), material, process; geometry interrogated; "Found in Files" + DFM warnings reviewed; Review Items raised by Rules.
Cost — Kalk runs per operation × quantity; estimator overrides where confidence is low. Line-item status → In Progress.
Source — Purchasing sends Vendor RFQs for purchased components / outside services; vendor quotes return and slot into cost.
Price — estimator applies pricing items (markup/margin), discounts, add-ons, expedite/lead-time options.
Review / Approve — line items → Completed; Manager reviews flagged Review Items; resolves before send (handoffs via Tasks/@mentions; chat history carries forward on requote).
Send — Salesperson finalizes → Digital Quote sent (or mark-sent); status → Sent.
Buy — External buyer opens Digital Quote, picks quantity + lead-time option, checks out (PO/SEPA/QR-bill) → Order (Confirmed); Quote → Won.
Fulfil — Order moves Confirmed → In Production → Shipped → Delivered; post-order changes via OrderAdjustment.

Additional state machines (formal enums)

Beyond the quote-status and line-item enums above:

Order:       Confirmed ─▶ InProduction ─▶ Shipped ─▶ Delivered      (Confirmed ─▶ Cancelled)
RFQ:         Received ─▶ Processed ─▶ (Quote created)
Task:        Open ─▶ Overdue ─▶ Resolved        (↺ Reopen)
Review item: Open ─▶ Resolved

Line-item/component rollup: quote In Progress % = completed ÷ applicable line items; any On Hold item → quote shows On Hold; No Quote counts as completed for rollup but is unselectable on the Digital Quote.

DACH — the Order / invoice path carries e-invoice (ZUGFeRD / XRechnung) and MwSt/USt; see DACH delta. Order-status tracking beyond Confirmed is ERP-owned in v1 (Orders list).

User stories & acceptance criteria

Per-feature units of work (As a <role>, I want <capability>, so that <outcome> + AC). These sit beneath the per-demo Definition-of-Done in Acceptance Criteria; numeric ACs bind to SEED-AND-FIXTURES.md goldens, visual ACs to the demo screenshots.

E1–E2 — Intake, quote setup & part analysis
  • S1.1 Email RFQ ingest — email to {slug}@rfq.tolera.eu with CAD/PDF/ZIP → Draft quote, sender matched/created as Contact, files attached + processing started, rfq_received_date set.
  • S1.2 Smart RFQ form — embeddable form submit (contact + files + qty + date) → RFQ + Draft quote; funnel (view→submit) tracked. KB: the-smart-rfq-form
  • S2.1 Part-field suggestions — Lens suggests part #/rev/desc/material/units as purple (55% opacity), apply only on Accept; never auto-applied to cost. KB: Found-in-files-extractions
  • S2.2 Geometry interrogation — Core-4 single-body file + process → dimensions/features/feedback match goldens within tolerance; assemblies decomposed first. KB: interrogations-basics
  • S2.3 Found in Files + whiteout — findings grouped into 5 categories, clickable to source, whiteout isolates a category, "build rule" available.
  • S2.4 Swap primary file — double-arrow swaps CAD↔PDF primary; blocked when part is multiply-referenced or BOM structures differ. KB: swap-primary-and-supporting-files
E3–E5 — Costing/pricing, assemblies, review
  • S3.1 Operation costing — Kalk COST/DAYS per op × qty; child costs roll up to root; match goldens (roll-up model).
  • S3.2 Override with confidence — override applies at the variable freeze point; calc value preserved separately.
  • S3.3 Markup/margin pricing items — margin Sell=Cost/(1−pct), markup Sell=Cost×(1+pct); per-category; custom via get_components().
  • S3.4 Discounts & add-ons — discount % after markups (unit×(1−d%)), add-ons after discounts; unit×qty=total.
  • S3.5 Dynamic lead times — relative days-faster + % expedite options; apply-to-all + per-item; shown highest-price-first on Digital Quote. KB: dynamic-lead-times-guide
  • S3.6 Config-change freeze — config edits don't change open drafts; Regenerate/Refresh/Bulk Refresh opt-in; overrides preserved on Refresh.
  • S4.1 BOM Builder — split → detect → extract → child BOMs → publish; published tree matches golden. KB: The-BOM-Builder
  • S4.2 Repeat part quoted once — a part with multiple nodes shares one component; make-qty from tree × requested qty; editing triggers deep-copy guard when multiply-referenced.
  • S5.1 Requirements review — a Rule raises Review Items on quote/line-item; signals include DFM warnings + Lens findings; resolved before send (Rules engine).
E6–E10 — Collaboration, send, sourcing, admin, analytics
  • S6.1 Tasks — task = assignee + due date + message (+ annotation); email + in-app notify; Tasks page filter by assignee/status/quote; reopen/resolve. KB: tasks
  • S6.2 Team vs external chat + annotations — TEAM + EXTERNAL channels; 2D/3D annotations; external share via tokened link (expiry/revoke). KB: internal-collaboration
  • S7.1 Send quote — preconditions checked (contact present, items complete or warning acknowledged); status → Sent; white-label A4 PDF (EUR + MwSt). KB: sending-quotes
  • S7.2 Buyer checkout — option selection updates price; PO/SEPA/QR-bill checkout → Order; Quote → Won; shipping/billing captured. KB: online-checkout-in-paperless-parts
  • S8.1 Outbound vendor RFQ — batch send → vendor portal (unauth, soft cutoff) → quotes return → slot into Kalk; in-flight status chip + workflow-stage warning (Vendor RFQ Portal).
  • S9.1 First-run Quick Setup — seed catalog provisions ops/processes/materials/rules; Quick Setup checklist; missing-operation-rates soft warning.
  • S9.2 Custom interrogation — per-material DFM thresholds editable, linked by material class/family/material + op; most-specific match applied. KB: custom-interrogations
  • S10.1 Analytics query builder — measures aggregate by dimensions + time + filters, save to dashboards; win rate + draft-to-send tiles default. KB: analytics

ColumnContent

Columns are configurable (the gear). Sortable, filterable. Row → opens quote.

3 · Bottom-middle "Review items"

Empty state mirror; lists review items assigned to the current user. "View all" / paginated.

4 · Right "Notifications" panel

Reverse-chronological feed with "Mark all as read". Two notification archetypes observed:

  • New Quote created from Email Forwarding — "Created Quote #15217" + relative time. Generated by the email-ingest pipeline.
  • New RFQ received — "<person/company> submitted a new RFQ as Quote #1510" + timestamp.
Global search must do full-text over: quote number, contact, part number, PDF/drawing text, notes, keywords. This is a hard requirement implied by the placeholder text and is what makes the Part Library and "Found in Files" features work.

Quotes & Saved Views

Route: /quotes · SS 02 / 4589d67a

  • Right: search ("Search quotes by number, RFQ, and more") + CREATE QUOTE button.

Quote status folders & lifecycle

Quotes live in one of seven folders. Transitions are mostly manual except where noted:

FolderMeaningHow a quote enters
DraftsIn-progress, not yet sentCreated (manually or by email ingest/Smart RFQ)
OutstandingSent to customer, awaiting responseAutomatically on Finalize Quote → Send
AcceptedCustomer placed an orderAutomatically when customer checks out on the digital quote; an Order is simultaneously created on the Orders page
ExpiredPast the expiration dateAutomatically when the quote's expiration date passes (default 30 days from send); the digital-quote link remains live but customer cannot interact. Requote available.
LostCustomer chose another supplierManually via Actions → Change Status; preserves data for win/loss analysis
CancelledNo longer relevantManually via Actions → Change Status
TrashSoft-deletedManually; recoverable by copying the deleted quote back to Drafts
Expiration: the default is 30 days but is configurable per quote. After expiry the digital-quote link is still reachable, but the customer can only request a re-quote. A re-quote auto-drafts a new quote pre-filled with the original pricing as a reference baseline (estimators can then adjust). The shop receives an email notification when a re-quote is requested. Build divergence: Bid Factory uses soft-expiry — acceptance remains enabled past the expiry date (see Settings → Requotes).

Quote actions (Actions menu)

ActionBehaviour
Copy QuoteCreates a new Draft with a new quote number, preserving all line items, operations, quantities, materials, and prices from the source quote.
Create RevisionCreates a new revision (new quote number) of an Outstanding/Accepted quote. The original is moved to Expired. Quantity adjustments made in the Facilitate Order window persist to the new revision. View Revisions lists the full history.
Resend QuoteAvailable on Outstanding quotes. Accessible via Last Viewed → Resend Quote or Actions → Resend Quote. Prompts for an email template then re-sends the existing digital-quote link.
Bulk Update StatusesMulti-select quotes, apply a status change in one action.
ExportExport quote data and line item information to CSV (see Settings).

Sending a quote

Preview — "Preview" button (top right of Quote Detail) opens the buyer-facing digital quote view.
Finalize Quote — opens the send composer: To / CC / BCC recipients, select email template, editable subject + body (merge-filled, includes the secure digital-quote link), "Include quote PDF in email" toggle.
Send Quote — quote moves from Drafts → Outstanding. Customer receives email. The digital-quote link is now live for checkout.
Quote number is auto-incremented sequentially and cannot be changed in the UI (backend-only, configurable starting number). Even a quote drafted and then trashed increments the counter.

Line Item Management (Quotes & Line Items sidebar)

Introduced July 2024 (Power Lunch Ep 25). The left sidebar in the Quotes list splits into two sub-sections — Quotes and Line Items — each with its own set of saved views. Replaces the need for external spreadsheets and Rolling Action Item Logs.

Why it exists — key workflow questions answered

  • What are the most important parts for me to quote today?
  • Which parts don't currently have an owner (estimator)?
  • Which parts/quotes are just waiting for me to review them?
  • Which quotes do I need to follow up on (outstanding > N days)?
  • How are each of my facilities doing?

Line item ownership

Individual line items (parts) can be assigned to a specific estimator independently of the quote-level estimator. This enables parallel work on large RFQ packages — different team members own different parts within the same quote. The "Which parts don't have an owner?" view surfaces unassigned parts across all open quotes.

Line item status tracking

Each line item carries its own status (Not Started → In Progress → Complete) independent of the parent quote's workflow tracker. The quote-level "Line Item Status" column in the grid shows an aggregate bar (e.g. "In Progress — 3/8 complete"). Build implication: LineItem needs its own status field and assignee_id distinct from the quote-level estimator_id.

Build implications: LineItem adds assignee_id (user FK, nullable), line_item_status enum (not_started | in_progress | complete | needs_review), and priority. Saved views are user-owned filter + sort presets (SavedView with view_scope: quotes | line_items, filters JSONB, sort, owner_id). The "Overdue" and "Outstanding" system views are derived from quote/due dates, not stored. Multi-facility filtering uses the existing facility_id.

RFQ Receive Date & Turnaround Tracking (Ep 22)

When a quote is created from a forwarded email, the RFQ Receive Date field is auto-populated from the email's timestamp — not today's date, not user-entered. This is critical: the email may have sat in the salesperson's inbox for hours before being forwarded to Paperless Parts, and using the ingest timestamp would inflate the apparent turnaround time.

Turnaround time = Quote Submitted Date − RFQ Receive Date. Historically PP customers report 3–5 min/line item for straightforward quotes; multi-day for large packages. Accurate receive date is the prerequisite for meaningful turnaround-time analytics.

Build implication: Quote.rfq_received_at is a distinct field from Quote.created_at. When the quote originates from an inbound email, rfq_received_at is set to the email's Date: header (UTC). When created from local files or direct entry, default to created_at (or leave nullable and let the user set it). Do not conflate the two in turnaround KPI queries.

The Edit Dimensions dialog has X (red) / Y / Z (blue) inputs, Volume (Raw Value + Removal %), and Area, with Swap units. When a print is present, dimensions are best set by clicking the AI-extracted callout on the drawing (in the Part Setup panel) rather than typing. Inputs accept math expressions (e.g. 2.27 + .359 auto-evaluates) and typed units (e.g. "1 meter") that auto-convert. SS DemoH 06/07

PartGeometry attribute catalog — the geometry ↔ Kalk contract

folded from PartGeometry-Attribute-Catalog.md The single most load-bearing contract in the product — simultaneously the output schema of GeometryService and the input surface of Kalk's part object (object model). Every consumer (3D viewer, DFM, costing, nesting, Part-Library hashing) reads these same names/units. Source: KB: operation-p3l-cheat-sheet. Metric by default; imperial after units_in() (store metric, convert at read). v1 = OCCT acceptance checklist: ✓ yes · ~ partial/family · man manual/non-geo · asm assembly-derived.

Core geometry & dimensions · Material

AttributeDescriptionMetric / Imperialv1
part.size_x/y/zresolved dim — 3D: overwrite › optimal-bbox › AABB; 2D: file/overwrite (z = thickness); non-geo: manualmm / in✓
part.max_dim / med_dim / min_dimmax / median / min dim of the optimal bounding boxmm / in✓
part.area · part.volumesurface area · volumemm²/in² · mm³/in³✓
part.weight · part.densitypart weight · material densityg/lb · g/cm³÷lb/in³✓ / man
part.material / global_material / material_family / material_classmaterial name (custom + global DB), family (Aluminum…), class (Metal…)—man
part.mat_cost_per_volume · part.mat_added_lead_timematerial cost per volume · added lead-time from process config$/mm³÷$/in³ · daysman

Store both the raw extraction and any override for size_* so the precedence (overwrite › optimal-bbox › AABB › manual) stays reproducible and auditable.

Quantities · Identity & assembly position · Purchased component

AttributeDescriptionv1
part.qty · part.bom_qty · part.innate_quantitymake qty (current break) · deliver qty (break) · deliver qty for one top-level part✓

part.part_number · part.revisionidentityman

part.purchased_component.oem_part_number, .internal_part_number, .piece_price (4dp), .description + custom columns (e.g. .insertion_time); or Noneasm

Analyzer-derived geometry (NOT on the static part object) comes from analyze_*() results — .features[*].properties.* + family scalars (.thickness, .pierce_count, .total_cut_length, .stock_radius, milling .runtime…). See the per-family family_scalars + DFM catalogue.

DACH — map material / material_family to DIN/EN designations (Werkstoffnummer, e.g. 1.4301 = X5CrNi18-10 = 304); seed a DIN↔global-material crosswalk. Store metric, present metric-first.

Selection data overlay

3D viewer — tool & display contract

folded from VIEWER-AND-FILE-TYPES.md The capability contract for the 3D viewer (renders what GeometryService tessellates). Build-vs-buy: build the 3D viewer on the GeometryService tessellation (three.js-class WebGL). KB: 3d-viewer-tools · KB: part-viewer-display-options
ToolBehaviourv1?
Selectionclick edge/face → geometric props; annotate the face into chat✓
Measuredistance between features; caliper on parallel faces / concentric cylinders; angle between two faces. Exact on parallel planes / concentric cylinders / perpendicular cylinder+plane; otherwise approximate (leading ~) — preserve this trust signal.✓

Display options (gear): units toggle (dims/area/volume in↔mm, weight lb↔kg — Tolera default metric); native-model-colours/opacity toggle (restores feature-highlight contrast when export colours clash); user-set decimal precision.

3D rendering limits

  • Vertex budget 25 M concurrent. Over budget across multiple bodies → smallest/least-significant bodies become blue bounding-box simplified reps (blue cube icons in the tree); isolate a subassembly to restore.
  • Single body over budget → orange bounding-box rep (orange cube icons); such a body also exceeds interrogation limits → no feature results display.
  • File-size limit: PP's own docs disagree (3D viewer cites 250 MB; supported-file-types 150 MB; Lens ≤250 MB). BF decision: one platform MAX_UPLOAD_MB = 200 (aligned to the email-ingest cap — see Supported file types), with a soft-warning band approaching it. KB: 3D-viewing-limits
DACH — viewer opens in mm / kg / deg; comma-decimal per de-DE / de-CH / de-AT; ISO GPS symbology in GD&T highlighting; never default to imperial.

PDF / 2D Drawing Viewer + Part Setup + Extraction

Route: /quotes/viewer/:id?part_setup=&quote_item_id= · SS 09 / b8397caf / Demo C: part-setup, popover, whiteout, add-missing

Full-screen engineering-drawing viewer where the estimator "steps through line items and parts to consider extractions and enter part and costing information — keeps focus on prints when they exist." Breadcrumb: "Quote #8511 DRAFT / Line 3 ▣ 3601215 REV C". Line-item pager top-right: "3 of 7 items", SKIP TO ▾, PREVIOUS / NEXT.

folded from VIEWER-AND-FILE-TYPES.md §4 The PDF/print viewer is where Lens findings overlay and estimators mark up prints. Build-vs-buy: buy the PDF engine (Apryse/PDFTron-class) — decide the SDK before the viewer milestone. KB: pdf-viewer-guide

Full capability set to ship

  • View / navigate: continuous / page-by-page; single / double / cover-facing spread; rotate doc or page; pan (P); zoom (% / Cmd± / fit-width / fit-page / marquee Z).
  • Search: in-document (case + whole-word); global search across all PDFs in the Parts Library.
  • Pages: multi-select (1,3,5 or 1-5); rotate / extract (split one-page-per-part) / delete.
  • Compare PDFs (revision diff): red = removed, blue = added, black = identical; eye toggle. Canonical use: rev B vs rev C → re-run Lens BOM/extraction.
  • Annotate / Shapes: underline / highlight / rectangle / text / free-hand / note / squiggly / strikeout / eraser; rectangle / line / polyline / arrow / arc / ellipse / polygon; presets; undo/redo.
  • Measure: distance / arc / perimeter / area / count; snapping. Set scale first — pick a ratio or calibrate from one known dimension. Save annotations through Collaboration (threads to the quote chat).
  • Redact text / sections / pages → new supporting file (original retained), recolourable fill; share the redacted file only via scoped external collaboration.
  • Tips: collapsed sidebars persist across sessions; download with annotations; viewer language; dark mode.
DACH — PDF viewer language German by default; redaction → new-file + scoped share is the GDPR-safe mechanism to send prints to vendors without leaking PII/IP (pairs with Lens PII detection on ingest).

Toolbar (left → right)

hamburger/layers · panel-layout toggle · page/thumbnail toggle · zoom % (84%) with − / + · fit-to-screen · fit-width · region-select · hand/pan · marquee-select · View ▾ mode · search · Extractions ☑ (show/hide all overlays) · grid/table icon · pencil/edit · Units [in ▾].

Extraction overlay (GD&T & callouts)

Bid Factory paints extracted entities directly onto the drawing: datums, feature control frames, holes, countersinks, counterbores, dimensions, tolerances — alongside title-block fields, material, specs, finishes, and note keywords. Each is clickable. KB: quoting-from-pdfs--tips-and-tricks KB: PDF-Extraction-Beta

Callout popover

Clicking an extraction (on the print, or its pill in "Found in files") opens a popover: a plain-language description ("Position control frame with 0.005" tolerance in reference to datums A and B"), the source ("Found on page 1 of 3601215-C.pdf"), and two actions: Mark as inaccurate ✶ (red) and Create rule (blue). The plain-language text doubles as GD&T training for junior estimators. SS popover

"Part Setup" panel (right)

The print-side data-entry panel. Two tabs: Part fields and Found in files [n] (badge = extraction count, e.g. 43). A NUM / REV radio toggle (top-right edge) governs which title-block field the click-to-fill applies to. Each content section has a Whiteout toggle. Sections:

Part Number / Revision / Description fields carry AI-suggestion sparkles (✦); dimensions (X/Y/Z) can be set here too (click extraction or type, incl. math/units).

Whiteout mode

Toggling Whiteout (per section or global) clears callout clutter so the bare part geometry is readable — useful on note-dense prints ("a snowstorm of callouts"). You can spotlight a single callout while whited-out to locate it in the cluster. A bottom page nav (◀ "Sheet 1" [page#] ▶) handles multi-sheet drawings. SS whiteout

Extraction correction & AI-training loop

Because extraction is AI-driven and imperfect, two correction actions feed training data:

  • Mark as inaccurate — removes a wrong extraction (from the popover).
  • Add missing extraction — pencil tool → draw a region over a missed callout → modal: Callout Type (Dimension), Dimension Type (Linear), Value (e.g. 0.784), Include tolerance, Include multiplier; CANCEL / ADD DIMENSION. Adds it to the system and labels training data. SS add-missing modal

Units detection

The system reads units off the print ("we found that on the print"), sets the Units selector, and appends units to dimensions. It parses alternate-unit ratios when both are shown; users can override in/mm (reloads re-render values in the chosen unit).

Review Items panel

The same drawing viewer hosts the rule-driven Review Items panel (toggled via the double-chevron icon). Filters: Unresolved ▾ / Anybody ▾ / Any Type ▾. Detailed in § Rules & Review Items.

The Redact tool (View modes) lets users hide ITAR/CUI/proprietary content before sharing a drawing externally — important given the ITAR flags and "Contains ITAR/CUI" findings.

Part Library (historical match)

Nav: Parts · Modal from "N matching parts" · SS 09 / b7b3fc3d / 1c31d6f4 KB: navigate-and-manage-the-part-library KB: uploading-parts-to-your-part-library

Every part ever quoted is indexed. When a new part is added, the system finds historical matches so estimators can reuse prior pricing/routers. Modal title: "2526 Matching Parts", with the subject part preview (thumbnail, file name, part #, rev) and match buckets:

Match categoryMeaningExample count
Exact File Matchsame CAD file hash1509
Exact Geometric Matchidentical geometry signature1542
File Name Matchsame filename141
Part Number Matchsame part number1488
Similar Geometriesfuzzy geometric similarity917

Each match expands to 3D thumbnail cards: Part # + Rev, file name, and historical QUOTES count (7438 / 5957 / 4995) — click-through to prior quotes. Selecting a match copies its router, operations, and all manual overrides onto the current part (the overrides are stored per quote-item and surfaced when viewing historical matches — this is the core time-saving value: skip from-scratch estimating on repeat geometry).

How matching works — geometry engine

Every file is processed asynchronously after upload. The processing pipeline produces several indexes per part, each serving a different match category:

Match categoryIndex typeHow computedDB implication
Exact File MatchFile content hashSHA-256 (or similar) of the raw uploaded bytes — computed before any processingpart_files.file_hash — indexed, unique lookup
Exact Geometric MatchGeometry signatureA canonical fingerprint of the normalized solid geometry — computed by the geometry kernel (OCCT). Same manufactured geometry produces the same signature regardless of originating CAD software, face tessellation, or file format. PP's geometry engine is designed to handle topology variation (e.g., a hole described as one 360° face vs two split 180° faces) and still produce the same signature.part_files.geometry_signature — indexed, deterministic hash
File Name MatchTokenized filenameNormalised filename string (strip extension, lowercase, tokenize) — compared against all existing part filenamespart_files.filename_normalized — indexed text column
Part Number MatchPart number indexPart number extracted from the file (title block text extraction for PDFs, CAD metadata for STEP/IGES) and from the manually entered Part Library part numberpart_files.part_number_extracted — indexed; also joins against parts.part_number
Similar GeometriesFeature vector (embedding)A numeric vector encoding manufacturing-relevant geometric properties: bounding box dimensions, volume, surface area, hole count, bend count, thin-wall flags, aspect ratio, etc. — computed by OCCT interrogation. Fuzzy nearest-neighbour search over this vector returns geometrically similar but non-identical parts.part_files.geometry_vector — vector column requiring pgvector (or equivalent ANN index). Nearest-neighbour query with cosine or L2 distance. This is the one index that requires a vector extension.

Processing pipeline (per file upload)

Ingest — store raw file, compute file_hash, trigger async geometry job.
Geometry kernel (OCCT) — parse the CAD file; extract solid geometry. For PDF files, no 3D geometry — skip geometry indexes; proceed to text extraction only.
Compute indexes — (a) geometry_signature from the normalized B-rep; (b) geometry_vector from the feature extraction (volume, area, holes, bends…); (c) extract part_number_extracted from model metadata or title block.
Text extraction — extract all text from PDF (and from CAD model metadata). Store in part_files.pdf_text for full-text search.
Match query — on insert, run all five match lookups; store results in a part_matches cache table (or surface on-demand). Results available when the user opens the line item.
Thumbnail — render a 3D thumbnail for the part card (three.js or server-side render of the STEP geometry).
Key build risk: the "Similar Geometries" fuzzy match is the hardest. PP has patented their part-similarity engine (patent 11947491 "Apparatus and methods for geometric searching"; plus two later patents on de-identified part design search). Bid Factory must implement a comparable approach — the simplest viable version is a feature vector of geometric scalars (OCCT-computed) stored in a pgvector column with an IVFFlat or HNSW index. This is the primary reason to confirm pgvector support in the database choice. If the stack uses Postgres (confirmed), pgvector is the natural fit. The exact feature vector schema should be decided during the geometry sprint. ✓ v1 scope (decided): The scalar feature vector approach (OCCT-computed geometric scalars → pgvector ANN search) ships in v1. The "geometry-vector similarity" in § Deliberately out of v1 refers to the learned/deep-learning embedding approach (PP-patented); the deterministic scalar vector ships first. If scalar similarity proves insufficient, a Claude vision pass over the 3D thumbnail can generate a richer embedding — integrate via the same pgvector column.

What "selecting a historical match" does

When an estimator clicks a match card and chooses "Import historical work", the system copies:

Copied dataSource
Operations (router)The quote-item's operation list from the historical quote
Manual overridesAll non-default variable values (highlighted in yellow in the UI — e.g. adjusted runtime, custom setup count)
Pricing variablesUnit prices, operation costs, markups as they stood in the historical quote

This is also why the Operations interface was designed this way — it mirrors ERP operation records, making the historical reuse data clean enough to transfer. The overrides feed back to improve PP's geometry algorithms (manual overrides are signals that the auto-estimate was off).

DB model: QuoteItem → OperationInstance (operation_def_id, variable_overrides JSONB, is_overridden boolean[]). When importing historical, copy the OperationInstance rows to the new QuoteItem. The variable_overrides JSONB stores per-variable {value, source: 'manual'|'auto'} so the UI can highlight yellow anything with source='manual'.

Uploading & organising parts

Two upload methods from the Parts Library view: (1) Upload New Part button — file browser; (2) drag and drop anywhere on the page. Files must be supported types; multi-component parts must be exported correctly before upload.

Auto-bundling: files with matching names but different extensions (e.g. Bracket.stp + Bracket.pdf) are automatically bundled into a single part. The CAD file becomes the primary file (source of geometry). If names don't match, files come in as separate parts and must be manually merged.

Merging parts manually: Select Parts → select files → Actions → Merge Parts as Supporting Files → choose primary file (CAD preferred) → Merge. Non-primary file becomes a supporting file.

Part properties (set in Part Viewer): Part Number, Revision, Material, Description. Part Viewer also hosts: measurements, teammate messaging, and supporting file attachments (Upload Files button, view by clicking filename).

Two part/revision number types

There are two independent part/rev numbers that can differ:

TypeEdited fromBehaviour
Part Library part/revPart ViewerThe canonical identifier; stored on the Part record
Quote Item part/revBuild-a-Quote line itemQuote-specific override. If no Part Library number exists, updating this also sets the library number. Global search finds either.

Part lifecycle

StateHow to enterEffect on existing quotes
Active (Team Parts)Default after upload—
ArchivedSelect Parts → Actions → ArchiveNone — quotes retain all interrogation data. Archived parts can be added to new quotes from the Archived tab.
DeletedFrom Archived → Actions → DeleteQuote shows "File Deleted" placeholder; all dimensions and costing are preserved. Quotes cannot be downloaded for deleted parts. Irreversible.
Deep copy behaviour: deep-copying an archived part creates a new part in the Archived folder. Deleting a part removes it from the library but does not break completed quotes — pricing and routing are preserved in the quote record.

Historical data import (SFTP)

Existing part files can be bulk-imported into the Part Library via SFTP. Process: generate public/private key pair (PuTTYgen) → share public key with PP support → PP creates an SFTP account (1–2 business days) → upload files via WinSCP. Folder structure is preserved as metadata (customer name, job number) which feeds the search index. Indexing takes up to one week after upload completes. Recommended for shops migrating from paper/ERP archives.

Full-text search

The global search bar indexes across all of the following simultaneously:

Searchable fieldNotes
Part number (library level)Both Part Library and Quote Item level numbers are matched
Quote number / Order number—
Contact informationName, company, email
Processes, operations, finishes, add-ons—
Quote notesFree-text notes on the quote
PDF / drawing textFull-text index of all text inside uploaded PDFs/drawings — enables "Found in Files" feature; hard requirement for Part Library repeat-part discovery
Build implication: PDF text must be extracted at upload time and added to the search index (not just stored). This is what makes searching for a part number that only exists in a drawing title block possible.

Supported file types

Bid Factory must accept the same file types as PP. Three tiers determine how much the system can do with a file:

TierFormatsCapabilities
Standard BRep (full interrogation)STEP: .step, .stp, .stpz · JT: .jtFull geometry interrogation, all 7 process families, DFM warnings, pgvector embedding, thumbnail, flatten
Converted BRep (full interrogation, requires kernel import)Autodesk Inventor: .ipt · ACIS: .sat, .sab · Rhino: .3dm · CATIA V4: .exp, .model · CATIA V5: .catpart, .catshape · Solid Edge: .par, .psm · Parasolid: .x_b, .x_t · SolidWorks: .sldprt · Creo: .neu, .prt · NX: .prt · IFC: .ifcSame as Standard BRep — requires licensed import kernel (Spatial/HOOPS); note .prt is used by both Creo and NX (disambiguate by header)
Mesh (viewable, limited interrogation)STL: .stl · 3MF: .3mf · 3D PDF · Autodesk: .3ds, .dwf, .dwfx · CATIA: .cgr, .3dxml · COLLADA: .dae · GLB · FBX · OBJ · PRC · VRML/WRLAdditive interrogation (bounding box, volume, orientation); no feature recognition for subtractive processes
2D Flat / VectorDXF · DWG · SVG · PDF (vector) · TIF/TIFF · JPEG/PNGPDF text extraction (title block, notes); waterjet/laser flat-feature recognition from DXF/DWG/vectorized PDF; 2D viewer; sheet-metal interrogation from DXF/DWG/vectorized PDF only
Document & attachment (stored, no interrogation)Office: .pptx, .xlsx, .ods, .odp · Spreadsheet: .csv · Plain text: .txt · Email: .msg, .eml (attachments also parsed)Full-text indexed for search; email attachments extracted as supporting files; shown in Files tab
Pack-and-go ZIP (multi-body assemblies)SolidWorks: .sldprt + .sldasm · Creo: .prt + .asm · CATIA: .catpart + .catproduct · Inventor: .ipt + .iamUnpacked by the system; each unique geometry becomes a Part; nodes mirror the assembly tree from the CAD file
ConstraintValue
Max file size200 MB per file (BF target — the reference product caps at 150 MB; BF raises this to 200 MB to match the email ingest pipeline limit)
Interrogation file size limitsSheet Metal / Tube Laser: up to 50 MB · CNC Milling / Lathe / Cast Urethane / Wire EDM: up to 20 MB · Additive: unlimited
IGS/IGESGeometry is "healed" before interrogation — minor modifications equivalent to normal CAD processing; treat same as STEP for purposes
Best practiceRecommend STEP + native CAD for maximum interrogation value; Sheet Metal: DXF/DWG/vectorized PDF for 2D flat interrogation
Interrogation scopeOnly single-body, non-assembly parts can be interrogated. Assembly files are parsed for tree structure but individual bodies are not separately interrogated through the assembly wrapper.
Build implication: The file ingestion pipeline must detect file type from extension + magic bytes, route to the correct geometry worker tier, and handle the pack-and-go ZIP extraction before geometry processing. A FileType enum drives the pipeline branch decision. STEP is the only format that needs zero licensing — all native CAD formats require the Spatial/HOOPS import kernel. folded: VIEWER-AND-FILE-TYPES.md §5 Explicit interrogation-supported extensions: exp, step, stp, sldprt, iges, igs, ifc, catpart, catshape, cgr, 3dm, x_b, x_t, ipt, jt, model, neu, prt, psm, sab, sat, par, stpz. Pack-and-go requires both the assembly file (instructions) and the part files (geometry), else "unable to display"; support drag-drop of whole nested folders. IGS/non-solid "healing" raises a "Resolved error" warning; downloading a Resolved-error file returns the original unmodified file. KB: supported-file-types

BOM Builder (PDF → hierarchical BOM)

SS 07 / fb3a38f2 + Demo D 02,06–12 KB: The-BOM-Builder KB: intro-to-the-assembly-toolkit KB: building-boms-additional-faq

For complex assemblies, Bid Factory parses BOM tables out of customer PDFs into an editable multi-level bill of materials. Opens as a near-full-screen modal overlay from a file's "Create line item"/BOM badge or the line-item banner. Header: title, "Draft saved just now" + trash, CHECK BOM (validate only), CHECK AND PUBLISH (primary), ✕. Toolbar: UNDO / REDO, Expand all / Collapse all, search ("Search by part #, description, files, or type"). Left = Quote Files pane (RFQ / Vendor Quotes / Supporting Files, same as the quote). Footer counter "N/1000 unique parts" (per-account capacity).

Guided workflow (Demo D)

Split PDF the assembly package → 52 single-page files; Lens tags pages with BOM tables (purple icon).
Assign the parent file to the quote (creates the root line item).
Open BOM Builder — Lens has already recognized the root BOM table and extracted root part #, item numbers, part numbers, qtys, descriptions.
Add Files — Lens matches the split pages to line items by the part number in each drawing's title block and attaches them as supporting files. Banner: "Add files to matching parts? — 52 quote files match 10 parts".
Accept Child BOM suggestions — Lens finds nested sub-assembly BOM tables in child drawings; accepting expands a flat row into a subassembly with auto-created children.
Components Library matching — purchased parts auto-match to records in the org's components library.
Check and Publish → "Successfully published BOM" toast; returns to the line item with an "Open in BOM Builder" banner for future edits.

Grid columns & row types

Columns: Item No. · Part Number · Rev · Primary File (initial) / Type (after Add Files) · Description · Qty. Each row has a six-dot drag handle (reorder) and, on hover, "⋮ +" (kebab + add-child).

TypeIconMeaning
Assembly rootgrid/matrix (purple)top-level assembly (once per BOM), e.g. 002-00001 Rev 000
Subassembly▾ + grid (purple)parent with children; expands to decimal items (2 → 2.1…2.8)
Manufacturedgrey cubefabricated/machined part
Purchasedgear + green dotbought part matched to the Components Library (green dot = active match)

Item No. uses decimal-dot notation reflecting depth (1, 1.1…1.7, 2, 2.1…2.8, 6.1…6.5). A purple sparkle on a row + tooltip "Child BOM suggestion available" indicates an unaccepted nested BOM. The unique-parts counter climbs as subassemblies are accepted (10 → 25 → 43 …).

Child BOM suggestion

Per-row prompt when Lens detects a sub-assembly BOM table inside that part's drawing. Accepting converts a Manufactured row into a Subassembly and inserts its children as nested line items (recursively). This is how a flat 10-row root BOM becomes a deep multi-level tree.

Validation & publish

CHECK BOM validates structure (missing files, unmatched parts) without committing. CHECK AND PUBLISH commits: the tree becomes BomNode records on the line item, the modal closes, and a success toast shows. A persistent banner "BOM table found in <file>, page N" + OPEN IN BOM BUILDER remains on the line item to re-edit.

Build implications: (1) a PDF page-splitter; (2) a Lens BOM-table detector (per page) + extractor (item/part/qty/description from table + title block); (3) part-number-based file→node matching using title-block extraction; (4) recursive child-BOM detection with accept/reject; (5) a Components Library match service for purchased parts; (6) draft autosave + undo/redo on the tree; (7) a per-account unique-parts cap. Works the same for machined and fabricated assemblies.

Assembly Data Model — Terminology

KB article: Assemblies – Data types and terminology KB: assemblies-data-types-and-terminology KB: purchased-components

Understanding the object model is critical for correct BOM rendering and pricing roll-up. These are the canonical PP terms that map to database entities:

TermDefinitionKey properties
PartAny physical thing designed for a specific form or functiongeometry, part_number, revision, files
AssemblyAlso a Part; a structured collection of multiple individual partshas child parts via nodes
Root PartThe top-level part in an assembly treeno parent
Child PartAny part in an assembly that is not the root partconnected via a node
TreeDisplay of the structural hierarchy of parent/child relationshipsflat BOM shows unique parts; child BOM shows nodes
NodeAn occurrence of a part in a tree — describes both location and quantityparent_node_id, part_id, quantity, flat_qty
Root NodeThe special node linked to the root part; always quantity = 1, no parentalways present, one per tree
BOMBill of Materials — multiple views exist (flat, child, multi-level)flat BOM = unique objects; child BOM = node occurrences
ComponentA Part + pricing information; has materials, operations, customer quantitiestied to one part + one quote scope
Root ComponentThe component tied to the root part. Pricing items/add-ons/expedites configured here; child costs roll up here; shown to customer on digital quote= Quote Item in the UI
Child ComponentAny component that is not the root componentcosts roll up to parent component
Quote ItemLinks a root component to a quote — equivalent to root component in the UIquote_id, root_component_id, customer_qty
Flat QtyTotal count of a part/component in an assembly for customer requested qty = 1computed from node quantities up the tree

BOM building actions — what each does under the hood

When an estimator takes a BOM action in the quote, the system performs multiple object mutations simultaneously. Key examples:

UI actionObjects created/modified
Add a manufactured child to a subassembly(1) New Part, (2) New Node linking Part to parent Part, (3) New Component storing pricing
Upload CAD model to a quoteCreate Parts for each unique geometry; create Nodes mirroring the CAD file's tree; create Components per part; set customer requested quantity
Add a library part to a quote (not previously quoted)Create Components for each part in the tree; set root component customer qty = 1
Move a node down a BOM levelChange parent_node_id; recalculate BOM quantity, deliver quantity, make quantity, and pricing for the child and all ancestors
DB model implications: Part (geometry, files), Node (part_id, parent_node_id, quantity, flat_qty), Component (node_id, quote_scope_id, materials[], operations[], customer_qty), QuoteItem (quote_id, root_component_id). Flat qty must be recomputed on any node move. Pricing is applied only at the root component level and displayed on the digital quote; child component costs roll up automatically.

Assembly Components & Bulk Edit

SS 07/13 + 9e29bca8 / d6928e11 KB: quoting-children-of-assemblies-as-separate-parts KB: add-an-existing-part-in-your-library-to-an-assembly-bom

When a line item is an assembly, the part view shows an Assembly Components section with a search, a Grouped toggle, and FLAT BOM / CHILD BOM view switch. Components are grouped:

  • Sub-assemblies (n) — expandable; show "N children".
  • Manufactured Components (n) — each a row: thumbnail, name (e.g. BOT WASHER:1.STEP), Node Qty, rollup cost, remove/expand/⋮.
  • Purchased Components (n) — with supplier badges (e.g. DB Roberts), part flags; "No purchased components" empty state.

Each level shows "Showing rollup cost to parent" (or "Showing cost without rollup") and a Node Qty column; a Flat Qty column shows the flattened per-assembly quantity. Per-qty cost columns appear to the right. Actions: ADD COMPONENTS TO TOP LEVEL ▾, EDIT BOM, BUILD BOM STRUCTURE, ADD PURCHASED COMPONENTS. Component Summary rolls up qty + cost across the quantity columns.

Component context menu (⋮)

Each component row's ⋮ menu: Update process / material / finish Update workflow status Copy pricing — and for a manufactured component Convert to purchased component Delete component. SS DemoL 02

Convert to Purchased Component (Smart Match)

"Convert to purchased component" opens a modal that matches the component against the OEM catalog + previously-used purchased components, rather than inline editing. SS DemoL 03

  • Header "Converting Assembly Component" (thumbnail, Part #, Rev, filename). Tabs: Smart Matches (AI/geometry-filtered) | All (full catalog).
  • Previously Used Purchased Components — result cards each showing OEM Part # + Internal Part #, a brand logo (e.g. PEM), and match indicators: OEM Part Number Match OEM Geometric Match Historical Geometric Match (N) — each ✓/✗, with the historical count (e.g. "(21)") of how often that geometry was matched before. Expandable "Product Specifications".
  • Unlinked OEM Products (geometric matches not yet linked to OEM records) and a Create New Component fallback for no-match.
  • CANCEL / CONVERT (CONVERT disabled until a match is selected).
Persistent geometry memory (build behavior): once a part is identified as a purchased component (by OEM part number or exact geometry), the system auto-tags the same geometry as purchased in all future quotes — the estimator never re-converts the same fastener. Model a geometry/part-number → purchased-component mapping at org level (this is the same signature/hash service behind the Part Library).

BOM navigation & reordering SS DemoM

  • Single-level drag-and-drop reorder: turn the Grouped toggle off (in CHILD BOM) → rows get drag handles → reorder components within one level to match the customer's parts-list order; reflected in the left tree. This is order-only confirmation, not cross-level BOM restructuring (moving a node to another level is not supported via drag).
  • Breadcrumb navigation: a deep child shows a breadcrumb (root ▸ ASM-654 ▸ PP-137-4) — click the parent to go up a level; a sibling dropdown jumps between parts at the same level without using the tree.
  • Unique per-component URLs: every BOM node has a stable deep link (…/quotes/edit/:id/:rootItem/:childId?nodeId=…) — copy/share it to send a colleague straight to that exact component.
  • Thumbnail hover: hovering a tree/row item shows a 3D thumbnail + Node Qty + status — identify near-identical parts (e.g. many hex nuts) by geometry, not just filename.
  • Component Overview (per child): a Qty × Cost matrix — Qty as Main / BOM / Make, Cost as Unit / Total / Self / Rollup (self = the part alone; rollup = including its children).
Performance target: BOM manipulation (move, add/convert purchased component, add file) must stay fast and not time out for large BOMs (>100 components) — async, ~3× the naive speed. Belongs in the non-functional set.

Fixing auto-classification (verify before costing)

On upload, any multi-component CAD is auto-classified as an assembly/sub-assembly (the system can't always tell a made part from bought hardware). Best practice: verify each node's type icon (sub-assembly grid / manufactured cube / purchased gear) before costing, and correct via the ⋮ menu:

  • Convert to Purchased Component — a node that's really bought hardware → see Smart Match above.
  • Replace Assembly with Manufactured Component ("part with hardware") — when an auto-detected sub-assembly is actually one manufactured part that consumes purchased hardware: pick which child is the parent made-part; the others become its consumed purchased components, and the redundant sub-assembly router level is removed. Powerful for cleaning up over-nested CAD.

Copy Pricing

Copy a costed component's Material and/or Operations (incl. overrides) onto other components — to siblings in the same quote or, via "Choose different quote", to components in another quote (browse quote → line item → component). Checkboxes select what to copy; CANCEL / COPY PRICING. Use for left/right-hand or otherwise identical parts. SS DemoM / DemoL copy-pricing panel

Limitation (Q&A): copy pricing is one source component → one target at a time — no multi-component or whole-assembly copy in v1 (noted as a wanted feature).

Import / merge historical parts into a BOM

"Import historical work" / Merge Quote Items as Components pulls a previously-quoted part or sub-assembly into the current BOM as a component, preserving its costing — for shops re-quoting product lines that share sub-assemblies. "Replace reference part with new version" first, so historical quotes aren't altered. SS DemoM (Q&A)

Assembly-router best practice (modeling rule)

An assembly/sub-assembly node's router should hold only assembly steps (Assembly / Assemble-Weld — "putting the children together"), no material or machining/laser ops. Geometric interrogation & automation apply only to manufactured child parts — so cutting/material/run-time costs live there, not on the assembly. The system can't interrogate an assembly node. CHILD BOM shows rollup cost to parent; FLAT BOM shows cost without rollup (and a Flat Qty column).

"Assembly | Parent-Level" process type SS DemoO 03

When a STEP assembly is uploaded, the root node is assigned the built-in process Assembly | Parent-Level. Unlike manufactured child parts, this node:

  • Has no geometric interrogation (the engine does not interrogate the assembly; it interrogates only manufactured children).
  • Gets its own Operations router containing assembly-labour operations: e.g. Assembly | Manufactured, Assembly | Hardware Installation, Sheet Metal | Weld Manual, Generic | Shipping Prep. These are the "putting the children together" operations — see best practice note above.
  • Shows an Operation Summary for those parent-level ops separate from the children's cost.
  • Has a Pricing section showing the full rollup: Material Margin / Inside Margin / Outside Services Margin aggregated across all children, plus Total Price, Unit Price, Total Profit, Total Markup %, Profit Margin %. SS DemoO 11
  • Has an Add-Ons section at the root level: NRE (Non-Recurring Engineering) and FAI (First Article Inspection) appear here, toggled "Not Required" by default. When required, they add to Total Price with Required Add-Ons. SS DemoO 10
Build implication: Pre-seed the operation library with an "Assembly | Parent-Level" process template whose default router contains at minimum: Assembly | Manufactured and Generic | Shipping Prep. These are always-needed on any assembly root. Hardware Installation and Weld ops should be in the library but not auto-added — the estimator adds them as needed. The root pricing rollup is computed server-side by summing all child node costs recursively.

Bulk Update Components modal SS DemoO 06

Select multiple components → mass-assign Process (e.g. Sheet Metal | Laser), Material (e.g. CRS), Finish (optional). Warning: "This action will delete all existing operations." CANCEL / CONFIRM. On confirm, the system regenerates the router from the new process for each selected component and reprices. SS 13 / DemoM 15

The shorter warning ("This action will delete all existing operations.") is shown in the bulk modal. The single-part "Update Process and Material" modal (⋮ menu → Update process/material/finish) offers two commits: UPDATE (regenerate router from new process — longer override-warning text applies) vs UPDATE AND KEEP EXISTING OPS (change process/material, preserve current operations). Material is a type-ahead over hierarchical paths (Metal / Aluminum / …).

Sheet-Metal Engine (Materials & Operations)

SS 08(interrogation) / 5c9d7779 / 5323a50a / a67accc4 KB: sheet-metal-interrogation KB: sheet-metal-process

Sheet Metal Interrogation Results

When a sheet-metal process is set, an interrogation block shows the auto-detected flat pattern + attributes: Sheet Thickness, Flat Surface Area, Bounding Dimensions, Number of Bends, with a flat-pattern thumbnail. If eligible, a banner appears: "Eligible for multi-component sheet metal nesting — Optimize this component's material and labor pricing." → OPEN NESTING MODULE.

Materials section

Lists the stock material line, e.g. Aluminum 5052-H32 Sheet | 0.063" x 48.0" x 144.0" with a NESTABLE chip. Buttons: ADD MATERIAL OPERATION, MATERIAL CALCULATOR. Material Summary totals per quantity. Opening a material line reveals the live material drawer (below).

Operations (router)

An ordered list of operations; each row: drag handle, name, optional NESTABLE chip, Setup Time, Run Time, expand, delete — and per-quantity price columns (total + per-unit beneath). ADD OPERATION. Operation Summary, Yield (%), Make Quantity per qty. Rows can show a note icon (hover to read the op's notes inline without opening it) and per-variable info-tooltips (ⓘ). SS DemoH 04

Operation drawer (per-op detail)

Opening an operation shows a high-density drawer with collapsible accordions whose expanded state is remembered: SETTINGS (Setup Time Display + Runtime Display units + Outside-Service toggle), PRIMARY (Setup Time, Runtime — each Calculated vs editable Override), PRIMARY RUNTIME VARIABLES (Volume Removed, Material Removal Rate in³/min, Hog Out Runtime, Additional Runtime — each Calculated/Override + ⓘ), SETUP VARIABLES, GEOMETRIC VALUES, REMOVAL RATE FACTORS, ADDITIONAL RUNTIME VALUES, COSTING PREVIEW, and NOTES. "Edit operation template" link; CANCEL / SAVE CHANGES. SS DemoH 05 / PL#37

  • Parts-per-hour / per-minute runtime unit: runtime can be expressed as parts/hour or parts/minute (set on the op definition, overridable per quote); recosts on change.
  • Programmatic operation notes: the NOTES field can be auto-filled by the operation formula (set_notes / set_notes_from_list) — e.g. "Setup count: 6.0 / Volume removed: 36.885" ("From template") — to flow values down to the ERP.
  • Variable info-tooltips (ⓘ): per-variable descriptions (e.g. "Additional Runtime — runtime based on surfacing, threading, tool change, spin-up…") shown in the list + drawer; configured per operation; aid estimator training.
  • Inline notes preview: a note icon on the router row reveals the op's notes without opening it.

Operations table — Display Options

The Operations-table gear opens Display Options: Show unit values (per-unit beneath totals), Truncate values, and a Cell Type toggle — Money vs Lead Time (Business Days) — so the per-qty columns can show cost or lead-time. SS PL#37 display-options

Each operation computes cost = f(setup_time, run_time × qty, machine_rate, complexity). Operation Summary example: $5,470.65 at qty 500.

Process taxonomy observed: processes are multi-level paths — Fab | Sheet Metal | Laser, CNC | Milling, Machining → Milling → 5-Axis. Each process owns a default router template + eligible materials + DFM rule set. Build processes/routers as configurable templates (Configure). Setting a process generates its default router; rule "Add operation" resolutions append to it (with duplicate prevention).
