Sheet-Metal Nesting Module

SS 11/12 + f955bdda / 7a80808a KB: multi-component-sheet-metal-nesting KB: multi-component-nesting-module

Optimizes material cost by nesting multiple parts (across different line items) onto shared stock sheets.

Nesting Overview

Reached from Quotes nav → Nesting. Left: Sheet Metal (n) Linear Metal (n) + "Your Nests" (empty state "No nesting for this quote"). A toggle "Allow selecting components with non-identical thickness". Main table groups all sheet-metal components in the quote:

ComponentNested?MaterialFlat Dims (in)Flat Area (sq in)Contour Length (in)Thickness (in)
PP-617-02109--Aluminum 5052-H3220.571 × 21.004426.604144.7880.0440
33000--Aluminum 5052-H323.000 × 10.39129.56337.0510.0290
PP-08470001 ✓--Aluminum 5052-H322.010 × 2.9452.18914.1940.0625

Select components (checkbox) → footer "Selected 1× 0.063in thick Aluminum 5052-H32 components from 1 quote item" → create a nest.

Nesting configuration dialog

"Prepare Multiple Sheet Nests From Same Quote Item". Inputs:

GroupFields
StockMaterial, Thickness, Grain Direction (Lengthwise…), Cost Method (Per Sheet)
Quantity-break-specific stockper break (100/500/1000): Length × Width (96 × 48 in), ERP code, Cost per sheet ($250.00)
Nest SettingsCommon line (✓), Part-in-part (✓), Nesting Direction (Maximize Utilization), Edge Buffer (0.125), Part-to-Part Clearance (0.125), Kerf Width (0.01)
Pricing SettingsPrice Distribution Method (Area of parts), Drop Threshold (25)
Component Settingsper component: Bounding Dims, Surface Area, Contour Length, Make Quantity, Cost Distribution (%), Grain Direction (N/A / ↔ / ↕)

Warning: "Operations and quantities will be locked… to protect the integrity of the shared costing." CANCEL / GENERATE NESTS.

Nesting algorithm (estimation-grade)

parts_area      = Σ(flat_area × make_qty)         # per quantity break
usable_area     = (sheet_L × sheet_W) × utilization_factor   # from buffers/kerf
sheets_needed   = parts_area / usable_area          # fractional (e.g. 0.0966)
material_cost   = ceil_or_frac(sheets_needed) × sheet_cost
# distribute material_cost back to parts by chosen method (Area of parts)
# Drop Threshold = % below which a partial sheet is "dropped"/not charged
Nesting is estimation-grade (area packing with buffer/kerf factors), not a true 2D bin-packing layout. Output: # sheets, material cost per qty, per-part allocated cost. Linear-metal nesting is the 1D analog (stock bars) — stub if time-constrained.

Single-component Nesting Module (per part)

Reached from a part's material drawer ("OPEN NESTING MODULE"). Auto-populates from interrogation + shop defaults: Sheet Size, Drop Threshold, Sheet Thickness, Grain Direction (N/A → e.g. Horizontal), Part Buffer, Edge Buffer; Common Line Cutting checkbox; Outside Service toggle; per quantity-break tabs (25/50/100). Generating a nest yields metrics: Parts/Sheet, Yield %, Drop (area + %), Scrap (area + %), and a rendered sheet layout. The auto-optimization line shows as "Sheet Optimization Choice — 120 × 48 — 88.83%"; once nested, material rows gain an orange HAS NEST badge beside NESTABLE. SS DemoJ 04/05/06

Nest result object (multi-component "Nest #N")

Generating multi-component nests produces Nest objects. Header: "Nest #1", stock "96.000 × 48.000 × 0.080 in. Aluminum 5050-H32", "Associated with N other nests", ACTIONS. Aggregate metrics: Total Sheets (15), Total Cost ($3,750.00), Total Used Area (+%), Total Scrap Area (+%), Total Drop Area (+%), Total Contour Length. Then per sheet type: Quantity of this sheet, used/scrap/drop areas, a rendered layout, and a "Components in Nest (N)" table (Component · Parts per sheet · Area per sheet). SS DemoJ 10

Overview grid columns (full): Component · Nested? · Material · Bounding Dimensions · Profile Surface Area · Contour Length · Thickness · Quote Item · Make Quantities · Nest-Co Material. Toolbar: "Update nesting defaults ↗", "Select all compatible", "Deselect all", PREPARE SHEET NESTS. Prepare dialog adds orientation (Lengthwise) + Cost Method (Per Sheet) atop the fields already documented above.

Costing & Pricing Engine

SS 14/16 + d14360b6 / dbcec2c9

The financial core. Everything computes across the line item's quantity breaks simultaneously; each cell shows total with per-unit beneath. KB: costing-a-part KB: costing-table

Costing & pricing are computed PER LINE ITEM (per part), never at the project/quote level. Every part carries its own Costing + Pricing + Discounts stack and its own Total/Unit Price. The quote total is simply the sum of the line items (each part total × its ordered quantity). There is no quote-wide markup pass. SS Demo E (per-part), client confirmation

Cost categories (color-coded)

RowTagSource
Total Raw MaterialMaterialmaterial drawer (sheet/stock × usage)
Total Inside ProcessingInsidein-house operations (laser, brake, mill…)
Total Outside ProcessingOutsideoutside services (anodize, plating…)
Total Purchased ComponentsPurchased Componentbought parts / hardware
Total Component Overrides—manual cost overrides on components
Total Estimated Cost—sum of ONLY the 5 standard categories above
Total Estimated Cost = Material + Inside + Outside + Purchased + Component Overrides. These five are mutually exclusive and exhaustive — every dollar of cost lands in exactly one. Custom cost categories are NOT added to Total Estimated Cost — they are informational re-slices of cost already counted in the five standard categories, displayed as extra rows below Total Estimated Cost solely as markup targets. Cost is therefore never double-counted. (Verified: Difficult Material $539.36 is the titanium portion of the $674.20 Total Raw Material; Total Estimated Cost stays $1,702.37 either way.)

Custom cost categories & custom markups

The five standard categories are not the limit. An org can define any custom aggregation of cost — a subset of materials, a single work center, labor-vs-overhead, piece-price-vs-tooling, a finish type, a complexity band — give it a name + color, and mark it up / margin it independently. The custom category shows as a colored row in Costing, and a matching row in Pricing. This is the headline of Demo E. SS Demo E 04,07–15 KB: pricing-items-p3l-cheat-sheet KB: custom-tables

Custom category (example)What it aggregatesCosting row → Pricing markup
Difficult Materialonly material cost of "difficult" materials (e.g. Titanium), not all material$539.36 of the $674.20 raw material → 10% markup
Laser Workcentercost of one work center within Inside Processing$178.16 of the $1,019.80 inside → 10%
Inside Labor / Inside Overheadsplits inside processing into operating vs setup/upkeep$632.67 labor (10%) + $387.12 overhead (10%)
Piece Price / Toolingseparates tooling cost from the piece price$1,612.37 piece (10%) + $90.00 tooling (10%)
Outside Finishesonly outside-service finish ops (e.g. powder coat), not all outside$300.00 → 10%
Part Level 3complexity band read from a "PartLevel" operation30% markup (Level 2 → 20% when overridden)
Build implication: model a CostCategory (the 5 standard + N custom, each with a color) and a PricingItem that targets a category. A custom category's cost is computed by a Kalk formula (sum over the BOM of matching cost), exposed via set_custom_cost(); its markup/margin is a second formula. The category cost is informational (a re-slice, not added to Total Estimated Cost); the markup it drives is added to price.
Overlap is intentional and additive — not exclusive, not double-counted cost. A custom category usually overlaps a standard one (a subset, a partition, or the whole). When both a standard-category markup and an overlapping custom markup exist, both apply and their $ amounts sum — the overlapping slice effectively gets the combined %. Verified: Inside Operations 22% on all Inside ($1,019.80 → $224.35) plus Laser Workcenter 10% on the laser subset ($178.16 → $17.81) → laser portion carries 32%, the rest 22%; cost is counted once. This stacking is the shop's pricing strategy (extra margin on difficult material / a hot work center / tooling, etc.).
Custom categories are shop-private pricing levers. Each shop defines its own (names, colors, formulas) to match its strategy; they are internal by default. Whether any of it is disclosed to the customer is the shop's choice and is governed by Quote Display Settings — the buyer-facing quote need not show custom categories at all. Custom cost + markup columns flow to Excel/CSV export, analytics, and the API for internal reporting. (Edge case from Q&A: export-controlled/ITAR items currently cannot be marked up — treat as a known exception.)

Pricing section — independent, additive pricing items

An ordered stack of pricing items (drag handle, expand, ⋮). Each item targets a cost category (standard or custom) and carries a Calculation Type (Markup or Margin) and a % — fixed or Kalk-computed. ADD PRICING ITEM, ACTIONS ▾. Output rows: Total (excl. Discounts), Unit Price (excl. Discounts), Total Profit, Total Markup (%), Profit Margin (%). KB: pricing-a-part KB: pricing-table

Calculation model (verified against demo numbers)

The key correction: pricing items are computed independently off cost and summed — they do not compound on a running price, and they are not mutually exclusive.

1. Per operation:  op_cost = setup_cost + (run_time × qty × machine_rate) …
2. Roll operations → Inside / Outside processing totals
3. Material drawer → Raw Material total
4. Components (BOM rollup) → Purchased components; component overrides
5. Total Estimated Cost = Material + Inside + Outside + Purchased + Overrides
6. Custom category costs = Kalk re-slices of (1–5); informational, NOT added to cost
7. For EACH pricing item i, independently off its target category cost C_i:
      Markup:  amount_i = C_i × pct_i            (price contribution)
      Margin:  amount_i = C_i × pct_i / (1 − pct_i)   (to net pct_i margin on that slice)
   (every amount is computed from COST, not from a running subtotal)
8. Total markup $  = Σ amount_i
   Total (excl. Discounts) = Total Estimated Cost + Σ amount_i
9. Apply Discounts (see below) → Total (incl. Discounts)
10. Unit Price = Total / qty (per quantity break)
11. Total Profit = Total − Total Estimated Cost
    Total Markup % = Total Profit / Total Estimated Cost
    Profit Margin % = Total Profit / Total
Worked example (Difficult Material, qty 1): Total Estimated Cost = $1,702.37 (Material 674.20 + Inside 1,019.80 + Purchased 8.37). Pricing: Raw Material Markup 60% × 674.20 = $404.52; Difficult Material Markup 10% × 539.36 (titanium subset) = $53.93. Σ markup = $458.45 → Total = $2,160.84; Total Markup % = 458.46 / 1,702.37 = 26.93%. The titanium material thus carries 60%+10% = 70%; the rest carries 60%. The same additive, off-cost pattern reproduces the Laser-Workcenter, Labor/Overhead, Piece-Price/Tooling, Outside-Finishes, and Complexity examples exactly.
Markup vs Margin is a per-item toggle and different math — markup adds cost × pct; margin adds cost × pct/(1−pct) so the slice nets pct margin. All standard-category names in the demos vary ("Raw Material Markup", "Material | Margin", "Customer Specific Markup", "General Markup") — these are just org-named pricing items targeting a category; honor each item's type + target. A "General"/account markup targets the whole Total Estimated Cost.
Quantity breaks are first-class: every cost/price/lead-time/add-on is computed per break, and a pricing item's % can differ per break. Breaks differ per part ([1,5,20] vs [100,500,1000]).

Complexity-driven dynamic markup

Markup % can be computed from router/part data, not just typed. Example: a "PartLevel" complexity operation classifies a CNC part (Level 2 / Level 3); the pricing item reads that level and applies the matching markup automatically (Level 3 = 30%, Level 2 = 20%). Overriding the complexity operation instantly re-prices ("Part Level 3 Markup" becomes "Part Level 2 Markup"). SS Demo E 13–15

Discounts

A Discounts section sits below Pricing; totals are expressed as "Total (excl. Discounts)" / "Unit Price (excl. Discounts)". Discounts are their own pricing-item type (e.g. "Promotional Discount") configurable under Configure → Discounts. SS Demo E 14 KB: discounts KB: discounts-p3l-cheat-sheet

Pricing language (Kalk) — full language reference

folded from KALK-REFERENCE.md + PRICING-ENGINE-SPEC.md This subsection is now the authoritative Kalk reference (those sub-specs are archived). Kalk is the Tolera name for Paperless Parts' P3L: a restricted, AST-sandboxed Python-3 dialect. Function/object names below are the semantics to implement — rename surface strings freely but keep the contracts. Source: KB: what-is-paperless-parts-pricing-language-p3l + the nine P3L cheat sheets (cited per subsection).

Pricing items and custom-category costs are authored in this Python-like language with a CHECK + SAVE editor (versioned). It iterates the assembly BOM and reads part/component/router data — confirmed in Demo E (no longer inferred). The same language drives operation cost/lead-time, operation generation, add-ons, and discounts — five formula contexts (see Context-specific reference). The pricing-item object model:

for component in get_components(order='leaf_to_root'):   # or 'root_to_leaf' (default)
    component.uuid, component.part_uuid
    component.self_cost      # total cost of materials + operations + overrides
    component.lead_time      # process + material + operation lead-time contributions
    process = component.process            # optional
    process.name, process.lead_time
    part = component.part
    part.part_number, part.revision, part.obtain_method, part.is_assembly,
    part.is_root_component, part.innate_quantity, part.qty, part.bom_qty,
    part.quantities, part.make_quantities, part.bom_quantities,
    part.material, part.global_material, part.material_family, part.material_class,
    part.density, part.weight, part.mat_cost_per_volume, part.mat_added_lead_time,
    part.size_x, part.size_y, part.size_z, part.max_dim, part.med_dim, part.min_dim,
    part.area, part.volume,
    part.count_purchased_children, part.count_manufactured_children
    pc = part.purchased_component          # optional
    pc.oem_part_number, pc.piece_price, pc.internal_part_number, pc.description, …

# custom-category cost: sum matching cost across the BOM
total = 0
for component in get_components('order_to_leaf'):
    if "Titanium" in component.part.material:
        for material in get_material_operations(component):
            total += material.cost
set_custom_cost(total)
PERCENTAGE = 10

Helpers: get_components(order), get_material_operations(component), set_custom_cost(total); markups also read custom tables and account/contact data (so customer-specific markups are possible). The same language drives operation/material cost formulas (geometry × machine rates × lookup tables). Editor chrome: "Pricing Formula — <name>", last-saved-by, Versions, CHECK, SAVE CHANGES.

Execution model — five formula contexts

Kalk runs once per (operation × quantity break) and per pricing-context formula; outputs sum up the component → quote-item → quote tree. Five documented formula contexts (the engine groups these into three interpreters sharing one sandboxed core). folded: KALK-REFERENCE.md §1

ContextOutput(s)RunsCheat sheet
Operation costCOST (number) + DAYS (int business days)per operation, per quantityKB: operation-p3l-cheat-sheet
Operation / process generationmutates routing; sets custom attrswhen a Process is applied to a partKB: custom-operation-generation
Pricing itemPERCENTAGE (+ set_custom_cost() for custom)per cost-category, per quantity (root component)KB: pricing-items-p3l-cheat-sheet
Add-onPRICE (number)per add-on, per quantity (root component)KB: add-ons-p3l-cheat-sheet
DiscountPERCENTAGE (positive)per discount, per quantity (root component)KB: discounts-p3l-cheat-sheet
COST vs PRICE — standardise. PP's operation cheat sheet uses COST; some PP examples use PRICE for operations. Tolera decision: operation context outputs COST+DAYS; add-ons output PRICE; pricing/discount output PERCENTAGE (DECISIONS.md).

Pricing-theory baseline. Most operations reduce to COST = setup_time × rate + runtime × make_quantity × rate — setup_time is one-time per op, runtime is per-unit. Internally runtime/setup_time are in hours (UI display unit configurable s/min/hr; overrides entered in display units). Evaluator contract:

Kalk.evaluate(formula, context_type, eval_context, quantity)
  -> { output (COST/DAYS | PRICE | PERCENTAGE),
       declared_variables[], applied_overrides[], notes,
       operation_name, errors[] }
DACH — part data is metric by default (mm, mm², mm³, g, g/cm³); call units_in() as the first statement to switch a formula to imperial (default path stays metric). currency values format as EUR / de-DE (1.234,56 €). Tax (MwSt/USt) is applied at quote/order level — never inside Kalk.

Shared Python subset

folded: KALK-REFERENCE.md §2 KB: operation-p3l-cheat-sheet — Operators: * / + -, augmented += -= *= /=, exponent **, comparisons < <= > >= == !=, boolean and/or, membership in; standard if/elif/else and for. Built-ins (Python 3.6 semantics): min() max() mean() median() (iterable or varargs), round(n[,ndigits]), abs(), sum(iter[,start]), floor() ceil() str(), '{}'.format(...) (dynamic strings for names/lookups), split(sep) (e.g. part.material.split(' ')). Removed / restricted (enforced by the AST sandbox): no imports, no file/network/system access, no arbitrary attribute access.

Variable system

Variables create overridable inputs in the live quoting UI. All declaration functions (var, table_var, table_lookup, variable_group, drop_down_var) must NOT be called inside if/elif/else or loops. folded: KALK-REFERENCE.md §3

var(name, default, description, value_type,
    default_visible=True, frozen=True, quantity_specific=False)
  • value_type ∈ number | currency | string (type-enforced).
  • default computes once at save and cannot depend on part or other variables — unless frozen=False.
  • frozen=False → dynamic variable: initialise to default, .update(expr) (may read part/others) any number of times, then .freeze() before using it for cost. The freeze point is where a UI override applies — load-bearing.
  • quantity_specific=True → value shown/overridable per quantity break; default_visible=False hides it from the Processes page.

Special names runtime and setup_time surface directly on the operations list (not behind the modal); internally in hours. drop_down_var(name, default_value, default_options, description, value_type, …) = discrete-option variable for logic switches / dynamic forms (.clear_options(), .update_options(), .select_option(), .select_default(); search ≤50 — KB: drop-down-variables). variable_group(name, default_collapsed=False) collects vars into ordered collapsible UI sections via .add_by_name(...) (declaration order = UI order); default groups are primary (runtime/setup_time) and declared. Per-qty mechanics: KB: quantity-specific-variables.

Worked example — dynamic runtime from a tapped-hole count, with variable groups 
labor_rate = var('Labor Rate', 50, '$/hr', currency)
machine_rate = var('Machine Rate', 40, '$/hr', currency)
rates = variable_group('Rates', default_collapsed=True)
rates.add_by_name('Labor Rate', 'Machine Rate')

tapped_hole_count = var('Tapped Hole Count', 0, '# tapped holes', number)
runtime_per_hole   = var('Runtime Per Hole', 2, 'min/hole', number)
setup_time_per_hole= var('Setup Time Per Hole', 5, 'min/hole', number)

runtime = var('runtime', 0, 'hours', number, frozen=False)
runtime.update(tapped_hole_count * runtime_per_hole / 60)
runtime.freeze()
setup_time = var('setup_time', 0, 'hours', number, frozen=False)
setup_time.update(setup_time_per_hole * tapped_hole_count / 60)
setup_time.freeze()

COST = runtime * part.qty * machine_rate + setup_time * labor_rate
DAYS = 0
KB: operation-p3l-cheat-sheet — note runtime/setup_time can never be added to a variable group.

Lists — P3LList

create_list(*args) → P3LList. Methods (chainable): append extend reverse copy clear remove pop sort filter map reduce unique join; helpers create_multi_sort(*keys) (prefix - = descending), iterate(list); min/max/mean/median/sum on numeric lists. Used heavily to process the .features of an analyzer. folded: KALK-REFERENCE.md §4 KB: p3l-lists

sm = analyze_sheet_metal()
long_bends = sm.features.filter(
    lambda x: x.name in create_list('bend','curl','open_hem')
              and x.properties.length > 10
).sort(lambda x: create_multi_sort(-x.properties.length, -x.properties.radius))

Custom tables

folded: KALK-REFERENCE.md §5 KB: custom-table-p3l · KB: custom-tables

  • table_var(name, description, table_name, filters, order_by, display_column_name, frozen=True, quantity_specific=False) — look up a custom table, overridable in the live UI. frozen=True → first matching TableRow (or None); frozen=False → a TableVariable. Cap ≤200 rows.
  • table_lookup(table_name, filters, order_by, …) → P3LList of all matching rows (≤10,000); does not create an overridable variable — use for tooling sets / ERP-derived routing.
  • Filtering: create_filter(*[filter|exclude]), filter(col, cond, val) / exclude(col, cond, val), create_order_by(*cols) (prefix - descending), create_range(a,b).
ConditionTypesExample
=bool, numeric, stringfilter('material','=','Aluminum 6061-T6')
> >= < <=numeric, stringexclude('length','>',10)
rangenumericfilter('diameter','range',create_range(5,6))
containsstringfilter('material','contains','Aluminum')

TableRow: dot-access columns + row.row_number; .to_keys_list() / .to_values_list() / .to_list(). TableVariable (from frozen=False): .value (selected row / None), .rows; selection methods freeze on call — select_first/last(), select_by_row(), select_by_row_number(). The selection point is the override point.

Geometry analyzers (the interrogation → pricing contract)

Run interrogation and return an analysis object with computed attributes + a .features P3LList. These returns are simultaneously the output surface of GeometryService and the input surface of Kalk — the single contract between the two engines (full part.* table → Part Estimating View). folded: KALK-REFERENCE.md §6 KB: operation-p3l-cheat-sheet

analyze_mill3() · analyze_lathe() · analyze_sheet_metal() · analyze_tube_laser() · analyze_wire_edm() · analyze_casting() · analyze_additive() · manual_nest(). Each feature has .name + .properties.* (length, radius, volume, area, side_length, side_width…); sheet-metal exposes .thickness, .pierce_count, .total_cut_length; lathe exposes .stock_radius, .stock_length.

v1 OCCT ceiling. v1 GeometryService runs on OCCT and populates a reduced feature set per family; Kalk authors must know which .properties.* are guaranteed in v1 vs Spatial-only. See § Geometry & DFM (DECISIONS.md: OCCT-first, Spatial swap-in later).

Workpiece & cost / price dictionaries

Workpiece — a dict that flows operation→operation along each quantity line: set_workpiece_value(key, value) / get_workpiece_value(key, default) (e.g. pass a tapped-hole count from a Tapping op to a downstream Anodize op that must mask them). Cost dict — get_cost_value(key) sums cells whose operation/op-def name matches key for the current quantity; special keys --material--, --outside--, --inside--, --total--. Price dict (add-ons) — get_price_value(key); keys --required_add_on--, --non_required_add_on--. folded: KALK-REFERENCE.md §7 KB: operation-p3l-cheat-sheet

# a material-markup operation reading the accumulated material cost
markup = var('markup', 0.3, '', number)
set_operation_name('{} markup'.format(part.material))
COST = get_cost_value('--material--') * markup
DAYS = 0

Custom part attributes

Top-level part properties usable in Kalk, set manually at quote time or from code — they bridge geometric (CAD) and non-geometric (PDF) files so one formula prices both (e.g. set outer_diameter from analyze_lathe() when available, else read the manual value). set_custom_attribute(key, value) (number/bool/string; type-stable) · get_custom_attribute(key, default). Defaults configurable in Settings and copied to every new part. folded: KALK-REFERENCE.md §8 KB: operation-p3l-cheat-sheet

BOM / assembly iteration

get_quantities() / get_bom_quantities() / get_make_quantities() → index-aligned P3LList iterators. get_children(obtain_method=None, is_assembly=None, recursive=False) → P3LList of child objects (.obtain_method PURCHASED|MANUFACTURED, .is_assembly, .part_number, .revision, .count, .purchased_component); recursive=True = flat BOM (descendant counts to base of tree), False = direct children. Pricing-item context only: get_components(order='leaf_to_root'|'root_to_leaf'), get_children(component), get_operations(component), get_material_operations(component). folded: KALK-REFERENCE.md §9 KB: operation-p3l-cheat-sheet

Worked example — assembly insertion-time costing (sum purchased-child insertion times) 
runtime = var('runtime', 0, '', number, frozen=False)
rts = 0
for child in get_children(obtain_method=PURCHASED, recursive=True):
    if child.purchased_component.insertion_time:
        rts += child.purchased_component.insertion_time * child.count
runtime.update(rts / 3600)
runtime.freeze()
rate = var('Rate', 60, '$/hr', currency)
COST = runtime * rate * part.qty
KB: operation-p3l-cheat-sheet — switch to recursive=False and apply per subassembly to break costs down by level.

Cost roll-up — formal model

folded from PRICING-ENGINE-SPEC.md §3 The load-bearing math — verified against the Demo E numbers above and bound to the Fechner golden fixtures.
  1. Make quantity per component = BOM-tree position × ordered Q (a child appearing 6× at Q=1 has make_qty 6). Operation formulas multiply runtime by part.qty (= make qty for that component/break). Deliver qty ≠ make qty (scrap).
  2. Operation cost (per component, per qty) = Kalk COST; lead-time contribution = DAYS. Tagged material / inside / outside / purchased-component.
  3. Component cost = Σ its operations' COST, accumulating MATERIAL_COST, INSIDE_COST, OUTSIDE_COST, PURCHASED_COMPONENT_COST, TOTAL_COST.
  4. Roll-up: child-component costs roll up the tree to the root component (= quote item) per quantity. Pricing items, add-ons, and expedites apply only at the root component.
  5. Price (root, per break): pricing items apply markup Sell = Cost × (1 + pct) or margin Sell = Cost / (1 − pct) to a cost category → rounded unit price. Discounts apply after: discounted_unit = rounded_unit × (1 − Σ discount%/100). Add-ons apply after discounts (one-time line fees, required or optional). Expedite: each option = relative days-faster + % price markup.
  6. Invariants: unit_price × quantity = total_price; totals rounded to 2 dp; both pre- and post-discount unit prices shown to the buyer. Quote total = Σ selected quote items at chosen qty/lead-time.

Persist per-break results as ComponentQuantity + per-quantity cells (operation / pricing-item / discount / add-on), with calculated vs manual(override) values separated (Data Model; resolve with COALESCE(manual_*, calc_*)).

Config-change semantics (FREEZE)

Config edits (operations, Kalk, materials, pricing items) do not re-price existing drafts/revisions — only new quotes. Opt-in re-run actions: Regenerate Operations (items without overrides — delete + repopulate from the latest Process), Refresh Pricing / Bulk Refresh Pricing (re-run while preserving overrides, then manual cleanup of stale/added ops). The engine supports both a re-evaluate-with-preserved-overrides mode and a clean regenerate mode. folded: PRICING-ENGINE-SPEC.md §5 KB: working-with-existing-quotes-after-an-update-to-pricing-configuration

Determinism, security, performance

  • Deterministic (same inputs → same outputs) — prerequisite for golden tests.
  • Sandbox: no imports / IO / system; AST allowlist of nodes + builtins (min/max/mean/median/round/abs/sum/floor/ceil/str/format/split + operators); resource / time / recursion caps; safe inline error surfacing via the editor's CHECK.
  • Performance: cache analyze_*() results; target >100-component BOMs (NFR); interrogations are async jobs the engine awaits.

Acceptance / golden tests

  • Golden costing: the verified Demo-E numbers + Fechner fixtures → assert exact COST/DAYS/PRICE/PERCENTAGE and final unit/total prices for known inputs.
  • Margin / markup: unit tests for Sell=Cost/(1−pct) and Sell=Cost×(1+pct); mixed pricing items by category; additive off-cost summation (not compounding).
  • Roll-up: assembly with a repeated child → make-qty + child→root roll-up; discounts after markup; add-ons after discount; unit×qty=total.
  • Per-context: each context evaluates with correct globals/outputs; declaration-in-loop rejected; freeze-point override applied.
  • DACH: currency formats EUR / de-DE; tax (MwSt/USt) applied at quote/order level (not in Kalk); metric default.

Add-Ons, Lead Times & Expedite

SS 17/18 + 295be166

Add-Ons

Per-line extra charges, each per-qty, with a Required toggle (Required/Not Required) and flat or computed price. Example: First Article Inspection (FAI) $100 flat. Rows roll into "Total Required Add-Ons" and "Total Price with Required Add-Ons". ADD ADD-ON. KB: add-ons-p3l-cheat-sheet KB: final-touches-add-ons-discounts-and-dynamic-lead-times

Add-On types (dropdown)

  • Manual Add-On
  • Certificate of Conformance
  • Non-Recurring Engineering (NRE)
  • Tooling Charge
  • Special Packaging (Extra Padding / Fragile Labeling)
  • First Article Inspection (FAI)

(Searchable; admin-configurable list of AddOnType.)

Lead Times

Per quantity break (e.g. "6 Days" across 1/5/20), plus an Expedite Option row that applies the quote's expedite tiers (days faster + markup). Build-order ship dates derive from lead time + expedite. SS 17 KB: dynamic-lead-times-guide

Dynamic lead times (E4-c). Beyond a flat per-break value, lead time can be rules-driven — auto-adjusted by quantity, process, or shop load. Model lead-time rules alongside the Rules engine; the DAYS output of each operation and the expedite tiers feed the ship-date calculation. KB: dynamic-lead-times-guide

Expedite

Configured at quote level (see Quote Detail); "APPLY TO ALL" pushes the three tiers to every line item's Lead Times.

Digital Quote — Buyer Portal

Route: /q/:token (public, no login) · Token = signed, scoped, per-recipient JWT · SS DemoK digital-quote / DemoI checkout

The customer-facing quote. Sent via email as a link; the buyer never logs in — the token scopes them to exactly this quote. Bid Factory's shop branding (logo, facility name, accent color) is applied to the header. KB: the-digital-quote KB: digital-quote-settings KB: Configuring-finalized-quotes KB: online-checkout-in-paperless-parts

Page layout (top → bottom)

  1. Shop header: logo, facility name, contact info (phone/website per Quote Display Settings toggles).
  2. Quote header strip: Quote #, optional RFQ #, Expiration date, "Prepared by" (salesperson/estimator per Display Settings), customer notes + T&Cs.
  3. Pricing range chip: AVAILABLE FROM $X–$Y /ea computed from the lowest and highest unit prices across all line items and quantity breaks. SS DemoK digital quote range chip
  4. Line item cards (one per part — see below).
  5. Order summary + checkout button (see below).

ElementContent / rules

Checkout flow (PO only in v1)

Buyer selects a qty break per line item (at least one must be selected to proceed). "Order Summary" panel shows selected lines, subtotal (net), VAT line, total (gross). PROCEED TO CHECKOUT.
Company & PO form: Company Name (pre-filled from Account if known), PO Number*, Billing Address, optional notes. VAT ID field (for EU B2B — if provided and VIES-valid, quote shows reverse-charge net).
Shipping: buyer selects one of the four shipping options configured by the shop (Bill Me at Time of Shipment / Use My Shipping Account / No Shipping Fees). Charge Me for Shipping (CC) is hidden in v1 (cards deferred).
Review & Submit: summary of selected parts, quantities, prices, VAT, shipping method, PO#. PLACE ORDER → creates the Order record in Bid Factory + notifies the shop (dashboard notification + email).
Confirmation page: order # confirmation, "Your order has been received — the team at <facility> will be in touch." Shop facility email + phone shown.

Expired quote state

When the quote expiration date has passed, the portal remains reachable but checkout is blocked. The header shows an EXPIRED badge and the expiry date. If the org has Requotes enabled (Settings → Requotes), a "Request Updated Quote" button appears — clicking it sends a notification to the shop and shows "Your request has been received." The shop can then create a new revision or reopen the quote.

Token security

  • Token is per-quote, per-recipient. Revocable (Settings → revoke a specific recipient's access).
  • Access is logged (feeds the CUI audit log).
  • Vendor share links use the same infrastructure but with file-scope permissions (see Collaboration & Sourcing).
  • "Require 2FA for external share" setting enforces an OTP before the portal loads.
Build implications: QuoteToken entity (unified — covers all external access): token (signed JWT — omit the JWT exp claim; expiry enforced at the application layer — required for soft-expiry behaviour), scope ENUM(buyer_portal | vendor_share | vendor_rfq), quote_id (nullable), external_share_id (nullable), vendor_rfq_recipient_id (nullable), recipient_email, revoked_at, file_permissions JSONB (for vendor shares and vendor RFQ). The buyer portal is a separate React entrypoint (unauthenticated, no sidebar) at /q/:token — it fetches quote data via a public API endpoint that validates the token and returns only buyer-visible fields. VAT display: if the buyer presents a VIES-valid VAT ID, re-render line totals as net + "Reverse charge (§13b UStG)" note; otherwise show net + VAT% line + gross. PO submission creates an Order with source=buyer_portal and fires the shop notification.

Submitting it auto-creates a draft Quote in Bid Factory and fires the same dashboard notification as an email-forwarded RFQ. Behavior on submit

Settings page shows: the live Form URL (https://<domain>/rfq/<org-slug>) for direct sharing + a copyable embed code (<iframe src="…" width="100%" height="700" frameborder="0"></iframe>). The page also shows toggles for which process types are available and which fields are required.

Build implications: The Smart RFQ form is a second unauthenticated React entrypoint (/rfq/:slug, iframe-safe). The form POST hits a public endpoint that resolves org_slug → Org, validates input, creates the Quote, and returns a 200. No org_id in the URL query string (use slug only). File upload uses the same S3 pipeline as internal uploads; apply the same malware/ZIP scan. The form respects the org's locale (if de, labels are German). Disable CSRF-via-referer since it's intentionally cross-origin iframed.

Order Build / Checkout

SS 4cd65e7a (Build Order) / 5f3e9a2e (Shipping & Payment)

Converting an accepted quote into an order. Two-step flow. KB: order-facilitation KB: post-order-changes-and-limitations

Step 1 — Build Order (Facilitate Order drawer)

Header "N/N selected" (select-all + edit) · EDIT SHIPS ON (bulk date) · search by part #/filename. For each line item, choose one quantity break to order (radio), showing Qty, Lead Time, Ships On (date picker), Unit Price, Total Price. Per-line sub-sections: Discounts (e.g. "Customer loyalty program" — Percent 5.00%) and Additional Charges (e.g. "Tooling (Required)" — Price $500.00); links "Add Adjustment", "Add Additional Charge". Footer: "N PARTS IN ORDER · $<subtotal> · CONTINUE TO SHIPPING & PAYMENT". SS DemoH 12

Order editing (2025 opt-in): orders can be edited before shipment — change PO number, shipping address, add/remove items — with an order history trail and optional buyer notification. Limited to non-credit-card orders without shipments. ERP integrations generally receive data at order creation; editing keeps systems in sync rather than re-triggering. Enabled via Settings → Facilitate Order Updates.

Step 2 — Shipping & Payment (Optional)

Payment Method: Not Provided / Purchase Order / Credit Card. Collapsible: Ship To Facility, Bill To, Shipping Options (each default "None"). GO BACK / REVIEW ORDER → creates the Order + OrderLines.

Orders live under the Orders nav tab. An OrderLine references a LineItem + the chosen QuantityBreak + ship date + adjustments. This is where ERP hand-off happens (§ Integrations).

Shipping options

Two contexts: digital quote checkout (customer self-serves) and facilitated order (team facilitates manually).

Ship by date: determined by the quote item's lead time + the time at which the order is placed. The Ships On field in the Build Order drawer reflects this calculation and can be manually adjusted per line item.

Collaboration & Sourcing

SS DemoF ep2_04/05/06/07 + CAD/PDF viewers

The viewer (CAD + PDF) hosts internal/external collaboration and in-context supplier sourcing — a major theme of Demo F. Goal: ask the right people the right questions fast, and source components without leaving the quote.

TEAM vs EXTERNAL channels

  • TEAM — internal messaging. Post a message bound to a selected feature/face/annotation (@mention a teammate, e.g. "@engineer any suggestions on how we might achieve this?"); the recipient gets an email/notification whose link opens the viewer zoomed to that exact feature. Reply / Edit / Delete; Tag Teammate; Assign Task (surfaces on the Dashboard).
  • EXTERNAL — per-vendor/customer channels (e.g. "Plating RFQ", "Custom Tool"). Vendors log in free; messages, annotations, and uploaded quotes are captured in one place.

Secure external sharing (RFQ to vendors)

Start a new EXTERNAL channel → an email-style composer ("widget"): pick recipients from the vendor/customer database (BCC several for blind multi-quoting), edit subject + body ("please quote qty 1, 10, 20"), and set per-recipient permissions and an access window:

ControlOptions
File accesschoose which files the vendor sees (e.g. STEP + the redacted print only)
PermissionsView · Download · Upload supporting file (so the vendor drops their quote back in, not via email)
Access durationexpiry window (e.g. 1 week) after which access is revoked

Recipients can't see each other or each other's uploads. All correspondence is recorded against the part/quote.

PDF redaction

In the PDF Edit mode, the redaction tool blacks out regions (e.g. title-block customer info, anodize callout owner) and saves an exact redacted copy (e.g. AR15Bolt_RevA-redacted.PDF) stored alongside the original; one file is flagged PRIMARY. Used to sanitize prints before sharing with outside processors/competitors. Protects the shop, its customers, and vendors from leaking sensitive data. SS DemoF ep2_07

Annotation suite (2D + 3D)

Annotations can be attached to messages so a vendor opens straight to the point: highlight a drawing note ("see spec here"), or click faces/holes on the 3D model ("please mask these holes"). Clicking a message's annotation toggles the relevant document/feature and highlights it. (Toolbar detail in § PDF Viewer and § CAD Viewer.)

Supplier sourcing integrations

IntegrationWhat it does
Tolera Advisor (MSC)Free in-context tooling chat with a named MSC specialist. Share the part file + annotations; time-boxed access; SLA "respond in under an hour" (Mon–Fri 8–5). 3-step flow: describe problem → Add Annotation (faces/edges/vertices/measure/PDF highlight) → upload supporting docs (tolerances, material, machine).
Tolera Source (PEM)BOM-driven fastener availability + pricing. Supplier selector (e.g. DB Roberts); Make Quantities breaks recompute Per Unit / Per BOM / Total; availability dots (🟢 Available / 🔴 Insufficient); "Select Fastener (N matches)" to resolve unknowns; Add/Remove fastener; SEND RFQ.
Online Metalsraw-material sourcing link-out.

RFQ-to-distributors modal

"Request for Quote (Entire BOM)": a distributor list (DB Roberts, Aerospace Fasteners, Bisco, Bossard, Genfasco, HTF, Mouser, Nappco, S.W. Anderson, Tower…) each with an availability dot + checkbox (select-all header), an optional MESSAGE, and CANCEL / SEND RFQ. Sending populates an email body to the selected distributors. SS DemoF ep2_06 (RFQ modal)

Real-time purchased-component pricing & availability (in the BOM)

Once components are tagged purchased, each row in the BOM shows the preferred distributor logo (e.g. DB Roberts) + brand (PEM) and live pricing + inventory per quantity-break column, with a "Pricing updated <date/time>" timestamp. SS DemoL 04/05

Inventory indicatorMeaning
🟩 Greendistributor holds ≥200% of the required quantity for that make-quantity break ("Stock is available")
🟡 Amber / ⚠️"Stock is at risk" — insufficient inventory for that break
❓availability uncertain / loading

Dynamic make-quantity refresh: changing a make quantity (or adding a higher break, e.g. 1500/5000) instantly re-queries the distributor and refreshes price + availability across all quantity columns — so the estimator sees where high-volume stock becomes a risk.

Real-time Pricing Settings panel BETA

Per purchased-component flyout. SS DemoL 07 Contents: part thumbnail + brand; a distributor selector (change preferred supplier per component); tabs Pricing Breaks | Inventory Locations. Pricing Breaks lists Dynamic (live per-piece) plus stepped tiers (1–99, 100–499, 500–999, 1000–4999, 5000–9999) with a Piece Price each; the estimator picks per-piece dynamic vs a fixed bulk tier (buy-in-bulk-and-hold behavior). Timestamped; CANCEL / SAVE persists the chosen mode for that component.

Build implications: model ExternalShare (recipients[], per-recipient permissions, file scope, expiry, channel) and a SupplierIntegration abstraction with three capabilities — availability/pricing lookup (Tolera Source-style, BOM-driven, qty-break aware, with per-break inventory status + a per-component pricing-mode of dynamic|tier and a preferred-distributor selection), OEM smart-match (geometric/part-number matching for Convert to Purchased Component + persistent geometry memory), and advisory chat (Tolera Advisor-style). Cache distributor responses with a timestamp; refresh on make-quantity change. The ≥200%-green / at-risk-amber rule is a display threshold over (distributor on-hand ÷ required qty). Treat each named partner (PEM, DB Roberts/distributors, MSC, Online Metals) as an adapter behind the abstraction.

Vendor RFQ Portal (outside-process sourcing)

New feature — no Paperless Parts equivalent. Full spec from product design session 2026-06-13.

Replaces the shop's current workflow of emailing vendor quotes manually and copying numbers into the costing screen. Vendors respond via a structured portal form or by email reply; either way the response lands directly against the originating line item's outside-service cost field — no copy-paste.

Supplier Directory (nav: Suppliers)

Top-level sidebar item Suppliers, parallel to Quotes / Orders / Contacts. Vendors are separate entities from Accounts (customers) — distinct data model, separate auth scope.

Vendor List

ColumnNotes
Vendor nameLinks to detail view
ProcessesTag chips (anodize, heat treat, plating, laser OSV, paint, …)
MaterialsTag chips (aluminium, titanium, steel, …)
Active RFQsCount of open vendor RFQs awaiting response

Toolbar: search by name; filter by process/material; ADD VENDOR button. Acceptance rate, last used, and status (Active/Inactive) live in the detail view, not the list.

Vendor Detail — Tabs

TabContent
OverviewCompany name, address, quoting contact(s) (primary + CC list), phone, ERP sync status, Active/Inactive toggle, acceptance rate, last used date
RFQ HistoryAll RFQs sent to this vendor — date, parts, quoted prices per qty break, accepted/rejected, applied to which quote
CapabilitiesProcesses + materials they handle (editable; also populated from ERP sync). Used for AI suggestions and list filtering.
NotesInternal notes — never visible to the vendor

Vendor data sources

Vendors may be populated from the connected ERP (one-way sync ERP → BF; ERP is source of truth for company identity and contact data) or entered manually. BF-only data (capabilities, portal credentials, response history, AI signal) never writes back to the ERP. Manual entry is always available regardless of ERP connection.

Outbound RFQ Flow

Trigger points

Two entry points, same underlying modal:

  • Part Estimating View → Outside Services section: a "Get Vendor Quote" button opens the batch send modal with the current line item pre-selected.
  • Quote Detail → line item multi-select → toolbar: estimator checks one or more line items and hits "Send Vendor RFQ". Batch-friendly — all selected items pre-populate the modal.

In both cases the modal allows adding/removing line items before sending.

Operation-level vs part-level distinction

TypeWhat is outsourcedVendor receivesResponse maps to
Operation-levelOne step in the router (e.g. anodizing on an otherwise in-house CNC part)Full part package — drawing, material spec, geometric requirements, qty breaks, estimator notes (vendor may need full context)Outside Services operation cost for that line item
Part-level (Buy)Entire part outsourced — make vs buy decisionFull part package (same as above)Line item flipped to Buy mode: internal costing (machine time, materials) hidden/disabled; vendor price becomes the total cost. Pricing layer (markup, margin) remains active.

Batch send modal

Fields:

  • Line items — checklist of selected parts (part number, thumbnail, process, qty breaks). Add/remove.
  • Vendors — multi-select with AI-suggested vendors pre-checked (ranked by: last accepted → historical acceptance rate → process match → material match). New vendors show a New label. Each vendor's quoting contact shown; estimator can swap or add recipients inline.
  • Need-by date — deadline for vendor response (soft cutoff — see portal behaviour below).
  • Message — optional free-text appended to the outbound email and visible on the portal.
  • Files — pre-filled with relevant drawings/CAD; estimator can toggle which files each vendor receives (supports redacted versions).

On send: one RFQ email per vendor (BCC-isolated — vendors cannot see each other). Each email contains the RFQ summary + a signed portal link scoped to that vendor + that RFQ batch.

Outbound RFQ email

System-generated. Contains: shop name + logo, RFQ number + reference, need-by date, parts table (one row per line item: part number, rev, description, thumbnail, process requested, qty breaks, estimator notes), file download links, portal link button ("Submit Your Quote"), estimator contact details. Vendor can reply directly to the email instead of using the portal — both channels are valid.

Vendor Portal (unauthenticated)

Route: /vendor-rfq/:token. Access via signed JWT token embedded in the outbound email link — no vendor account or password required. Token scoped to one RFQ batch + one vendor.

Portal page layout

  • Header: shop name + logo (tenant branding), RFQ number, need-by date.
  • Parts table: one row per line item — part number, rev, description, process requested, qty breaks, estimator notes, thumbnail, file download link(s).
  • Download RFQ PDF button — generates a PDF of the full RFQ for the vendor's records.
  • Response form: per line item — unit price per qty break, lead time (days), notes/caveats, and a "Cannot quote this part" checkbox (partial response supported). Quote-level fields: valid-until date, optional file attachment (vendor's own PDF quote).
  • Submit button.

Soft cutoff behaviour

After the need-by date, the portal stays open. A late submission triggers an in-app alert to the estimator: "Late vendor response received from [Vendor Name]." The estimator decides whether to consider it. The portal never shows a hard "closed" state.

Email-channel ingest (Lens)

Vendors who reply to the outbound RFQ email (rather than using the portal) are equally valid. Lens parses the inbound reply:

  • Matches the reply to the open vendor RFQ by RFQ reference number in the email thread — not by sender address (vendor may reply from a shared inbox or different email).
  • Extracts prices, lead times, and any "cannot quote" signals from the email body or attached PDF.
  • Surfaces extracted data in the Vendor Quotes panel with an AI extracted — verify flag. Estimator confirms before applying to costing.
  • File attachment (vendor's PDF quote) saved to Quote Files.

Estimator — Vendor Quotes Panel

Visible on the Quote Detail screen and the Part Estimating View (Outside Services section). Shows all vendor responses for the relevant line item(s) side-by-side.

ColumnNotes
VendorName + quoting contact
Price per qty breakUnit price for each requested quantity
Lead timeDays per qty break
Valid untilVendor's quote expiry
NotesVendor's caveats
SourcePortal submission or Email (AI extracted)
Cannot quoteFlag shown if vendor marked part as unquotable
ActionApply button — writes vendor price to Outside Services cost (operation-level) or flips line item to Buy mode (part-level). One vendor wins per line item.

Unselected responses are archived and remain visible in RFQ History on the vendor's detail page.

In-flight status chip

While an RFQ is open, the Outside Services cost field (and the line item row on Quote Detail) shows a chip: "⏳ Awaiting N vendor response(s)". Disappears once all vendors have responded or the estimator applies a response.

Workflow stage soft warning

If the estimator tries to advance the quote's workflow stage (e.g. Estimating → Sales Review) while vendor RFQs are still in flight, a soft warning fires: "3 line items have pending vendor quotes." Estimator can override and proceed — not a hard block.

Follow-up nudge

If a vendor has not responded by the need-by date, a suggested action appears in the Dashboard work queue: "Follow up with [Vendor Name] on RFQ #X — no response received." The estimator reviews and clicks Authorize Follow-up; the system sends a follow-up email to the vendor's quoting contact. No follow-up fires without explicit human authorization.

Notifications

EventRecipientChannel
Vendor RFQ sentVendor quoting contact(s)Email (RFQ email + portal link)
Vendor submits portal responseEstimator assigned to quoteIn-app + email
Vendor email reply parsed by LensEstimatorIn-app ("AI extracted — verify")
Vendor partially responds (cannot quote some parts)EstimatorIn-app flag on affected line items
Late response received (after need-by date)EstimatorIn-app alert
Need-by date passed, no responseEstimator (dashboard suggested action)In-app only — human authorizes follow-up before email fires
Follow-up authorized → sentVendor quoting contactEmail
All vendors responded (or deadline passed)EstimatorIn-app summary

GDPR / data handling

Vendor contact data stored on Hetzner EU (covered by existing data residency). Vendor contacts are B2B data subjects — no special consent flow on the portal. The signed token URL is the access control mechanism. Confidentiality of part files and drawings is governed by the shop's existing supplier agreements — no portal-level acknowledgment required. No vendor account is created; no cookies beyond session minimum.

Build implications:
  • Data model: Vendor (name, address, erp_vendor_id nullable, status, capabilities JSONB, quoting_contacts[]), VendorContact (vendor_id, name, email, is_primary, cc), VendorRFQ (id, org_id, quote_id, line_item_ids[], created_by, need_by_date, status ENUM(open|closed|cancelled), token), VendorRFQRecipient (rfq_id, vendor_id, contact_id, token_hash, sent_at, opened_at), VendorQuoteResponse (rfq_id, vendor_id, source ENUM(portal|email), submitted_at, valid_until, line_item_responses JSONB, attachment_id, ai_extracted BOOL, verified BOOL, applied BOOL).
  • Token (decided — unified): All three external-access mechanisms (buyer portal, vendor file-share channels, vendor RFQ portal) use a single QuoteToken table with a scope ENUM(buyer_portal | vendor_share | vendor_rfq). Remove the standalone VendorRFQ.token field; access is always per-recipient via QuoteToken with the relevant FK (quote_id, external_share_id, or vendor_rfq_recipient_id depending on scope). Revocation, CUI audit logging, and "Require 2FA for external share" enforcement are all handled in one place. No JWT exp claim — soft cutoff enforced at application layer.
  • AI suggestions: rank vendors by (1) most recent accepted response for same org, (2) historical acceptance rate for matching process, (3) process capability match, (4) material match. New vendors (zero history) shown with New label, not hidden.
  • Buy mode: LineItem.costing_mode ENUM(make|buy). Buy mode: hide all inside-cost fields in UI, disable costing compute, set outside_service_cost = applied vendor price. Pricing layer (markup items, margin) remains live. Nesting interaction: if a Buy-mode line item was previously enrolled in a shared nest, clear its nesting associations and display an estimator warning ("This part was removed from Nest #N — nesting requires internal material costing").
  • Unauthenticated React entrypoint at /vendor-rfq/:token — separate bundle, no auth middleware, no access to tenant data beyond the RFQ scope.
  • Usage instrumentation: instrument portal open rate, submit rate, apply rate from day one.

Intelligence Layer

AI Extraction & Email Ingest ("Lens")

SS dashboard notifications / 04 / found-in-files

Two automated pipelines feed the quote builder. KB: wingman-assistance

1 · RFQ email ingest

Customer forwards an RFQ to a per-org inbox (e.g. quotes@org.bidfactory…).
System creates a draft Quote, matches the sender to an Account/Contact, stores the .eml as the ORIGINAL RFQ, and extracts attachments (PDF/STEP/ZIP/SLDPRT) into Supporting Files.
It parses the email body + attachments for a parts list (part #, rev, qty) → pre-fills Bulk Create Line Items.
Fires a Dashboard notification: "New Quote created from Email Forwarding: Created Quote #15217".

2 · Document extraction (per file)

For each drawing/model, extract a structured taxonomy and surface it in the "Found in Files" panel and as Review Items:

ExtractionExamples
Identitypart number, revision, description, part name
Material & spec"ALUMINUM 5052-H32", AMS-4016
FinishPowder Coat, Anodize
Tolerances±0.10 in, ±5°, "True Position"
Process notes"REMOVE ALL BURRS", "Pack Individually"
Quality / certMIL-SPEC (MIL-A-8625), Gold Callout, Cert of Conformance
Compliance"Contains ITAR/CUI"
GD&T — feature control framesposition/profile/circularity etc. with symbol + tolerance + datum refs (⊕ |.005| A | B)
GD&T — datumsA, B, C (and datum reference counts)
Features — holes12X ⌀.136 ↧.58 (qty multiplier, diameter, depth)
Features — countersinks / counterborestype + dims
Dimensions (lengths).353, .601, 2X .375 … (with multipliers)
Title-block fieldspart number, revision, description (NUM/REV click-fill)
Units & findings countdetected in / mm; "Found in files 43", "63 findings"

Each finding carries a plain-language description and a page + bounding box so it can be spotlighted on the print and explained in a popover.

3 · AI-training feedback loop

Extraction is explicitly imperfect; the product turns user corrections into training signal:

  • Mark as inaccurate → removes a wrong finding, labels a false positive.
  • Add missing extraction → user draws + types a missed callout, labels a false negative.

"The more work you do to tell us when we miss, the better the tool over time." Persist each correction with the source region for retraining/QA. SS Demo C add-missing / mark-inaccurate

4 · BOM detection & assembly building

On assembly packages, Lens additionally: detects BOM tables in PDFs (purple badge + "N BOM tables found"); extracts the table (item no., part #, qty, description) and the drawing title block (part #, rev, description); matches split pages to BOM nodes by title-block part number; recursively finds nested sub-assembly BOM tables ("Child BOM suggestion"); and matches purchased parts to the Components Library. Feeds the BOM Builder. SS Demo D

Build approach: a vision/LLM extraction service that runs on upload, writes ExtractionFindings to the part (with page/bbox/units/plain-language), and feeds the rules engine. Findings must link back to source so users can verify, spotlight, and "Discuss this". Extraction also powers full-text search over PDF content. Lineage to be aware of (from the webinar): this sits alongside prior automation — model interrogations, a Kalk pricing language, assembly quoting, sheet-metal nesting, email-forwarding quote setup, and the BOM Builder — all Lens-powered.

5 · "Purple signal" — AI-assistance UI indicator (AI Quote Setup demo)

Wherever Lens can accelerate a step, the UI surfaces a purple signal — a visual cue (purple dot, chip, or banner) indicating AI assistance is available or has pre-filled something. Examples from demos:

  • The "Found in Files" panel header shows a purple chip with the findings count.
  • The "Line items autofilled" green banner on Bulk Create is the email-parse equivalent.
  • BOM tables detected in a PDF get a purple badge ("N BOM tables found") in the file list.
  • A purple dot on any Part #, Rev, or Description field indicates a click-to-fill suggestion from extraction.

The purple colour is consistent across the product as the "AI/Lens" system colour. It should be treated as a design token (e.g. --color-ai-signal: #7C3AED or PP's exact hue) applied anywhere Lens has acted or can act. Build implication: extraction service writes a confidence score per finding; show purple signal only above a threshold (e.g. ≥ 0.7).

6 · File security & gap detection (AI Quote Setup demo)

Malware / corruption scan: All uploaded files — including ZIP archives — are scanned before processing. A malformed or malicious ZIP is rejected with a visible error before unzipping and before any extraction begins. Build implication: run antivirus / file-integrity check in the upload pipeline before the extraction worker dequeues the job.

Visual gap detection: During triage the system flags structural gaps:

  • Line items without files — parts on the parts list that have no matching drawing or model file. Shown as a warning in the thumbnail rail so the estimator knows to go back to the customer before pricing.
  • Files without part numbers — files that could not be matched to any line item (likely named inconsistently). Shown as unassigned orphan files, with a prompt to manually assign or request a clean re-submission from the customer.
Compliance note (from Lens Power Lunch Ep 27): Lens is CMMC-compliant, FedRAMP Moderate Equivalent, hosted on Amazon GovCloud, and subject to U.S. Persons data-handling requirements. Any data residency or access-control decisions for the Bid Factory AI layer should account for similar compliance requirements if targeting aerospace/defense customers. For Tolera this US model is reframed to EU/GDPR routing — see the Lens engine routing below.

Lens engine internals

folded from AI-LENS-ENGINE-SPEC.md The third hard subsystem (alongside GeometryService + Kalk). Lens reads 2D prints, PDFs, emails, file metadata (text + vision AI); the GeometryService reads 3D solid bodies (and MBD/PMI). Lens does NOT parse DXF/DWG GD&T or MBD/PMI. Lens outputs are suggestions (AI-Governor, purple 55% opacity, explicit Accept) — never fed directly into Kalk costing; they inform the human + Rules.

Four pipelines: (1) file processing (every upload); (2) email/RFQ ingest (§1 above); (3) document extraction ("Found in Files", §2); (4) BOM detection & assembly building (§4).

File-processing pipeline (ordered, with failure handling)

  1. Upload ≤ platform MAX_UPLOAD_MB (200) — drag-drop / click / API. Fail → stop.
  2. Virus scan — malware → block download + prompt delete (part may still be created).
  3. Unlock — password-protected PDF → require password, then re-upload + re-scan.
  4. PDF-as-ZIP unpack — OEM "PDF that is really a ZIP" (PDF + STEP242 + HTML) → extract siblings to the same location.
  5. 3D-PDF model extraction — embedded model → STL (+ PMI); if the PDF was a part's primary, the STL becomes primary and the PDF moves to supporting.
  6. Scanned-PDF OCR — no text layer → OCR to make text extractable.
  7. Print content analysis — text-search + vision AI → part-field suggestions + extractions.

Two-pass extraction & the 5-category taxonomy

Runs on PDF/TIFF prints, first 10 pages. Quote-setup pass (whole file, on upload): part#, revision, description, drawing#, document_units (sets default in/mm), tables, BOM tables, export_controlled, PII. Requirements pass (per page, "print"-classified only): the full taxonomy. Tolerances detected as unilateral / bilateral / limit.

CategoryFindings
Quote setuppart_number, revision, description, drawing_number, document_units, tables, bom_tables, export_controlled, pii
Requirementsprocess_keywords (heat-treat, anodize, passivate, weld, deburr, grind, etch, powder-coat…), material, specifications (AMS/ASTM/MIL/NADCAP + OEM), global_tolerances, flag_notes
Featureshole (Ø, depth, tol, thread), thread (class/Ø/hand/pitch/depth), countersink, counterbore, control_frame (type, value, datum refs, material condition; stacked), datum, chamfer, surface_finish, bend_lines, welds
Dimensions & toleranceslength / diameter / radius / angle — value, tolerance, role (basic / critical-to-quality / reference), indicators (STOCK, TYP)
Regionstitle_block, notes_list, section/profile_view_caption, 2d_view, 3d_view (hidden by default; spotlightable)

Output contract — ExtractionFinding

ExtractionFinding {
  id, component_id, source_file_id, page,
  category: quote_setup|requirements|features|dimensions|regions,
  type: 'hole'|'control_frame'|'material'|'part_number'|…,
  raw_text, value, normalized_value, units,            # mm default (DACH)
  tolerance?: { kind: unilateral|bilateral|limit, upper, lower },
  role?: basic|critical_to_quality|reference,
  bbox/geometry_ref,                                   # viewer overlay
  confidence: float,
  status: suggested|accepted|rejected|edited,
  gdt?: { symbol(ISO GPS), datum_refs[], material_condition }
}

Consumers: part-field suggestions, Found-in-Files panel, Rules/Review signals (integration map), viewer overlay. Not Kalk.

Models, providers & routing

v1 = a vision-LLM pipeline, not a trained custom model. Per-task: print-classification (vision LLM); part-field extraction (vision LLM + text search); table/BOM detection (layout model + LLM); GD&T/callout OCR (vision LLM, ISO-GPS-aware); email body parse (text LLM).

Never hallucinate. The model may pick a wrong existing string but must never invent a value not on the print. Per-finding confidence; below threshold → suggestion only (55% opacity, explicit Accept); never auto-applied to costing. v1 targets (calibrate vs Fechner): print-classification ≈ 95%; field recall ≈ 65%; field precision ≈ 85%.

Training loop: mark_inaccurate / replace / user-fills-different → persist {finding, predicted, corrected, tenant, file_ref} for an eval set + few-shot/prompt tuning + optional per-tenant correction memory. OPEN (DECISIONS.md): correction-storage scope — per-tenant by default; global only on anonymised opt-in.

DACH — EU/GDPR routing (replaces PP's US GovCloud/CMMC model). Default = an EU-region LLM under a DPA/AVV, no training on customer data. Export-control / dual-use-flagged parts → a restricted path (no-external-LLM mode or explicit per-org opt-in); never send flagged files to a non-DPA endpoint. Provider + region configurable in AI & Automation Settings. German title-block/keywords; ISO GPS GD&T; mm units; PII handled under GDPR.

Scope, limits & acceptance

  • Scope: digital + scanned (OCR) PDF/TIFF prints, first 10 pages, single part per print. Not DXF/DWG-with-GD&T, MBD/PMI/3D-PDF annotations (→ geometry engine).
  • Golden extractions per fixture (this print → part# X, rev Y, material DIN Z, units mm, N holes w/ tolerances, M control frames); never-hallucinate test; EU-routing test (flagged file never leaves the DPA region); email-ingest test (.eml + ZIP → Quote + Contact + files + extraction) bound to the Fechner .eml fixture.

Rules Engine & Review Items

SS 06/10/14 + f761b846 / ea41d7ce

Extracted findings + geometry are matched against org Rules to produce Review Items — actionable cards that ensure nothing in the drawing is missed and that the router reflects every requirement. KB: requirements-review KB: building-review-rules

Review Item card

Each card: type label (e.g. A - Anodize, A - Deburring required, MIL-SPEC, A - Pack individually, A - True Position, Gold Callout, Material Review, AMS-4016), the matched callout text, an assignee avatar, a checkbox, and 1–N suggested actions:

  • Add operation(s) — e.g. "Add 'Finish | Anodize' operation", "Add 'CNC | Deburr' operation", "Add 3 operations", "add additional time for packing".
  • Get supplier RFQ — "Get Anodize RFQ".
  • Quality / review — "Cert to this", "Check that this is acceptable", "Double Check".
  • Dismiss — "Not applicable".
  • Discuss this — opens chat/annotation thread.

Items have a resolution state; the panel filters by Unresolved, assignee (Anybody), and type (Any Type). At quote level, items aggregate across all parts with a SET ALL button (apply a resolution to every part that has the flag) and part thumbnails with qty badges. The unresolved count drives the quote's "Outstanding Work" / "Incomplete Quote Items".

Create Rule dialog

Two-column modal: left = rule editor, right = a live Preview of the resulting card. Reached two ways: (a) the "Create rule" link on a callout popover — pre-seeds a "Selected item" signal from the clicked entity; or (b) from scratch via the Signals "Add a signal" picker. SS 14 / Demo C 06–09

Fields (top → bottom)

  1. Name * (required), Description.
  2. Signals — "Add signals to indicate when this action should appear." Contains:
    • A blue "Selected item" chip showing the matched entity pill (e.g. ⊕ |⌀.001| A | B) + CLEAR.
    • A signal-path dropdown ("⊕ GD&T and callouts / Features / Control frames / Position ▾").
    • ADD FILTER (value comparison) and ADD COUNT (count comparison).
    • Logical-grouping chips AND + / OR + with drag handles — stack multiple conditions.
  3. Resolution Options — "Add resolution options to enable one-click actions…". Stack one or more via "Add a resolution ▾"; each becomes a button on the review card.
  4. Assignee — "An assignee will receive a notification whenever this action appears." (appears once a resolution is added)

Footer: CANCEL / CREATE RULE (disabled until valid).

Filter row (example)

Under a signal path, a filter row (indented ↳): operator dropdown ≤ Value / Less than or equal, value input 0.005, unit in. Used to express "tight true-position tolerance ≤ 5 thou".

Resolution Options dropdown

OptionEffect when applied on the review card
🔧 Add operationinserts a named operation into the router (Inspection, Deburr, Passivation…)
👤 Assign estimatorroutes the line item to a specific person
☰ Set processsets the part's process (automate process selection from print data)
🔴 No-quote this line itemmarks the line item No Quote
✓ Resolvesimple acknowledge/clear; also supports custom "complete with Outside Processing"-style steps

Signal selector tree

The "Add a signal" picker is a cascading multi-column tree — rules are not limited to prints. SS 09 signal tree

ALL
 ├─ ⊕ GD&T and callouts ──┬─ Features ──┬─ Holes
 │                          │             ├─ Countersinks
 │                          │             ├─ Counterbores
 │                          │             ├─ Control frames ── Position / Profile / Circularity …
 │                          │             └─ Datums      ── count: [2x Count] [✓ Any] [⊘ None]
 │                          ├─ Dimensions ── Lengths → longest dimension ≥ value
 │                          └─ Tolerances
 ├─ 🔍 Any text          ── keyword match (1+ keywords, case sensitivity toggle)
 ├─ Geometric analysis  ── interrogation features/warnings (model-derived)
 ├─ 📄 Files            ── has print = true/false  (flag missing prints)
 ├─ 📦 Part             ── maximum dimension ≥ value (model-derived)
 ├─ Line item           ── line-item attributes
 └─ $ Quote             ── quote-level attributes

Helper text: "High-level properties and geometric attributes of parts. Geometric attributes must be populated (automatically with models, or manually without) for rules based on these signals."

Cross-source compounding. Logical grouping lets one rule match across sources — e.g. "part too big" ANDs Part → max dimension ≥ 2in (from the model) with Lengths → longest dimension ≥ 2in (from the print), so it fires whether the part arrived as a model or a print.

Example rule library (built live in Demo C)

RuleSignalResolution(s)
Tight true-position tolerancesControl frame Position ≤ 0.005 inNo-quote · Outside Processing · Add "Inspection"
Deburring requiredtext contains "EDGES TO BE BROKEN" / "deburr" / "broken"Add "Deburr" operation
First article inspectiontext contains "FIRST ARTICLE"Add "Inspection" operation
Passivation requiredtext contains "passivat*" OR "ASTM A967" (multi-keyword to catch misspellings)Add "Passivation" operation
Ask machinist (circularity)Circularity control frame ≤ 0.005 inAssign estimator/machinist
Part too bigPart max dim ≥ 2in AND print longest length ≥ 2inNo-quote / review
Missing printFiles → has print = falseflag → request print from customer

Review Items workflow (quote-level → line-item)

Quote level (/quotes/edit/:id): findings grouped by rule across all parts; each group has a SET ALL ▾ that resolves every matching line item to the same status in one click (e.g. no-quote them all). Each member row: leading count, thumbnail, filename, revision, finding-count badge, chevron. Footer: Configure Rules ↗ / Create Rule.

Line-item level (viewer or part panel): the rule card shows the double-chevron icon, rule name, assignee avatar (e.g. "DW"), matched entity pills, and the stacked one-click resolution buttons (🔴 No quote · ✓ Outside processing · 🔧 Add 'Inspection' operation), plus "Discuss this 💬". Clicking a pill spotlights the finding on the print. Creating a rule fires a success toast ("Rule 'Live: tight true position tolerances' created successfully"); a refresh re-evaluates the part.

Duplicate-operation prevention. Applying an "Add operation" resolution must not insert an operation the router already has; the system blocks duplicates and lets the user reorder the added op into the correct step.
Model & evaluation: Rule {name, description, signals[] (path + filters + counts + AND/OR groups), resolution_options[], assignee, scope}. On any extraction/geometry change, evaluate all org rules against the part → create/update ReviewItems (link to matched findings). Resolutions are suggested one-click actions until applied. The unresolved count drives the quote's "Outstanding Work / Incomplete Quote Items". Purpose framing: capture senior-estimator tribal knowledge, train juniors, quote faster with less risk.

Rule engine internals

folded from RULES-ENGINE-SPEC.md The authoritative engine model (that sub-spec is archived). A rule connects signals (conditions over print / model / text / quote data) to resolutions (the decisions a user takes). When a rule's signals match a component, a review item is created and optionally auto-assigned. Provenance: KB: building-review-rules (signal/resolution model + canonical JSON) · KB: requirements-review (why + lifecycle).

Signal catalog — what you can test

  • GD&T & callouts — holes, sinks, bores, control frames, dimensions, tolerances (source: Lens GD&T extractions + geometry).
  • Document text — raw text from any .pdf .tiff .doc .docx; keyword match (case toggle) or regex.
  • Interrogation results — properties, features, and DFM feedback for sheet metal / tube laser / milling / turning. ⚠ Only evaluated after the relevant interrogation has run.
  • Presence of files by type — has_model, has_print.
  • Part properties — geometric attributes (auto from models, manual from prints), is_assembly, is_root.
  • Line-item — min / max requested quantity.   Quote — the account being quoted.

Resolution catalog — what you can do

Resolution (type)EffectParams
NO_QUOTEset the component's line-item status to no-quote—
ASSIGN_ESTIMATORassign that line item to a specified estimatorestimator id
SET_PROCESSset the component's processprocess_id
ADD_OPERATIONappend operation(s) to the bottom of the routerop_def_ids[]
RESOLVE (custom label)resolve with no other change; label captures a real-world task the system can't model (e.g. "Outsource", "Contacted customer")custom_label

Canonical rule schema (build to this)

Rules import/export as a single JSON string (paste-in on the rules config page) so rule sets are portable and diffable. KB: building-review-rules

Rule {
  "uuid": "…", "name": "All tight dimension tolerances", "description": "…",
  "logical_operator": "OR" | "AND",        // how top-level signals combine
  "signals": [ Signal, … ],
  "resolutions": [ Resolution, … ],
  "default_assignee_id": null | "<user_id>"
}
Signal  { "logical_operator": "AND"|"OR", "groups": [ Group, … ] }
Group   { "document_path": "length_tolerances",
          "logical_operator": "AND"|"OR",
          "queries": [ Query, … ],
          "count_query": null | { "value": 7, "operator": "greaterThanOrEqual" } }
Query   { "field_name": ["smallest_delta"],
          "operator": "lessThanOrEqual"|"greaterThanOrEqual"|"equals"
                    |"includesCaseInsensitive"|"regex",
          "value": 0.13,                   // scalar | keyword[] | regex string
          "value_type": "distance"|"angle"|"number"|"string"|"boolean",
          "filter_type": "numeric"|"string"|"boolean",
          "units": "mm"|"deg"|null }       // DACH default mm/deg
Resolution { "type": "NO_QUOTE"|"RESOLVE"|"ADD_OPERATION"|"SET_PROCESS"|"ASSIGN_ESTIMATOR",
             "parameters": [ {"name":"op_def_ids","value":[14936]} ],
             "custom_label": null | "Outsource" }

Build note: this is a small, serializable query AST over the part's analyzed data. Implement an evaluator that, given a component's {extractions, interrogation_result, part_attributes, files, line_item, quote}, evaluates each rule and emits review items. Keep value_type/units so numeric comparisons normalise correctly.

document_path catalog (addressable collections)

Groupdocument_path valuesTypical field_nameSource
Length/size toleranceslength_/diameter_/radius_/angular_tolerancessmallest_deltaLens
Control frames (per characteristic)flatness_/parallelism_/perpendicularity_/position_/cylindricity_/straightness_/concentricity_/profile_of_line_/profile_of_surface_/runout_/total_runout_/symmetry_/circularity_control_framesvalue, datum_countLens
