Combination semantics (call out in the UI — the part everyone gets wrong): top-level OR across signals → match if any case matches (the usual "flag any of these"); AND → all cases must match; the same recursion applies within a Signal's groups and a Group's queries.

Worked rules — keep as regression fixtures

Real PP rules (re-unit'd to metric) to retain as engine goldens. KB: building-review-rules

  1. All-in-one tight tolerances — every tolerance/control-frame path, each smallest_delta/value ≤ 0.13 mm (5 thou), top-level OR; resolutions No-quote / Outsource / Feasible in-house.
  2. Machine-envelope — greatest_distance_dimension.value ≥ bed limit OR part.max_dim ≥ bed limit (mm); No-quote / Outsource / Feasible.
  3. Missing model or print — text includes "MODEL" AND files.has_model == false, OR files.has_print == false; Resolve "Contacted customer" / "Quote without the file".
  4. Part complexity (Level 3) — composite of control_frames.count ≥ 7, datum_count ≥ 2, position frames datum_count ≥ 3, datums.count ≥ 5, mill machine-direction count ≥ 5, tight least-distance dimension → routes to programming.
  5. Finish keyword → add op — text includesCaseInsensitive finish keywords → ADD_OPERATION(op_def_ids=[…]) (ties to the seeded Operation Library ids).
  6. Spec/standard detection — large regex over the shop's accepted spec list → Resolve.
  7. OEM-specific spec — regex ^SPX-[A-Za-z0-9-]+$ style → Resolve "We have / need the spec doc".
  8. Vendor picking (material spec) — text matches a material standard → Resolve with vendor options.
  9. Bulk-assign process — text includes "bracket"/"plate" → SET_PROCESS (sheet metal).

Review-item lifecycle & collaboration

  1. Trigger: after AI/interrogation finishes, matching rules create review items in the Review Items panel (burn-down list).
  2. Assign: default_assignee_id auto-assigns + notifies; reassignable.
  3. Collaborate: each item carries a chat thread; discussion/decisions permanently stored (audit trail).
  4. Context for consistency: show up to 5 past parts the rule flagged + the decision taken there.
  5. Resolve: user selects one resolution → mutates quote/router as applicable → item closes. Summary view + bulk actions to mass-resolve.
  6. Audit: resolutions, assignee, thread, timestamps retained for production handoff.

Permissions: building/editing rules requires the edit permission on processes (AUTHZ); resolving items needs quote-edit.

How the three engines feed signals

Signal sourceProduced byFeeds document_path
GD&T, dims, tolerances, control frames, material/spec textLens (ExtractionFinding — § AI Lens)*_tolerances, *_control_frames, text, dimension paths
Interrogation properties, features, DFM warningsGeometryService (AnalysisResult — § Geometry & DFM)family interrogation paths
Part attributes, is_assembly, is_rootgeometry / part recordpart
has_model / has_printfile pipelinefiles
min/max requested qty · accountline item · quote/CRMline-item / quote signals

A Lens or DFM finding surfaced in Found-in-Files offers "build a Review rule from this finding" — the one-click path from observation → reusable rule.

DACH adaptations. Metric-native default signal units (mm/deg); seed thresholds in mm (PP 5-thou → 0.13 mm; 12″ envelope → the org's bed size in mm); keep the in↔mm toggle but never default to inches. ISO GPS GD&T symbology (not just ASME Y14.5). DIN/EN / Werkstoffnummer + EU OEM spec detection (not US MIL/BAC/BMS). Export-control reframed to EU dual-use: a Lens dual-use finding → require manager review / confirm classification (seed rule). German keywords: eloxier (anodize), entgraten (deburr), passivier, schleifen (grind) → seeded German Operation Library ids. Starter rule library ships seeded (SEED-AND-FIXTURES.md §7).

Acceptance criteria

  • Schema round-trip: export a rule set to the JSON string and re-import byte-equivalently; the nine fixtures import and evaluate.
  • AST evaluation: given a fixture component's analyzed data, the evaluator produces the expected review items (and none for non-matching) — wired to /fixtures goldens.
  • Interrogation gating: interrogation-result signals do not fire before the relevant interrogation has run; they do after.
  • AND/OR semantics: a multi-case OR rule fires on any single case; an AND group requires all queries.
  • Resolution effects: No-quote sets status; Set process / Add operation mutate the router; Resolve(label) closes with no mutation; assignee notified.
  • DACH: thresholds evaluate in mm; ISO-GPS control frames; German finish keywords map to seeded ops; the dual-use rule fires and blocks send pending review.

Geometry Recognition & DFM

SS 12/13 + f6b7dce4

A geometry service processes solid models to extract attributes + recognize manufacturing features per a Geometric Analysis profile (e.g. "Default Mill", "Milling (Robodrill)", "Default Sheet Metal (Laser)"). Profiles are machine/process-specific and selectable. Drives costing inputs, feature highlighting, and DFM warnings. (A "CNC | Mill Complexity Indicator" process can also drive complexity-based pricing — see complexity-driven markup.)

This is the "no CAD license" alternative — and it is required, not optional. The native geometry engine performs full feature interrogation + DFM in the browser with no CAD seat and no Autodesk Fusion subscription on the customer's side (the product's own tagline: "Powerful Geometric Feature Interrogation — without expensive software licenses"). The Autodesk Fusion connector is a power-user convenience for shops that already own Fusion; every estimator must get the same recognition/warnings without it. So the engine below is the baseline path for all users, and the licensed on-prem toolkit that powers it must cover every process family here. SS DemoN 01

Process-family coverage (one engine, per-process feature trees + warnings)

The same engine recognizes features for six process families, each with its own feature taxonomy, its own DFM-warning set, and the relevant input formats. The viewer's left panel shows the feature tree (counts, color dots, per-row checkbox to highlight on the model); the right panel shows Manufacturability Warnings (severity dot + count + ⓘ tooltip, expandable to per-instance rows). SS DemoN 02–13

FamilyInputRecognized featuresDFM warnings (examples)
CNC MillingSTEP / STPPart Features: Hole; Milling Features: Setup Pocket (plus Counterbore, Countersink, Machine Direction, Simple Drilled Hole per profile)Deep Cut Radiused Deep Hole Tipped Hole (+ Chamfer, Fillets, Tight Corner, Tapered Wall, Small Internal Radius, Uncut Faces)
Lathe / TurningSTP / STEPExternal Cut Internal Cut Setup Lathe StockLive Tooling Required
Sheet MetalSTEP / STPSheet Metal Bend (each bend carries Radius, Length, Angle, K-Factor), Unfolded Part (flat blank), Bend Relief, Cutout FeatureClose Bends, Different Bend Directions, Non-90° Bend, Shallow/Thin Bend Relief, Short Flange, Small Bend Radius, W-Bending May Be Required
Wire EDMSTEP / STPSetup Cut LoopIncompatible Faces Flushing Issue, one away Angled Cut Explicit 4-axis Operation Wire Conflict

Waterjet & Laser (Flat)2D DXFFlat Features → Piercing (each pierce listed individually)Tight Corner (expands to per-corner Angle: nn.nnn degrees)
File-format support (engine must ingest all): solid B-rep — STEP / STP (and native SOLIDWORKS .SLDPRT, see engine); mesh — STL (additive); 2D — DXF (waterjet/laser flat parts). The active process family selects which interrogation profile + warning set runs.

Common viewer capabilities (every family)

Across all families the web viewer provides: bounding-box dimensions with colored X/Y/Z axes (e.g. X: 0.950 in / Y: 0.950 in / Z: 0.250 in); volume + surface area readout (e.g. Volume: 1.355 cu in / Area: 33.637 sq in); a top view-mode toolbar (shaded / wireframe-section / globe orientation); Part vs Supporting Files tabs; the feature-tree checkboxes that highlight geometry; and warning rows that, when checked, color the offending faces/edges on the model (e.g. tipped holes, incompatible faces, tight corners). Additive adds the build-plate grid + per-axis re-orientation buttons. SS DemoN 05/10/11

CNC milling — recognized features (with counts)

Counterbore ×2 Countersink ×2 Hole ×16 Machine Direction ×6 Pocket ×20 Simple Drilled Hole ×15 — each color-coded, expandable, with info tooltips; clickable to highlight on the 3D model.

DFM / Manufacturability warnings (example set: "79 Warnings")

Chamfer ×5 Concave Fillet ×32 Convex Fillet ×9 Deep Hole ×1 Deep Radiused Cut ×8 Small Internal Radius ×2 Tapered Wall ×3 Tight Corner ×12 Tipped Hole ×12 Uncut Faces ×1 — color-coded with info icons.

Sheet-metal interrogation (thickness, bends, flat area, contour length, nesting eligibility) is the sheet-metal analog (see §).

Sheet-metal feature recognition & warnings (profile "Default Sheet Metal (Laser)")

Sheet-metal parts use their own interrogation profile with their own features and warnings. SS DemoI 06

Sheet Metal Features (with counts, color-coded): Bend Relief Cutout Feature Sheet Metal Bend.

Sheet Metal Warnings (example: "25 WARNINGS"): Close Bends Different Bend Directions Non-90-Degree Bend Shallow Bend Relief Short Flange Small Bend Radius Thin Bend Relief W-Bending May Be Required — each with checkbox, severity dot, and ⓘ tooltip; checking one highlights the feature on the 3D model. (CNC milling has its own set, see above; the warning engine is process-/profile-specific.)

Configurable warning thresholds (Configure → Interrogations)

Warnings are computed against org-set thresholds, and many are material- and machine-specific. Demo F: a deep hole is flagged when depth÷diameter exceeds a per-material ratio (e.g. 8× for aluminum, different for inconel); a tight corner is a binary geometric flag (a 90° internal corner can't be cut by a round end mill). Thresholds can also vary by machine rigidity (e.g. an older Haas vs a newer Mazak). Each warning type can be turned on/off and tuned. Example aluminum part: 62 warnings across Chamfer, Concave/Convex Fillet, Deep Hole, Deep Radiused Cut, Tapered Wall, Tight Corner, Tipped Hole, etc. Clicking a warning's blue text jumps the 3D view to that feature.

Build approach: integrate a CAD kernel / geometry-analysis library (or service) that parses STEP and returns: bounding box, volume, surface area, weight (with density from material), and feature/warning lists. The viewer needs a WebGL renderer (e.g. three.js + an OCCT/`occt-import-js` pipeline, or a hosted CAD-viewer SDK) capable of face picking and feature highlighting. This is the single largest technical risk — see open questions.

GeometryService — engine contract

folded from INTERROGATION-ENGINE-SPEC.md The engine contract behind the viewer above (that sub-spec is archived). One Part (single CAD body) in → a structured AnalysisResult out, consumed by four clients: Kalk (costing via analyze_*()), the 3D viewer (feature overlay), the DFM/Review layer, and the Part Library (geometry signature). v1 = OCCT, Spatial swap-in later — the interface must be unchanged on swap. KB: interrogations-basics

Service interface

GeometryService.analyze(
  body: CadBody,          # single solid body (assemblies decomposed first)
  family: ProcessFamily,  # SHEET_METAL|MILLING|LATHE|TUBE_LASER|WIRE_EDM|CAST_URETHANE|ADDITIVE
  material: MaterialRef?,        # drives custom-interrogation selection
  inputs: InterrogationInputs?   # per-family task inputs / thresholds (DFM catalogue below)
) -> AnalysisResult

decompose_bom(file) -> {parts[], nodes[]}     # unpack an assembly CAD into Parts + Nodes
compute_signature(body) -> GeomHash           # Part-Library historical match + PC memory
render_thumbnail(body|drawing) -> image       # server-side 2D/3D thumbnails
vectorize_pdf(page) -> DXF                     # flat-pattern PDF → DXF primary (sheet metal; E4-j)
extract_pmi(body) -> PMI[]                     # post-pilot (Spatial-grade; E4-e)

Two trigger paths: (1) viewer — manual, does NOT feed costing; (2) Kalk analyze_*() — auto-runs when an operation formula calls it, results feed costing. Cache per (body, family, resolved-inputs) so viewer + costing share one run.

AnalysisResult schema (the contract)

AnalysisResult {
  family: ProcessFamily
  dimensions: { …PartGeometry attrs… }   # size_x/y/z, max/med/min_dim, area, volume, weight…
  family_scalars: { … }                  # per-family (below) — these ARE the Kalk inputs
  features: Feature[]                     # .name + .properties{…} + geometry_refs (face/edge ids)
  feedback: Warning[]                     # DFM: {type, count, threshold_used, geometry_refs, can_disable}
  confidence?: 'High'|'Medium'|'Low'      # where the engine estimates runtime (milling)
}

features + feedback are the same P3LList objects Kalk reads as result.features / result.feedback; each Warning carries the threshold that fired so the UI can show "why".

Per-family family_scalars (the Kalk inputs)

FamilyScalars (verbatim from the process articles)
Sheet Metal
analyze_sheet_metal		bend_count, thickness, size_x/size_y (unfolded), total_cut_length, total_air_move_length, pierce_count (internal only), punch_single_hit_cut_length, punch_multi_hit_cut_length, punch_single_hit_setups/count, counter_sink_setups/count, counter_bore_setups/count, simple_drilled_hole_setups/count, total_hole_setups/count
Milling 3-axis
analyze_mill3		setups[] (each: setup_time hrs, runtime hrs, confidence, features, feedback), setup_count, global INDEX. Engine estimates runtime + confidence — low confidence → manual override.
Lathe
analyze_lathe		setup_count, stock_radius, stock_length (recommended cylindrical stock); radial vs axial cut distinction; live-tooling callouts
Tube Laser
analyze_tube_laser		stock_type (round|rectangular|rectangular_radiused|angle|u_channel|incompatible), thickness, length, width, height, diameter, internal_radius, outside_corner_radius, leg_edge_radius, leg_angle, total_cut_length, pierce_count
Wire EDM / Cast / Additiveanalyze_wire_edm() / analyze_casting() / analyze_additive() — thinner schemas; define when those families enter scope (post-pilot)

Pipeline & v1 OCCT ceilings

  1. Ingest → file-type + size gate (Mill/Lathe/WireEDM/Cast ≤ 20 MB; SheetMetal/TubeLaser ≤ 50 MB; Additive none). Single body only — assemblies decomposed via decompose_bom.
  2. Resolve inputs (most-specific CustomInterrogation by material/op).
  3. Recognize (family-specific): unfold / setup-detection / feature recognition → dimensions + family_scalars + features + feedback (+ confidence).
  4. Persist + cache keyed by (geom-hash, family, resolved-inputs); compute GeomHash.
  5. Serve: Kalk reads it (sync wait or job); viewer overlays; Part Library indexes the signature. Interrogations run as async jobs; costing awaits the relevant job ("interrogating…" state).
Honest v1 ceilings (state in acceptance): v1 families = Core 4 (Sheet Metal, Milling, Lathe, Tube Laser); Wire EDM/Cast/Additive post-pilot. OCCT produces a reduced feature set per family — mark which family_scalars/features are guaranteed vs Spatial-only (cross-ref PartGeometry v1 column). 5-axis runtime is not auto-costed (manual override is the designed path); complex parts → partial recognition (surface confidence); GD&T/PMI flagged not costed; nesting is estimation-grade. Don't over-claim.

Acceptance criteria

  • Per-fixture golden AnalysisResult (this STEP → 3 bends, thickness 0.74 mm, unfolded X×Y, pierce_count N, total_cut_length L; this mill body → K setups, runtime R, confidence C) — bound to the Fechner fixtures.
  • File gates enforced; assemblies rejected for direct interrogation (decomposed first); most-specific CustomInterrogation selected.
  • DACH: metric-first storage; values convertible for Kalk units_in().

DFM warning catalogue (per-family thresholds)

folded from DFM-WARNINGS.md The warning names appear in the viewer above; here are the detect-conditions, threshold fields, and seed defaults (PP published defaults — treat every value as shop-calibratable; each warning has a should_detect_* toggle). Sheet-metal thresholds are mostly multiples of thickness (×t); milling/lathe/tube in inches unless noted. These warnings drive Review Items / Rules, so a warning is only as good as the v1 feature recognition behind it. KB: custom-interrogations

Model: 7 process interrogations; single-body only; file-size limits as above; two trigger paths (viewer = preview; Kalk analyze_*() = costing). A CustomInterrogation = a named bundle of task-inputs + thresholds linked to material class/family/material and/or operation defs; the engine applies the most-specific match. Always-on (cannot disable): sheet-metal "Additional Operations Required", milling "Uncut Faces". Seed-table shape: family → warning → detects → threshold field(s) → default → enabled-by-default.

Sheet Metal (analyze_sheet_metal) — strategy + warnings (×t)

Flavor: is_laser (default True) / is_punch / is_punch_laser. K-factor k = (0.65 + 0.5·log10(radius/thickness))·0.5 (not configurable). Strategy: smallest_cutout_size 1.0×t · single_hit_max_size_threshold 6 in · max_offset_height 0.25 in · press_length 240 in.

Configure tab structure (observed)

Left-nav categories at /processes etc.: Processes Operations Materials Pricing Discounts Add-Ons Purchased Components Tables Interrogations Workflows Review Rules. SS Demo E 16 / PL#37 (Workflows = the configurable line-item review steps; Tables = the CSV lookup tables; Interrogations = geometry-analysis profiles; Review Rules = the Requirements-Review rules engine.)

Purchased Components (Configure → Purchased Components)

The org's library of bought parts (fasteners, hardware, off-the-shelf items). Four default columns are always present: OEM Part Number, Internal Part No, Piece Price, Description. Custom columns can be added with a Column Name, a Code Name (auto-generated as snake_case, used in Kalk formulas as purchased_component.code_name), Value Type (numeric / string / boolean), and Default Value. Editing a column's default optionally backfills existing records. Bulk import/export via CSV (recommended: pull from ERP and map columns). Also manageable via the open API. Referenced in Kalk pricing programs as purchased_component.column_name.

Interrogations (Configure → Interrogations)

Interrogations are algorithms corresponding to manufacturing processes that run on 3D models. They extract geometry features, calculate manufacturability metrics, and feed cost formulas. PP offers seven process interrogations:

ProcessInput formatFile size limitKey outputs
Sheet MetalSTEP, DXF, DWG, vectorized PDF50 MBThickness, bend count, bend radius/length/angle/K-factor, flat area, nesting eligibility, DFM warnings
Tube LaserSTEP50 MBProfile, length, cut count
CNC MillingSTEP, native CAD20 MBPart features (holes, pockets, setups), milling features, DFM warnings (deep cut, deep hole, tipped hole)
CNC Lathe/TurningSTEP, native CAD20 MBExternal/internal cuts, setups, lathe stock; warning: live tooling required
Wire EDMSTEP20 MBSetup, cut, loop features; warnings: incompatible faces, flushing issue, angled cut, 4-axis, wire conflict
Cast UrethaneSTEP20 MBVolume, surface area, bounding box
AdditiveSTL, 3MFUnlimitedBuild-plate orientation (±X/±Y/±Z), bounding box, volume, surface area

Interrogation scope: Only single-body, non-assembly parts can be interrogated. Run from the Part Viewer (results preview, don't auto-apply to costing) or automatically via Kalk operation-generation formulas (results feed cost formulas). Shops can configure custom interrogation parameters via Configure → Interrogations for per-profile warning thresholds (e.g. deep-hole depth threshold specific to a machine/material combination).

Waterjet/Laser flat (2D DXF): reads Flat Features (piercing listed individually), flags Tight Corner per corner with angle.

Custom Tables (Configure → Tables)

CSV-backed lookup tables for data-driven costing in Kalk formulas (e.g. material cost per kg, laser cut rates by thickness, tooling time by hole diameter). Setup: create a table with a unique alpha-numeric name → upload a config CSV (column_name + value_type columns, types: boolean/numeric/string) → upload a data CSV (matching column headers, type-enforced values). Data can be refreshed by re-uploading the data CSV without re-uploading the config. Referenced in Kalk via table_var(name, description, table_name, create_filter(...), create_order_by(...), display_column) — returns the first matching row or None; access columns via dot notation. Filter conditions: = > >= < <= range contains. Ordering: prefix column name with - for descending. Tables are downloadable (config + data) and API-manageable.

Processes catalog (example list)

3-axis Machining (Sim), 5-axis Machining (Sim), Additive – No Machine, Additive – with machine, Assemble/Weld, Assembly, Cast Urethane, CNC Machining One Op Per CUSTOM, CNC Milling, CNC Milling (5-Axis), CNC Router, CNC Turning, Sheet Metal, … The in-quote "Update Process and Material" modal lists user-facing process names: Assembly | For Weld Layers Assembly | Parent-Level Assembly | Parent-Level [Mie Trak] CNC | Complex Milling (5 Axis, 3D Milling) CNC | Mill/Turn (Live Tooling) CNC | Simple Milling (3 Axis or Similar) CNC | Turn & Mill (Combo) CNC | Turn Only. Each process = default router + eligible materials + DFM/interrogation set + default pricing/finishes/add-ons/discounts. SS DemoG 01

Auto-routing on process selection

Selecting a process auto-generates the operation routing and pre-fills setup/run times from the part's geometry. Example (CNC Simple Milling on PP-2244-TRN): Part Complexity | Level 2.0, CNC Mill | Programming (1.50 hr → $132.00), SAW (60 min / 0.32 min → $100.53), MILL-03 (1.06 hr → $106.25), DEBURR (0.50 hr / 0.01 hr → $44.88) → Operation Summary $383.66; Material Aluminum 6061 Bar | 144.0" × 2.625" × 0.875" = $1.42. Estimators then review, adjust any value, or ADD OPERATION. SS DemoG 02

Editing these defaults tunes costing without code. SS DemoH 03

Custom Operation Generation formula (dynamic routing)

A versioned, line-numbered formula editor ("Pricing Formula — <process> — Dynamic", last-saved-by, Versions, CHECK, SAVE CHANGES) that generates the router conditionally from geometric interrogation. This is a sibling DSL to the pricing language but its job is operation generation, not cost. Observed:

units_in()
sheet_metal = analyze_sheet_metal()

generate_operation('Material | Sheet (Nesting)')
generate_operation('Engineering')
generate_operation('Laser')

if sheet_metal.thickness:
    if len(get_features(sheet_metal, name='drilled_hole')) or len(get_features(sheet_metal, name='countersink')) or len(get_features(sheet_metal, name='counterbore')):
        generate_operation('Drill Press')
    if sheet_metal.bend_count:
        generate_operation('Forming')
if part.count_purchased_children:
    generate_operation('Hardware Insert')
if part.is_root_component:
    …

Helpers: units_in(), analyze_sheet_metal(), get_features(part, name=…), generate_operation('<op>'); reads geometry (sheet_metal.thickness, sheet_metal.bend_count) and part props (part.count_purchased_children, part.is_root_component). Feature names seen: drilled_hole, countersink, counterbore. SS DemoG 07

Operation-formula Kalk (per-operation cost, name, notes)

A third Kalk context (besides pricing & operation-generation): the per-operation formula behind "Edit operation formula" computes the op's setup/run/cost and can set its display. Documented functions (PL#37 + Kalk cheat-sheet): SS PL#37 p3l-cheat-sheet

  • set_notes(notes: string) — set the note shown on the operation/add-on/pricing-item/discount; set_notes_from_list([…]) joins lines (programmatic notes, e.g. "Setup count: 6.0 / Volume removed: 36.885"; can be templated to match ERP notes).
  • set_operation_name(name) — rename the op dynamically at quote time (e.g. by thickness/material or "CNC Op-1").
  • set_workpiece_value(key, value) / get_workpiece_value(key, default) — a per-part workpiece dictionary shared across operations.
  • drop-down variables (discrete selectable values; "logic switches"/dynamic forms) and helpers like is_close(n1, n2, tol=0.001).
Three formula contexts to build (all versioned, CHECK-validated, Python-like): (1) operation-generation on a Process (build the router); (2) operation-formula on an OperationDef (cost + notes + dynamic name + workpiece values); (3) pricing on PricingItems/custom categories (set_custom_cost, get_components). They share syntax + a sandbox; expose the relevant object model in each.

Pricing items list & "New Pricing Item" modal

Configure → Pricing lists every pricing item with its category badge (Raw Material Markup [MATERIAL], Inside Operations Markup [INSIDE], Outside Margin [OUTSIDE], PC Margin [PURCHASED COMPONENT], Difficult Material Markup [DIFFICULT MATERIAL], Labor Markup (Exclude Overhead) [INSIDE LABOR], Overhead Markup (Exclude Labor) [INSIDE OVERHEAD], Laser Workcenter Markup [LASER WORKCENTER], Piece Price Markup [PIECE PRICE], Tooling Markup [TOOLING], Quantity-Based Markup, Variable Markup, PITA Markup, Customer Specific Markup, Promotional Discount, Custom markup [CUSTOM], …).

The New Pricing Item modal: NAME*, CALCULATION TYPE (radio: Markup / Margin), CATEGORY* (dropdown incl. "New Cost Category"), and — when creating a category — COST CATEGORY NAME* + COST CATEGORY COLOR (8 swatches: maroon, orange, gold, green, teal, blue, purple, grey). CANCEL / CREATE PRICING ITEM. Opening a pricing item reveals the Kalk editor. SS Demo E pricing list / New Pricing Item / Kalk editor

Custom-markup configuration is intentionally powerful and can require expert setup; expose both a no-code path (pick category, type a flat %) and the full Kalk editor for dynamic %s and custom aggregations.

DACH Costing Mode

Opt-in org setting. Activates the MSS Rate Calculator and the Zuschlagskalkulation pricing seed. When off, all costing uses plain hourly rate inputs — no change to standard behaviour. Decided 2026-06-18.

Toggle — Configure → Settings → "DACH Costing Mode"

An org-level toggle labelled DACH Costing Mode (German: Maschinenstundensatz-Kalkulation aktivieren). Default: off. DACH orgs are provisioned with it on.

StateBehaviour
OFF (default)run_rate is a plain €/hr text input. No MSS calculator. No Zuschlagskalkulation pricing items seeded. Standard Material / Inside / Outside margin pricing.
ONMSS Rate Calculator button appears on every operation edit drawer in Configure → Operations. Zuschlagskalkulation pricing items are seeded at org creation — or offered via a one-click seed banner for existing orgs enabling the toggle mid-lifecycle.
The toggle is a configuration switch, not a data migration. Turning it on does not alter any existing rates or pricing items; it only adds the calculator affordance and offers the seed. Turning it off hides the calculator but does not delete computed MSS values already saved into run_rate fields.

MSS Rate Calculator (Configure → Operations → edit operation)

When DACH Costing Mode is on, each operation's edit drawer gains a Berechnen (MSS) button next to the run_rate field. Clicking it opens the MSS calculator modal.

Inputs:

Field (DE)Field (EN)UnitNotes
WiederbeschaffungswertReplacement value€Current replacement cost of the machine (not book value)
NutzungsdauerUseful lifeJahreUsed for Kalkulatorische Abschreibung
Kalkulatorischer ZinssatzImputed interest rate%Opportunity cost on tied-up capital; applied to half the replacement value (average capital commitment)
Raumfläche MaschineFloor aream²Machine footprint including clearance
RaumkostensatzSpace cost rate€/m²/JahrRent + heating + lighting per m²
EnergieverbrauchPower drawkWConnected load × average utilisation factor
EnergiepreisEnergy price€/kWhBlended electricity tariff
InstandhaltungssatzMaintenance rate% von WBW/JahrServicing, repairs, spare parts as % of replacement value
WerkzeugkostensatzTooling cost rate% von WBW/Jahr oder €/Jahr (flat)Cutting tools, fixtures, inserts; toggle between % and flat €
Jährliche BetriebsstundenAnnual operating hoursStunden/JahrPlanned shift hours before downtime
AuslastungsgradUtilisation rate%Productive uptime as % of Betriebsstunden; typically 70–90 %

Calculated outputs (shown live as inputs change):

ComponentFormula
Kalkulatorische AbschreibungWiederbeschaffungswert / Nutzungsdauer
Kalkulatorische Zinsen(Wiederbeschaffungswert / 2) × Zinssatz / 100
RaumkostenRaumfläche × Raumkostensatz
EnergiekostenEnergieverbrauch × Betriebsstunden × Energiepreis
InstandhaltungWiederbeschaffungswert × Instandhaltungssatz / 100
WerkzeugkostenWiederbeschaffungswert × Werkzeugkostensatz / 100 or flat €
Jährl. Maschinenkosten (Σ)Sum of all six rows above
Produktive MaschinenstundenBetriebsstunden × (Auslastungsgrad / 100)
MSS (€/Std.)Jährl. Maschinenkosten / Produktive Maschinenstunden

The modal displays a cost-breakdown bar (each of the six components as a colour-coded percentage of Jährl. Maschinenkosten) so the admin can see at a glance where the machine cost is dominated. Buttons: ABBRECHEN (Cancel) / ÜBERNEHMEN (Apply) — on Apply, the computed MSS is written into the operation's run_rate field. The ten input values are persisted per operation so the admin can return and adjust individual components without re-entering everything.

FGK is embedded in the MSS — do not add a separate FGK pricing item. Raumkosten, Energiekosten, and Instandhaltung collectively represent the Fertigungsgemeinkosten (manufacturing overhead) that REFA/MSS methodology embeds in the machine rate. Adding a separate FGK markup on top would double-count. The seeded Zuschlagskalkulation items (MGK, VwGK, VtGK) do not include FGK.

Zuschlagskalkulation Pricing Seed

When DACH Costing Mode is enabled, four standard pricing items are seeded into Configure → Pricing. They implement the full Zuschlagskalkulation costing chain:

#German nameEN labelAbbreviationApplies to (cost base)Default %
1MaterialgemeinkostenMaterial OverheadMGKMaterial cost (MEK)10 %
2VerwaltungsgemeinkostenAdmin OverheadVwGKHerstellkosten (Material + Inside)8 %
3VertriebsgemeinkostenSales OverheadVtGKHerstellkosten (Material + Inside)6 %
4GewinnzuschlagProfitGewinnSelbstkosten (all costs + overheads)10 %

The chain these items implement:

MEK (Materialeinzelkosten)        = material cost from material drawer
+ MGK % on MEK                    → Materialkosten

FEK (Fertigungseinzelkosten)      = operation costs from MSS × time  [FGK already in MSS]

Materialkosten + FEK              = Herstellkosten
+ VwGK % on Herstellkosten
+ VtGK % on Herstellkosten        → Selbstkosten
+ Gewinn % on Selbstkosten        → Angebotspreis netto (before VAT)
Defaults are starting points, not prescriptions. German job shops vary widely: MGK typically 5–20 %, VwGK 5–15 %, VtGK 3–10 %, Gewinn 5–15 %. Admin should adjust to their actual overhead structure. All four items are fully editable and deletable like any other pricing item.

Seed mechanic: for new DACH orgs, the items are seeded automatically at org creation. For an existing org enabling the toggle mid-lifecycle, a dismissible banner appears in Configure → Pricing:

Zuschlagskalkulation-Preispositionen hinzufügen — Fügt MGK, VwGK, VtGK und Gewinnzuschlag mit DACH-Standardwerten hinzu. Bereits vorhandene Preispositionen werden nicht verändert. [HINZUFÜGEN] [Überspringen]

Kalk implementation of the four items:

ItemKalk cost baseNote
MGKMaterial cost category (MATERIAL)Standard Markup on Material — maps to existing pricing architecture
VwGKget_material_cost() + get_inside_cost()New Kalk helper get_herstellkosten() = Material + Inside; expose when DACH Costing Mode on
VtGKsame as VwGK — get_herstellkosten()Same base, separate line item for auditability
Gewinnget_selbstkosten() = Material + Inside + MGK + VwGK + VtGK + OutsideNew Kalk helper get_selbstkosten(); expose when DACH Costing Mode on
Two new Kalk helpers to build: get_herstellkosten() (sum of Material + Inside cost categories) and get_selbstkosten() (sum of all cost categories — Material, Inside, Outside, MGK, VwGK, VtGK — i.e. everything before Gewinn). Both are only available in the pricing formula context when DACH Costing Mode is on. This avoids exposing DACH-specific vocabulary to non-DACH orgs.

DACH delta layer — cross-cutting reference

folded from DACH-DELTA-LAYER.md The cross-cutting region overrides (that sub-spec is archived). This layer overrides US / imperial / ITAR / QuickBooks behaviour wherever any section above describes it. The DACH Costing Mode + Zuschlagskalkulation above are the costing half; this is the rest. The KB has no DACH content — validate against the Fechner pilot, not PP. Cyan DACH callouts throughout the spec point here. Regulatory facts as of Jun 2026 — keep rates/dates configurable.

Region / locale profile (DE | AT | CH)

A per-org Region profile drives currency, VAT, formatting, e-invoice format, accounting connector, language — never hardcode "Germany"; CH especially differs.

ConcernDE / ATCH

Currency1.234,56 €CHF 1'234.56

Tax & e-invoicing

CountryStandard VATReducedTerm
Germany19%7%USt / MwSt
Austria20%13% / 10% (verify)USt
Switzerland8.1%2.6% / 3.8% (verify)MWST / TVA
  • Reverse charge (intra-EU B2B): no VAT; invoice notes "Steuerschuldnerschaft des Leistungsempfängers" + both VAT-IDs (§13b UStG); requires the buyer's valid USt-IdNr validated against VIES (store result + timestamp).
  • §14 UStG mandatory fields: supplier+customer name/address, USt-IdNr, date, sequential unique invoice no., qty+description, delivery date, net per VAT rate, VAT rate+amount (or reverse-charge note).
  • E-invoicing (DE, phased): Jan 2025 must receive + archive EN 16931; Jan 2027 mandatory issue for > €800k turnover; Jan 2028 all B2B. Formats: XRechnung (XML; B2G via Peppol + Leitweg-ID), ZUGFeRD ≥ 2.1 (hybrid PDF/A-3 + XML), Peppol BIS 3.0. AT: ebInterface / Peppol via USP. CH: no mandate, QR-bill standard.
  • GoBD (DE): invoices / quotes / orders retained immutably ~10 years with audit trail. Kleinunternehmer (§19): per-org flag suppressing VAT lines.
  • Placement: a Tax/Region service computes VAT at quote/order level (not in Kalk); customer-facing manufacturing invoices are the shop's own (DATEV / e-invoice), separate from Tolera's Paddle SaaS billing.

Materials & engineering standards

  • Materials — DIN EN 10027: primary key Werkstoffnummer (e.g. 1.4301) + name (X5CrNi18-10); AISI/UNS (304) as alias. Al = EN AW-6061 / 3.3211. Material stores werkstoffnummer, en_name, aisi_alias, family, class (seed a DIN↔AISI crosswalk).
  • General tolerances — ISO 2768 (-f/-m/-c) / ISO 22081: default ISO 2768-m when a drawing specifies none (drives DFM + cost; configurable). GD&T → ISO GPS (ISO 1101), not ASME Y14.5. Surface finish Ra/Rz in µm (ISO 1302). Threads metric ISO (M-series). Welding ISO 2553. Sheet thickness mm.

Export control & data privacy (replaces ITAR/CUI)

  • GDPR / DSGVO: AVV/DPA with every sub-processor (Clerk, Mailgun EU, LLM, hosting); EU data residency; data-subject rights; ROPA; retention reconciled with GoBD; Impressum + Datenschutzerklärung on customer-facing surfaces.
  • EU dual-use export control: Reg (EU) 2021/821 + national AWG/AWV + EU control lists — replace the ITAR/CUI flag with export_control (Güterlistenrelevanz / dual-use) on Part / Quote Item / RFQ; optional restricted-party screening (EU sanctions list). Keep the CUI access-restriction/audit behaviour, relabelled + re-based on EU law. Export-control regime = config, not hardcoded.

Integration Manager

Each external integration gets its own Integration Manager panel accessible from Settings → Integrations → click integration banner. The manager provides three things: KB: integration-manager KB: integration-actions

EntityDescription
Integration ActionsA description of a capability of the integration (e.g. "Export Order", "Import Contact"). Defined per integration.
Integration Action LogsA record of each attempt to perform an integration action. Shows: timestamp, status (In Progress / Completed / Failed / …), an action-specific message. The main log view in the manager.
Integration Action RequestsRequests for the integration to perform a specific action. Can be initiated manually by a team member from the "Request Actions" tab (e.g. manually trigger an export).

Header of the manager panel: integration name, author, support contact. "Request Actions" tab allows manual triggering.

Integration Notifications: high-priority failures can be configured to send immediate email alerts. For PP-authored integrations, only export actions (data going out to another system) support notifications; import failures require contacting support.

API key: accessible in the "Connectivity" tab of each integration's settings — admin-only.

Build implications: model Integration (name, author, support_contact), IntegrationAction (integration_id, name, direction: export|import), IntegrationActionLog (action_id, status, message, timestamp), IntegrationActionRequest (action_id, requested_by, requested_at). Surface a per-integration manager drawer in Settings → Integrations. Notification configuration per action (email recipient, on-failure-only for exports).

API contract — REST + Streaming (webhooks)

folded from INTEGRATION-API-CONTRACT.md The two outward-facing APIs that wrap the Managed-Integrations framework above (that sub-spec is archived). Phasing: REST API + outbound webhooks are v1 (DATEV/ERP export, Paddle billing, Mailgun EU ingest need them); the full in-app Integration-Manager UI is post-pilot, but the contract is built once now. KB: integration-development-guide · KB: paperless-parts-streaming-api
APIDirectionUse
REST APIclient → Toleraask about / create / update / delete data (request-response)
Streaming API (webhooks)Tolera → clientreact to events in near-real-time (Tolera POSTs the client) — avoids polling / rate-limits

Event catalog (the bus)

Events are facts (noun.verb_past), driving both webhooks (push) and the Events polling endpoint (pull). v1 (from PP): quote.created, quote.status_changed, quote.sent, order.created, order.status_changed, integration_action.requested, integration.turned_on/off. Tolera additions (gate behind config): rfq.received (Mailgun), quote.approved, invoice.issued / einvoice.exported (XRechnung/ZUGFeRD/DATEV), order.shipped. First read via the Events endpoint marks an event dispatched; filter dispatched=false to poll only new.

Webhooks — delivery & signature

Per webhook: an HTTPS public endpoint + subscribed event types → Tolera generates a signing secret; a delivery log records each POST body + client response. On each event Tolera POSTs; the endpoint must respond 2xx immediately and do work async. Every dispatch carries a Tolera-Signature: t=<unix_ts>,v1=<hex_hmac> header (timestamp folded in to prevent replay):

message = f"{timestamp}.{raw_json_body}"          # exact transmitted bytes
sig     = HMAC-SHA256(key=unhexlify(signing_secret), msg=message).hexdigest()
# constant-time compare with v1; reject on mismatch (and if timestamp too old)
Reliability (Tolera net-new): exponential-backoff retry on non-2xx (N attempts); at-least-once delivery → consumers must be idempotent on event uuid; per-dispatch log retention; manual resend. Sign over the exact raw bytes transmitted (re-serialising JSON breaks the signature).

REST surface (v1)

Base https://api.tolera.eu/v1 (EU residency). Auth = per-integration API token (bearer, Admin-only, org-scoped → RLS tenant-filtered; machine integrations use tokens, not user creds). JSON / snake_case / ISO-8601 UTC; money = integer minor units + explicit currency. Cursor/page pagination; documented rate limit + 429 + Retry-After; error envelope {error:{code,message,details[]}}.

ResourceVerbs (v1)Notes
quoteslist/get/create/update + /{id}/sendnested quote_items → components → component_quantities (read)
orderslist/get/(create)from accepted quote
accounts, contactslist/get/create/updateCRM / DATEV import target
partslist/getlibrary; files/geometry read
eventslist/getpoll alternative to webhooks; filter type, dispatched
integration_actionscreate/update, list/getintegration writes the log (POST→queued, PATCH status)
webhookscreate/update/delete, liststanding config (permissioned, audited)
heartbeatPOST /integration/heartbeathealth indicator
Prohibited via API (server-enforced regardless of token scope): no endpoint may alter sharing/permissions/ACLs, hard-delete data, change security settings, or move funds. Billing state flows from Paddle webhooks into Tolera, never the reverse.
DACH connector set (replaces the US/QuickBooks set). Accounting = DATEV (priority outbound; drives invoice.issued/einvoice.exported); e-invoicing XRechnung / ZUGFeRD 2.1+ / Peppol (DE timeline: receive 2025 / issue >€800k 2027 / all 2028), GoBD-archived; German/EU ERPs (ProAlpha, abas, SAP B1) post-pilot; Mailgun EU RFQ ingest ({slug}@rfq.tolera.eu → Lens); Paddle (EU MoR) billing webhooks in; Autodesk Fusion CAD push post-pilot. All API traffic stays in the EU region.

Acceptance

  • Webhook round-trip: subscribe quote.sent → sending a quote POSTs a signed payload; a tampered body fails verification; endpoint returns 2xx synchronously, processes async.
  • Idempotency: same event uuid twice → one logical effect. Polling parity: Events endpoint returns the same events; dispatched=false returns only new; reading flips the flag.
  • Tenant isolation: a token for org A cannot read org B (RLS); token Admin-only. Safety: no API path mutates ACLs / hard-deletes / moves funds. DACH: money carries currency + minor units; DATEV export valid; e-invoice GoBD-archived; EU-only traffic.

External systems observed

  • ERP — Global Shop Solutions & JobBOSS (Demo A & E): nesting stock carries an ERP code per sheet; the quote ACTIONS menu lists "Relevant Integrations" (e.g. "Open JobBOSS Manager", "Open test Manager") and "Facilitate order". Build an ERP adapter interface (push order, sync item/stock codes); treat each ERP as one implementation behind a generic interface.
  • Export / analytics / API: quote data — including custom-markup cost + markup columns — is available via Excel/CSV/PDF export, analytics, and an open API. Custom categories must flow through to all three.
  • Supplier catalogs (CAD viewer right edge): Online Metals, PEM, MSC, Fastenal — quick-source links; DB Roberts appears as a purchased-component supplier badge.
  • CAD round-trip / source connectors — see the dedicated subsection below.
  • Email — inbound RFQ ingest (forwarding address) + outbound quote send.
Design integrations as pluggable adapters (ERP, supplier pricing, email, CRM, CAD source). Only email ingest + a stub ERP push are in MVP scope; the rest can be link-outs initially.

CAD round-trip / source connectors (pluggable adapters)

All authoring-tool integrations sit behind one CadConnector adapter interface (same pattern as ERP/CRM): authenticate to the user's CAD account/store, browse their projects/folders/vault, pull a file in to quote it, and — where the tool supports it — push a file out to heal / make a design change / prep CAM, then consume the result back. Two hard rules for every CAD connector: KB: autodesk-fusion-integration

  • It is opt-in and a power-user convenience, never required. The native geometry engine already gives every user full feature interrogation + DFM with no CAD seat (see no-CAD-license note); connectors only add value for shops that already own the CAD seat.
  • A connector talks to an external (often cloud) authoring tool, so it is separate from the self-hosted "files never leave" core. Connectors are individually enable-able and must be disable-able for CUI/ITAR/GDPR-restricted accounts.
ConnectorStatusSurface / authCapabilities
Autodesk Fusionv1 — in scope (adapter #1, demoed)Cloud-native — Autodesk 360 hubs/projects/folders on the APS open-data platform; OAuth ("Settings → connect → sign in with Fusion")Continuous two-way connectivity (not one-shot): browse files; access granular design data (tolerances, drawing callouts, part numbers, metadata); push changes (CAM/machining-strategy + design, heal bad files) with outputs automatically consumed back. Fusion integration + partnership demos
SOLIDWORKSPlanned (full round-trip, parity with Fusion)Desktop-rooted, not a single cloud — so the connector targets whichever surface the customer has: a local API/add-in (SOLIDWORKS API; requires the seat installed), SOLIDWORKS PDM vault (on-prem), or 3DEXPERIENCE / 3DSpace (cloud, OAuth+REST). Pick per-customer at setup.Same intent as Fusion — browse vault/projects, pull native .SLDPRT/.SLDASM, push out to heal/redesign/CAM, import results back. Note: native .SLDPRT import for quoting is already handled engine-side by Spatial (see engine); this connector adds the live authoring round-trip on top.
OnshapePlanned next (cleanest cloud fit)Cloud-native — OAuth + REST, browse Documents; maps onto the Fusion pattern almost exactlyBrowse documents, pull a part to quote, round-trip changes back. Lowest-friction cloud connector after Fusion.
Roadmap: Fusion is in v1; Onshape (clean cloud OAuth) and SOLIDWORKS (PDM/3DEXPERIENCE/desktop, enterprise, bespoke per customer) are fast-follows; Creo/NX (via PDM) as demand appears. Build the CadConnector interface now; ship adapters incrementally.
Why Fusion is cheap to ship in v1. Autodesk Platform Services (APS, formerly Forge) has a Free tier and full public API docs. The connector only needs the free-tier APIs: OAuth (identity), Data Management API (browse hubs/projects/folders + pull files), and Webhooks API (real-time change notifications) — "many APIs are completely free." The token-metered APIs (Flex tokens) — Model Derivative (translation) and Design Automation (cloud compute) — are not needed, because Bid Factory's own self-hosted engine does translation/interrogation. Caveat (unchanged): APS is cloud-only, no self-hosted option, so the connector is opt-in and must be disable-able for CUI/ITAR/GDPR accounts. API docs: aps.autodesk.com/developer/documentation. APS pricing

CRM integration (decided — replaces a native CRM module)

Bid Factory is the system of record for all of its data — Accounts, Contacts, Parts, Quotes, line items, Pricing — and integrates two-way with the customer's existing CRM via a pluggable CRM adapter (same pattern as the ERP adapter). It is not a marketing CRM and does not replace theirs, but it does own native, lightweight Account/Contact records (the sales-intel records shown in the demos). The adapter pulls accounts/contacts in and pushes quotes/pricing/status/win-loss (and account/contact changes) out. So the earlier "build a full Contacts/CRM module" gap shrinks to: native account/contact CRUD (lightweight) + a two-way sync adapter. KB: accounts-and-contacts KB: exporting-quotes-orders-and-accounts-contacts

DecisionValue
Target CRMsHubSpot, Salesforce, SAP, and a generic adapter for other common German-manufacturing CRMs. One CrmAdapter interface, one implementation per CRM.
DirectionTwo-way sync.
Objects syncedAccounts/Contacts, Quotes, line items, pricing, quote status, win/loss — the full quote (not a summary).
TriggerOn quote send, via real-time webhooks (BF→CRM on send/status change; CRM→BF on account/contact change).
System of recordBid Factory is SoR for ALL objects (Accounts, Contacts, Parts, Quotes, line items, Pricing, status). The CRM is a sync peer; on conflict, Bid Factory wins. Two-way: pull accounts/contacts in, push quotes/pricing/status/win-loss + account/contact changes out.
Build implications: (1) a CrmAdapter interface — upsertAccount/Contact, pushQuote(fullQuote), updateQuoteStatus/winLoss, plus inbound onAccountContactChanged webhook handlers. (2) Account/Contact are full native records (BF is SoR) and also carry external_crm_id, crm_source, last_synced_at; estimators can create one in Bid Factory (pushes to the CRM) or pick a synced one. (3) Map BF quote → the CRM's quote/opportunity object (line items + pricing + status + win/loss) and push on send. (4) Idempotent, retried, logged webhooks; surface a sync status + error log. (5) OAuth per CRM. Email-ingest sender→account matching should resolve against the synced CRM accounts.

HubSpot integration in practice — Zapier middleware pattern (Ep 33)

Paperless Parts customers with HubSpot run two tiers of integration complexity in practice:

TierWhat they doHow
SimpleOne-way sync: HubSpot companies → PP accounts (or PP accounts → HubSpot companies). Contacts follow companies. No deal-level sync.Direct API or Zapier (trigger: HubSpot company created/updated → action: PP account upserted).
AdvancedLink HubSpot Deals to PP Quotes. PP writes the quoted dollar amount back to the deal so the HubSpot pipeline stays live. Used for marketing campaign ROI: "the trade-show campaign generated $X in quoted work".Requires direct API (PP quote finalized → webhook → update HubSpot deal amount). Zapier can handle simple property writes; complex conditional logic is better served by direct API or n8n.

PP recommends HubSpot CRM Starter (paid but low-cost) to customers who don't already have a CRM, because the free tier lacks the API access needed for two-way sync. Contacts and companies can be bulk-imported from an ERP or spreadsheet into HubSpot first, then synced to PP on day one.

Build implication: The CrmAdapter should support an optional Zapier webhook endpoint (/integrations/crm/zapier/inbound) as a lower-friction entry point for shops that don't want to configure OAuth. Expose a deal/opportunity ID field on Quote (nullable crm_opportunity_id) and push quoted amount back to the deal on quote-finalize. The revenue-analytics angle (quoted pipeline by campaign/source) requires Quote.lead_source or Account.crm_lead_source field populated from the CRM sync.
Localization (decided): EUR only — no multi-currency (single base currency; the demo USD figures are sample data to convert). Multi-language in scope — Phase 1: English + German (en, de); later: Italian, Spanish, Danish, Dutch, Austrian. Build full UI i18n (externalized strings + locale-aware number/date formats) from day one, even though only en/de ship first. Metric default already supported via Unit Preferences. GDPR — resolved in v2.15: compliance-baseline package ships in v1 — EU-only data residency (Hetzner), DPA template + subprocessor list, per-org data export (JSON/CSV), contact/personal-data erasure workflow, audit log, configurable retention. See Resolved Decisions.

Operation Library

All decisions confirmed via grill session 2026-06-14. DIN 8580 taxonomy cross-referenced against IHK vocational curricula, Techpilot (~280 DACH practitioner categories), DACH ERP systems (Infor LN, Kieselstein, proAlpha), and CPQ competitors (TICC, HSi, Spanflug, Costimator). KB: operations KB: setting-up-operations KB: managing-materials KB: adding-a-supplier-material KB: online-metals-material-calculator

Overview

The operation library is the org-wide catalogue of work steps an estimator can assign to a part. Each operation has a name, a calculation mode, and a rate configuration. The library ships with 54 pre-installed operations covering the full range of DACH fabrication shop profiles (CNC machining, sheet metal, welding/joining, surface finishing, additive, assembly, logistics/QC). Users can add custom operations at any time — either from the library settings screen or inline while building a quote.

Auto-save: when an estimator adds a custom operation inside a part that does not yet exist in the library, it is automatically saved to the org's global operation library for reuse on future quotes. The library grows from real quoting activity without requiring an admin trip to settings.

Operation data model

FieldTypeNotes
idUUIDStable identifier
namestringDisplayed name (e.g. "Fräsen/Drehen"). Editable.
calculation_modeENUMmachine_plus_operator | labour_only | outside_process. Controls which rate fields appear and how cost is computed.
run_rate€/hrMachine hourly rate (MSS) for machine_plus_operator; labour rate for labour_only.
labour_rate€/hrOperator rate, only for machine_plus_operator mode. Applies to Nebenzeit.
setup_cost€ flatDefault mode: flat setup cost charged once per lot. Mutually exclusive with setup_time_mins (Advanced toggle).
setup_time_minsminutesAdvanced toggle: setup time in minutes × setup rate. Amortised over Losgröße.
surcharge_pct%Optional surcharge applied on top of computed operation cost. Handles sensitive-material risk premium and rework uplift. Default 0.
is_pre_installedboolTrue for the 54 seed operations. Pre-installed ops can be renamed or hidden but not deleted.
sort_orderintDisplay order in library list.
created_attimestampFor auto-saved ops: records when first used.

Calculation mode — cost formula per mode

ModeFormulaTypical ops
machine_plus_operatorsetup_cost/Losgröße + (Hauptzeit_min/60 × run_rate) + (Nebenzeit_min/60 × labour_rate)Fräsen, Laserschneiden, Kanten, EDM, Strahlen, Lackieren
labour_onlysetup_cost/Losgröße + (Arbeitszeit_min/60 × run_rate)Schweißen, Montage, Polieren, Entgraten, Verpacken
outside_processRoutes to Vendor RFQ portal — no internal rateAnodisieren, Galvanisieren, Wärmebehandlung
All time inputs are in minutes throughout the product. Never hours. The UI labels fields as "minutes" explicitly.
Rüstzeit (setup time) is a field within every operation — not a standalone operation type. There is no pre-installed "Rüst" operation. Setup cost is either a flat € amount or a time-based calculation (Advanced toggle per operation). This eliminates the double-counting risk present when setup is a separate line item.

Material line — yield factor

Each material line item carries a yield_factor field (default 1.0, range 0.01–1.0). When set below 1.0, the system automatically grosses up the material quantity and cost to account for expected scrap:

material_quantity_ordered = required_qty / yield_factor
material_cost = material_quantity_ordered × unit_material_price

Example: polished stainless, yield_factor 0.90 → 10% extra material ordered and priced in automatically. The estimator sets this field; it is visible on the material line and in the cost breakdown.

Operation surcharge

Every operation (pre-installed and custom) has an optional surcharge_pct field. When non-zero, the computed operation cost is multiplied by (1 + surcharge_pct/100) before rolling into Inside Processing totals. Use cases: sensitive material handling premium, rework uplift, complexity risk on tight-tolerance ops. The surcharge is visible as a separate sub-row on the operation cost detail for transparency. Default 0 on all pre-installed operations.

UI behaviours

BehaviourSpec
Searchable typeahead pickerWhen adding an operation to a part, the picker is a combobox: user types to filter by name (case-insensitive, substring match), or scrolls the full list. Essential at 54+ operations.
Add operationADD OPERATION button on the operations panel of a part. Opens the typeahead picker. Estimator can type a new name and create on the fly (auto-saves to library).
Duplicate operationDUPLICATE button (or ⋮ → Duplicate) on any operation row. Creates a copy with the same mode and rates. Used when a shop needs two instances of the same process at different rates (e.g. Schleifen Maschine A / Schleifen Maschine B).
Remove operationREMOVE (×) button on any operation row within a part. Removes from this quote-item only; does not delete from the library.
ReorderDrag handle on each operation row. Order is preserved in the quote and on the PDF.
Library managementSettings → Operations: full list, edit name/mode/rates, hide pre-installed ops, delete custom ops. Bulk rate update ("Apply rate to all") available here — see Onboarding Quick Setup.

Pre-installed operation library (54 operations)

Derived from: the pilot customer's live Excel calculation list, DIN 8580 taxonomy, IHK vocational trade curricula (Zerspanungsmechaniker, Konstruktionsmechaniker, Feinwerkmechaniker, Verfahrensmechaniker Beschichtungstechnik), Techpilot DACH practitioner categories, and DACH CPQ tools (TICC, HSi, Spanflug). Outside-process operations (Anodisieren, Galvanisieren, Wärmebehandlung) are handled by the Vendor RFQ portal — not pre-installed here.

Calculation modes are defaults; any can be changed per-org. Rates are not pre-seeded — every shop configures their own (see Quick Setup).

Machine + Operator (31 operations)

These operations have a machine running during the process. Cost = MSS (Maschinenstundensatz) + operator Nebenzeit. Both a machine rate and a labour rate are configurable.

#NameDIN 8580 groupNotes
1Fräsen/Drehen3.2 SpanenCombined op for shops where milling and turning share one rate. Pilot customer default.
2Fräsen3.2 SpanenStandalone milling — for shops with dedicated milling centres.
3Drehen3.2 SpanenStandalone turning — for dedicated Dreher shops.
4Bohren3.2 SpanenDrilling, includes countersinking/reaming unless separated.
5Senken3.2 SpanenCountersinking / counterboring on dedicated machine.
6Schleifen 13.3 Spanen undef.Grinding — first machine/grade. Duplicate for second rate.
7Schleifen 23.3 Spanen undef.Grinding — second machine/grade (e.g. surface vs cylindrical).
8Honen3.3 Spanen undef.Honing — bore finishing to tight tolerances.
9Zerspanen3.2/3.3 SpanenGeneric machining catch-all for shops that don't separate by process type.
10Sägen/Schneiden3.1 ZerteilenBand saw, cold saw, circular saw. Material prep.

24Plasmaschneiden3.4 AbtragenPlasma cutting — typically for thicker steel than laser.

27Erodieren Senk3.4 AbtragenSinker EDM. For toolmakers and precision shops.
28Drahterodieren3.4 AbtragenWire EDM. Pilot customer had it on their list (Abziehen context).
29Punktschweißen4.6 SchweißenResistance spot welding machine.
30Roboterschweißen4.6 SchweißenRobotic welding cell. Machine + operator (programmer/setter).
31Lackieren5.1 BeschichtenIn-house spray painting / KTL. Spray booth has MSS cost (energy, maintenance).
32Pulverbeschichten5.4 BeschichtenIn-house powder coating line + oven.

Labour Only (23 operations)

These operations are performed by an operator without a dedicated machine driving the cost. A single labour rate (€/hr) applies.

#NameDIN 8580 groupNotes
33Schweißen4.6 FügenGeneric manual welding — for shops that don't split by process.
34MAG/MIG Schweißen4.6 FügenMAG/MIG. Separate rate from WIG (different consumables, speed).
35WIG/TIG Schweißen4.6 FügenWIG/TIG. Slower, higher skill premium, higher rate typical.
36Heften4.6 FügenTack welding. Often quoted separately from full weld on complex fabrications.
37Löten4.7 FügenSoft or hard soldering.
38Kleben4.8 FügenAdhesive bonding.
39Schrauben4.1 ZusammensetzenBolted assembly — manual fastening.
40Nieten4.5 Fügen durch UmformenRiveting.
41Polieren 13.3 Spanen undef.Polishing — first grade/finish. Duplicate for second grade.
42Polieren 23.3 Spanen undef.Polishing — second grade (e.g. mirror finish on stainless).
43Nachpolieren3.3 Spanen undef.Re-polishing after welding or rework. Apply surcharge_pct for sensitive material risk.
44Entgraten3.2/3.3 SpanenManual deburring. Distinct from machine Strahlen or Bürsten.
45Gewinde3.2 SpanenHand tapping or drill-press threading. Distinct from CNC Gewindefräsen.
46Anpassen4.1 ZusammensetzenFitting and adjusting — hand filing, scraping to achieve fit.
47Abziehen3.3 Spanen undef.Manual draw-filing / pull-through finishing. Common on polished stainless.
48Montage4.1 ZusammensetzenSub-assembly or component assembly. Use surcharge_pct for complex/sensitive assemblies.
49Zusammenbauen4.1 ZusammensetzenFinal assembly of complete product. Distinct from sub-assembly Montage.
50Nacharbeiten—Rework. Normal labour-only op; apply surcharge_pct to signal cost premium. Not a special system type — no double-counting risk.
51Kommissionieren—Kitting / order picking. Legitimate billable labour in Lohnfertigung.
52Verpacken/Kontrollieren—Packing and QC inspection. Real labour required to fulfil the order.
53Sonstiges—Miscellaneous catch-all. Always pre-installed; every shop needs one.
54Reserve—Placeholder. Org can rename for any purpose.

Outside process (Vendor RFQ portal — not in operation library)

These operations route to the Vendor RFQ portal rather than being costed internally. They are not pre-installed as operations because they have no internal machine or labour rate:

Anodisieren/Eloxieren · Galvanisieren (Verzinken, Vernickeln, Verchromen) · Pulverbeschichten (outsourced) · Lackieren (outsourced) · Brünieren · Chromatieren/Passivieren · Thermisches Spritzen · PVD/CVD · Härten · Einsatzhärten · Vergüten · Nitrieren/Carbonitrieren · Glühen · Laserhärten

When an estimator adds an outside-process step to a part, they use the outside_process calculation mode — which triggers the Vendor RFQ flow rather than internal costing. See Vendor RFQ Portal.

Calibration over time

Default calculation modes are based on the pilot customer's operation list and DACH industry norms. Beta user feedback tunes them — e.g. a shop that does Bürsten manually (not by machine) switches that op to labour-only. No system migration required; it is a per-org setting.

Org Onboarding & First-Run Experience

Covers: seed script, Quick Setup checklist, Advanced Setup checklist, non-admin first login, empty states, demo data, and the operation rates quick-start banner. All decisions confirmed via grill session 2026-06-14. KB: setting-up-your-organization

Org provisioning — seed script

New orgs are provisioned via a CLI seed script for the pilot. The script is a thin wrapper around OrgService.create_org() — the same service that a future self-serve signup endpoint will call. No special-casing between pilot provisioning and self-serve; the service is the source of truth from day one.

Seed script interface

python -m scripts.seed_org \
  --org-name "Müller Maschinenbau" \
  --admin-email jan@mueller-maschinenbau.de \
  --admin-name "Jan Müller" \
  --locale de \
  --plan pilot

On execution: creates the Org row, calls Clerk.organizations.createMembership() for the admin user (Clerk fires the invite email directly — no magic links to copy-paste), seeds demo data (see below), and prints the org slug to stdout.

Self-serve path: when self-serve signup is built, POST /auth/signup calls OrgService.create_org() with the same parameters. The seed script becomes a thin CLI caller of the same service — no migration required.

Seed script demo data

Every new org is seeded with the following, all flagged is_demo = true:

EntityContent
Demo Account"Müller Maschinenbau GmbH" — a sample customer account with standard checkout methods (PO) and a standard lead time
Demo Contact"Thomas Müller, thomas@mueller-maschinenbau.de" — linked to the demo account
Demo QuoteOne realistic quote with 2–3 line items, pre-priced, in DRAFT state. Labelled with a DEMO badge everywhere it appears in the UI.
Placeholder ProcessesStandard DACH manufacturing template: CNC Turning, CNC Milling, Sheet Metal Laser Cut, Sheet Metal Bending, Anodizing. All rates seeded at €0.00 — the Quick Setup checklist step 3 drives the user to configure them.
Demo data lifecycle: auto-cleared when the first real RFQ is ingested (email ingest or Smart RFQ form submission). The trigger is RFQIngestService.on_first_real_rfq(org_id) — deletes all is_demo = true records atomically. Additionally, a "Clear demo data" button appears on the Quotes list page (visible only while demo data exists) for manual clearing. No demo data persists silently into production use.

Quick Setup checklist (admin first login)

The admin lands in the app after accepting the Clerk invite. A sidebar footer progress indicator shows "Setup 1 of 6 complete" with a small progress ring next to the user avatar. Clicking it opens the checklist as a slide-over panel from the right. The checklist does not block access to the app.

Once all 6 steps are complete (or the user explicitly dismisses), a Sonner toast fires: "Setup complete — you're ready to quote." The progress indicator disappears from the footer entirely.

#StepDeep linkCompletion trigger
1Connect your email — link Gmail or Outlook to send quotes from your own addressSettings → Email ConnectivityUserEmailConnection row created for the admin user
2Review company profile — logo, facility address, VAT number, date format, unit preferencesSettings → Company SettingsAdmin saves any change to Company Settings
3Set your operation rates — configure €/hr and setup costs for each operationConfigure → Operations (with quick-start banner)Any operation rate saved with a value > €0.00
4Invite your team — add estimators, salespeople, or other adminsSettings → TeamAt least one invite sent via Clerk.organizations.createMembership()
5Forward or receive your first RFQ — forward a customer email to your RFQ ingest address, or submit via Smart RFQQuotes → New (with ingest address shown)First Quote row created with source = email_ingest OR smart_rfq and is_demo = false
6Send your first quote — review the draft quote and send it to the customer—First quote with status = sent and is_demo = false
Steps are skippable individually — a "Skip for now" link on each step. Skipped steps do not block checklist completion but are flagged with a ⚠ in the slide-over until actioned. Step 3 (operation rates) is the only step that triggers a downstream soft warning if skipped — see § Missing rates warning below.

Operation rates quick-start banner

When the admin clicks Step 3 in the checklist, they are deep-linked to Configure → Operations. A dismissible banner appears at the top of that page until at least one rate is saved:

Quick start: apply a single rate to all operations
[€ ______ /hr] [APPLY TO ALL]
Or configure each operation individually below.

APPLY TO ALL sets the run rate on every process in the org's catalog in one write. The banner collapses once any rate is saved. The full per-operation table below the banner supports detailed configuration.

Operation rate model (Model C)

FieldDefault modeAdvanced mode (toggle per operation)
Run rate€/hr (required)Same
Setup costFlat € field (e.g. €150 setup charge)Setup time (minutes) × setup rate (€/hr) — auto-calculates flat equivalent
Setup timeHidden in default modeMinutes (not hours — all time inputs in this product are in minutes)
Time inputs: all time values across the entire product (setup time, operation cycle time, lead times where entered as duration) are in minutes, not hours. Display can convert to hours for readability but the stored unit is always minutes.

Missing operation rates — soft warning

If a user opens or creates a quote while any operation in the quote has a run rate of €0.00, a warning banner appears at the top of the Quote Detail page:

Operation rates not set — costs will show as €0.00. Configure rates →

The banner is not dismissible per-session — it reappears on every quote until rates are set. It is never a hard block; users can continue building and sending quotes. The warning also appears inline on any line item operation row where the rate is €0.00, as a subtle amber cell highlight.

Shown below the Quick Setup checklist in the slide-over panel. Starts collapsed with a disclosure arrow labelled "Advanced Setup — unlock the full platform (optional)". Expands on click. Items are organized in four groups.

Advanced Setup items are always optional and always skippable. There is no completion requirement. The slide-over panel shows a count ("3 of 16 complete") but no celebration moment — advanced setup is ongoing, not a milestone. New items may be added to this list as features are built.

Non-admin first login (estimators & salespeople)

When a non-admin user accepts their Clerk invite and lands in the app, they see a welcome modal (not a checklist). The modal contains:

  • Org name and their assigned role
  • One CTA: "Connect your email" — estimators and salespeople send customer emails directly from their own address (technical questions, quote sends). Deep-links to Settings → User Profile → Email Connectivity.
  • A "Get started" dismiss button

After dismissal: straight into the app. No sidebar footer progress indicator for non-admins. A persistent nudge banner appears on the user's profile page until they connect their email: "Connect your email to send quotes and customer messages from your own address."

Empty states

ScreenEmpty state treatment
Quotes listPre-seeded with one demo quote (DEMO badge). A "Clear demo data" button appears in the toolbar. Auto-cleared on first real RFQ ingest.
ContactsIllustrated empty state: icon + "No contacts yet" + CTA: "Import from ERP" or "Add contact"
Vendor RFQ queueIllustrated: "No vendor RFQs yet — mark a line item as outside service to create one"
Configure → OperationsIllustrated: "No operations configured" + CTA: "Add operation" — but seed script pre-populates placeholder processes, so this state should rarely appear
All other screensIllustrated empty state: relevant icon, one-line description of the screen's purpose, single primary CTA
Illustrated empty states: use a simple SVG illustration (not stock photography), a single sentence describing what the screen does, and one primary CTA button. No walls of text, no feature lists. The goal is one action, not a tutorial.

Authentication & SSO (decided)

An email-first login with single sign-on, modeled on the attached pattern (Shopify-style: enter email → continue, plus passkey + identity-provider buttons), localized per the language setting. SS user-provided SSO mockup (de "Einloggen")

MethodNotes
Email — "Continue with email"Email-first entry: password-primary, magic-link fallback. The reference uses a single "Continue with email" step.
Passkey (WebAuthn / FIDO2)"Sign in with a passkey" — device biometrics / security key; passwordless.
Identity providers (social/OIDC)Reference shows Google, Apple, Facebook, WhatsApp. For B2B manufacturing, recommend Google + Apple + Microsoft (Entra/365) and treat Facebook/WhatsApp as optional/consumer — confirm the v1 set.
Enterprise SSO (SAML 2.0 / OIDC)Org-level SSO (Microsoft Entra ID / Azure AD, Okta, Google Workspace) — important for larger shops & the German market; ties to multi-tenancy. Recommended for v1+ even if not in the consumer mockup.
2FAAlready in Settings → User Profile (Two-Factor Authentication) + "Require 2FA for recipients of external share" (Company → Security). Enforceable per-org.
Build implications: use an auth provider/IdP layer (e.g. Auth0/Clerk/WorkOS or self-hosted Keycloak) behind an AuthIdentity model — one User ↔ many identities (email, passkey, oauth:google/apple/microsoft, saml:<org>). Org-level SSO config (SAML/OIDC metadata, allowed domains, JIT provisioning, default role) lives in admin Settings. Localize the login UI (en/de first). Session management + the existing Login Activity page cover device/session visibility. Authorization (who can do what) is separate and still open — see the roles in § Personas and the permissions gap in open questions.

Admins invite team members by email; Clerk handles the invite link and account creation flow. Role assignment happens at invite time and is editable after the fact.

RolesBadge pills per role (Admin, Estimator, Salesperson, Viewer)
Last ActiveTimestamp of last authenticated session; "Never" for pending
StatusACTIVE / PENDING / DISABLED
ActionsEdit roles · Resend invite (pending only) · Disable (soft-remove)

Toolbar: search by name/email; filter by role; INVITE USER button (top-right).

Invite User Modal

FieldNotes
Email *Free-entry; validates format; rejects already-active org members
Roles *Multi-select: Admin, Estimator, Salesperson, Viewer. At least one required.
Personal messageOptional free text appended to the Clerk invite email

On submit: Clerk creates an org invite → invite link emailed to recipient → row appears in Users List with status PENDING. Pending invites can be Resent (re-fires Clerk invite email) or Cancelled (revokes the token). Invites expire after 7 days.

Remove / Disable User

Soft-disable only — no hard delete. Disabling a user: revokes active sessions via Clerk, sets user_org_roles.status = disabled, removes them from all assignee pickers, preserves all historical records (quotes, line items, audit log). Re-enabling restores access without re-invite.

Build implications: Clerk owns the invite lifecycle (organizationMembership API). Mirror into BF's user_org_roles table: user_id, org_id, roles TEXT[], status ENUM(active|pending|disabled), invited_at, last_active_at. Sync via Clerk webhooks: organizationMembership.created/updated/deleted. Role checks in FastAPI read from user_org_roles (cached in JWT claims). Gate INVITE USER and Edit Roles behind role = Admin. Update last_active_at on each authenticated request, debounced to 1 write per 5 min per user.

Email Templates

Per-type templates — Quote-send, Order-shipment, Order-refund — each can be flagged DEFAULT. Rich-text editor with merge fields: %%QUOTE_NUMBER%%, %%QUOTE_LINK%%, %%RFQ_NUMBER%%, %%PART_NUMBERS%%, %%CUSTOMER_FIRST_NAME%%/LAST_NAME, %%ESTIMATOR_*%%, %%SALESPERSON_*%%, %%FACILITY_NAME%%/PHONE. List columns: Template name · Last edited by · Last edited. SS DemoH 08

The send-quote composer ("Who will get this quote?") has To / CC / BCC recipients (each removable, recipient-type dropdown, free-entry), a "Select email template" picker, editable Subject + rich-text body (merge-filled, includes the secure digital-quote link), and an "Include quote PDF in email" option. PREVIEW / SEND QUOTE. SS DemoK 12

Quote Display Settings (buyer-facing) + Quote PDF

Controls what appears on the digital quote and PDF (digital changes apply immediately; PDF changes apply to quotes sent after saving). SS DemoH 09

  • Quote fields (checkboxes): Quote Number, RFQ Number, Facility Phone, Facility Website, Link to Digital Quote.
  • Total/subtotal display (radio): Price Range (min–max) · Maximum Price · Don't display total/subtotal.
  • Preparer & contact (radio): Salesperson · Estimator · Both.
  • Quote notes placement (radio): Above / Below line items.
  • Line-item fields (checkboxes): Part File Name, Part Thumbnail, 3D Part Preview (digital only), Part Dimensions, Process, …
Buyer-facing digital quote (preview/share): facility branding + address, "Requested By", Quote Details (number, payment terms, prepared by, contact), a status badge (e.g. QUOTE EXPIRED), SHARE, "My Order N of M" + CHECK OUT + running SUBTOTAL. Per line: headline "AVAILABLE FROM $X/ea – $Y/ea" range, View in 3D, MANUFACTURER'S NOTES, optional Additional Charges (e.g. NRE, Certificate of Conformance — buyer-selectable), and a selectable pricing grid (QTY / LEAD TIME / UNIT PRICE / TOTAL PRICE with radios) + finish. The customer selects a qty×lead-time tier and checks out online (ties to Order).

Controls the customer-facing digital quote and email output. Eight sub-sections:

SectionKey fields
Terms & ConditionsCustom T&C text displayed on digital quote; option to require customer acceptance before checkout
Manufacturer's NotesPersistent notes shown on every digital quote (facility-level); can contain HTML-like formatting

Requotes & Checkout Settings

Requotes: "Allow buyers to request requotes directly from the Digital Quote" — when off, the buyer's request-requote button disappears on expired quotes (they email the team instead). Checkout Settings: "Allow Local Pickup" and "Send Order Confirmation Emails" toggles. SS PL#37 18

Discrepancy to note (expiry): the reference product blocks acceptance on an expired digital quote (qty rows greyed; only request-requote). Your decided behavior is soft expiry — still acceptable. Build to the decision (acceptance stays enabled on Expired); this is an intentional divergence from the reference.

Company Unit Preferences & Date Formatting

Unit Preferences: Imperial / Metric default (applies everywhere except analytics & operations). Date Formatting: MM-DD-YYYY / DD-MM-YYYY / YYYY-MM-DD (same scope note). Security: "Require 2FA for recipients of external share". Dimension/nesting inputs support inline unit conversion — type "1 meter" or "1 inch" and it converts on blur — plus a Swap units button. SS DemoH 10

Email Notification Settings & CUI Audit

Per-email-address notification matrix: Quote Send BCC, Invoice BCC, SmartRFQ Received, Email-Fwd RFQ Received, Order Confirmation, Requote Request. CUI Audit: download CSV (compliance log).

Smart RFQ Form (Settings → Quote Creation → Smart RFQ Settings)

An embeddable iframe widget for the shop's public website. When a prospective customer submits it, the system auto-drafts a quote in Bid Factory. Key features:

  • Embeddable via <iframe> snippet — no customer account needed
  • Auto-drafts a quote on submission, attaches uploaded files, creates/matches a Contact from the submission email
  • UTM parameter tracking — captures campaign/source/medium from the URL when the form was loaded, stored on the resulting quote
  • Per-process availability: each Process template has an "Available in Smart RFQ Form" toggle (Configure → Processes → General) to limit what processes the customer-facing form offers
  • RFQ Notification Sender (Company Settings → Email Notification Settings): the email address that confirmation emails appear to come from (must be verified)
  • RFQ Received Recipient: the team member who gets notified each time an RFQ is submitted
Vendor RFQs [Beta] — clarification: "Vendor RFQs" in the reference product's sidebar nav is not a vendor outsourcing portal — it is real-time material pricing data from Online Metals + TK (Thyssen Krupp or similar). It surfaces current material prices from these suppliers for use during estimating. In BF, this should be modeled as a MaterialPricingFeed / supplier pricing integration (not VendorRFQ — that entity name is already used by the new Vendor RFQ Portal for outside-process outsourcing). This material pricing integration maps to the Würth adapter in § Integration Adapters.

Company Settings — detailed fields

Beyond what is already documented in the Settings tree, the key configurable areas are: KB: company-settings KB: creating-email-templates KB: email-notifications

SettingDescription
Company NameUpdates company name shown in top-right nav
Facility InformationOne or more facilities — each has: logo file (shown on digital quotes and PDFs), facility name, address. Multi-facility shops can assign a default and select per-quote which facility the quote comes from. "Preview" shows how it looks on the digital quote.
