Each is an email address field.

Facilitate Order Updates (opt-in)

A 2025 opt-in (auto-enabled for all accounts Jan 20 2026) that powers the upgraded order flow — see Order Build: select-all, Ships-On date, search, per-line discounts/charges, and order editing before shipment (PO number, shipping address, add/remove items) with order history and optional buyer notification. Editing applies to non-credit-card orders without shipments; most ERP integrations receive data at order creation.

AI & Automation Settings (Settings → AI & Automation)

A dedicated settings group giving org admins full control over AI features. All toggles are Admin-only. Changes take effect immediately for all users in the org — no restart required.

Master toggle

SettingDefaultEffect when off
Enable AI featuresOnDisables all AI features org-wide: Lens extraction falls back to manual entry, all AI suggestion chips + triage cards + coaching flags are hidden, the In-App Assistant is unavailable, MCP tools return 503 AI features disabled. The toggle state is shown as a banner on the Settings page when off ("AI features are currently disabled for your organisation").
When the master toggle is off, the individual toggles below remain saved but have no effect — they are preserved so enabling the master restores the previous per-feature configuration without needing to re-configure.

Feature toggles (visible only when master toggle is on)

Feature toggleDefaultWhen to turn off
Lens AI extractionOnCUI / ITAR / export-controlled shops that must ensure no drawing content leaves their environment. When off, all extraction fields are manual-entry only. Note: CUI/ITAR-flagged files are automatically skipped regardless of this toggle — this toggle affects standard files.
RFQ Triage BriefOnShops that prefer a minimal new-quote notification and will open the quote themselves to assess it.
Agentic Quote AssemblyOnShops where estimators always prefer to start fresh rather than import a previous router, or where imported-data liability is a concern.
Estimation CoachingOffDefault off — opt-in only. Recommended for shops with junior estimators. Experienced single-estimator shops will find the coaching chips unnecessary noise.
Pre-send Quote ReviewOnHighly experienced estimating teams who find the checklist redundant. Note: hard send preconditions (contact assigned, ≥1 priced line item) are enforced regardless of this toggle.
In-App AssistantOnOrgs that prefer not to surface the chat panel — e.g. where the AI assistant UX is not yet trusted by the team, or where the org wants to roll out AI features incrementally.
MCP Server (external agent access)OnCUI/ITAR-sensitive shops that want to ensure no external agent can read quote data, even via authenticated API calls. Disabling this returns 503 on all /v1/mcp/ routes for the org.
Customer Intelligence BriefOnOrgs that prefer the send-quote composer to be distraction-free. When off, the "About [Account Name]" card is never shown; estimators can still view account history manually from the Account page. Individual estimators can also dismiss the card per-session without touching this toggle.
Requote DiffOnOrgs that always want estimators to review a part fresh with no prior-quote influence. When off, the diff banner never appears even when a Part Library match exists; the "Import router from Rev A" shortcut is also suppressed. Estimators can still dismiss the diff per-use without touching this toggle.
Rule Auto-SuggestionOnOrgs with a mature, locked rule library where new rule suggestions from AI would be noise or confusing for junior estimators.
Margin CoachOnOrgs that prefer estimators set margin without any historical reference visible on-screen — e.g. where margin strategy is intentionally kept separate from estimating. The data layer continues to accumulate win/loss data regardless of this toggle; turning the toggle back on will immediately surface benchmarks once the data threshold is met.

Data & privacy

SettingDefaultDetail
Participate in aggregate benchmarkingOnAllows this org's anonymised win/loss + margin data to contribute to cross-org benchmarks used by Margin Coach. Turn off to use Margin Coach in read-only mode (your org's own data only, no cross-org comparison). No individual quote data is shared — aggregates only, minimum cohort size enforced before any benchmark is surfaced.
AI subprocessor disclosure—Read-only info panel: "AI features use the Anthropic Claude API under a zero-data-retention agreement. Anthropic is listed as a subprocessor in your DPA. File content sent to the Claude API is not stored or used for training." Links to the DPA annex and Anthropic's subprocessor page. Not a toggle — always shown for transparency.
Build notes: Store per-org AI settings in an org_ai_settings table (org_id, master_enabled BOOL, wingman_enabled BOOL, triage_brief_enabled BOOL, quote_assembly_enabled BOOL, estimation_coaching_enabled BOOL, presend_review_enabled BOOL, assistant_enabled BOOL, mcp_enabled BOOL, customer_brief_enabled BOOL, requote_diff_enabled BOOL, rule_suggest_enabled BOOL, margin_coach_enabled BOOL, benchmarking_opt_out BOOL). Cache in Redis per org (TTL 5 min — short enough to not delay disablement in sensitive situations). Every AI code path checks the relevant flag before executing; the master flag is checked first. Default row is inserted on org creation with the above defaults. Seed the fixture orgs with AI fully enabled so acceptance scripts exercise all features.

Recommended Tech Stack

Frontend

  • React + TypeScript, Vite.
  • TanStack Query (server state) + Table (data grids / saved views).
  • 3D CAD viewer = three.js + occt-import-js (WASM) in v1 (v2.15 — supersedes the licensed-SDK wording); a licensed viewer SDK (HOOPS Communicator) may replace it together with the Spatial swap-in. PDF.js for 2D drawings.
  • Tailwind (decided v2.15); dark theme matching this spec.

Backend

  • Python 3.12 + FastAPI (decided v2.15) — Pydantic schemas, SQLAlchemy/Alembic, Celery + Redis for async jobs.
  • PostgreSQL (JSONB for overrides/extraction findings), Redis + a job queue for async extraction/geometry/nesting.
  • Object storage (S3) for files; full-text search (Postgres FTS or OpenSearch) over PDF text + notes.
  • Geometry service = a separate, self-hosted worker wrapping licensed CAD toolkits behind a GeometryService interface (decided — see below). Async via the job queue.
  • LLM/vision service for print extraction; rules engine as a deterministic evaluator over findings.

Geometry engine (decided)

v2.15 update — supersedes this subsection: v1 builds on the open-source path (OCCT + three.js + trimesh + ezdxf) behind GeometryService; Core-4 families full, lathe/wire-EDM attributes-only, STEP-only solids. Spatial remains the swap-in target once on-prem/EU licensing is signed. Full detail in Resolved Decisions → Geometry.

v1 ships full automated interrogation with no customer-side CAD license (see no-CAD-license note) across all six process families — CNC milling, lathe/turning, sheet metal, wire EDM, additive, and waterjet/laser flat — i.e. attribute extraction + per-family feature recognition + per-family DFM warnings + sheet-metal flatten/interrogation. Input formats: solid B-rep STEP/STP and native SOLIDWORKS .SLDPRT, STL mesh (additive), and 2D DXF (flat). That scope makes a from-scratch build impractical, so the strategy is Hybrid: license proven, on-prem/self-hostable CAD toolkits and wrap them behind one swappable GeometryService boundary; build the DFM rule set + sheet-metal logic on top.

DecisionChoice
StrategyHybrid — license import + viewer + feature-recognition primitives; build DFM rules + sheet-metal interrogation in-house; everything behind GeometryService so pieces can be swapped/replaced later.
v1 scopeFull auto, no customer CAD license: dims/volume/area/weight + per-family feature recognition & DFM warnings for milling, lathe/turning, sheet metal, wire EDM, additive, waterjet/laser-flat; sheet-metal flatten (thickness/bends incl. radius/length/angle/K-factor/flat area/nesting eligibility). Formats: STEP/STP, .SLDPRT, STL, DXF.
Formats at launchSTEP/STP, DXF, STL, native SLDPRT (the lead toolkit imports 30+ formats, so IGES/Parasolid/CATIA/NX/Creo come largely for free).
3D viewerLicensed self-hostable web CAD-viewer SDK (face-pick / measure / section).
Data residencySelf-hosted / EU-region — files never leave your environment (CUI/ITAR/GDPR). This rules out cloud-only services.
Lead vendorSpatial (Dassault) — verified fit (see note below). Primary alternative: Tech Soft 3D HOOPS. Open-source-first fallback documented below.
Autodesk APS is NOT a fit for the engine. Autodesk Platform Services (APS / "Forge") — its CAD translation + web Viewer — is cloud-only (no on-prem/private cloud): files must upload to Autodesk's cloud and be converted to SVF before viewing. That conflicts with the self-hosted "files never leave" + CUI/ITAR requirement. (The CAD round-trip connectors — Fusion, SOLIDWORKS, Onshape — in § CAD connectors are a different thing: opt-in links to external authoring tools, explicitly not the geometry engine and separate from the self-hosted core.)
Lead vendor — Spatial (Dassault), verified. Spatial's costing-industry page confirms its SDKs cover the full pipeline this engine needs: 3D InterOp reads native SOLIDWORKS (plus CATIA, NX, Creo, Inventor, Solid Edge + STEP/IGES/JT/3MF, 30+ formats) with auto-healing → this resolves the prior .SLDPRT open question; CGM Modeler does feature recognition (holes through/blind/countersunk/counterbored/threaded, pockets, slots, fillets, chamfers, bosses, ribs), pattern recognition (bolt-circle counted as one pattern), and sheet-metal unfold/blank size; geometry queries (area/volume/bbox/mass props) on full-precision B-rep; semantic PMI/GD&T extraction with geometric associativity (feeds tolerance-based pricing + Requirements Review); HOOPS Communicator = browser viewer (no plugin) with section/measure/PMI/color-by-cost-driver. Production-proven for instant quoting (Prototek, Simus, Misumi, Zero Four). Open diligence: confirm on-prem / EU-region / air-gapped licensing — the page emphasizes cloud/server deployment but does not state data residency; this must be nailed down for CUI/ITAR/GDPR. spatial.com/industries/costing
Alternatives. (1) Tech Soft 3D — HOOPS Exchange (import incl. native SOLIDWORKS) + HOOPS Communicator (web viewer) — equivalent commercial stack. (2) Open-source-first fallback to avoid the license line: OpenCASCADE (OCCT) (LGPL-2.1-with-exception, free commercial use) for STEP/IGES import + geometry queries + topology, compiled to WASM (occt-import-js) with three.js as the web viewer; trimesh/numpy-stl for STL/additive; ezdxf (MIT) for 2D DXF. Trade-offs: no production-grade open-source automatic feature recognition (research-stage only — you'd build hole/pocket/bend recognition on OCCT's B-rep yourself), and no reliable native .SLDPRT (Parasolid is proprietary — either license just that one piece, e.g. CAD Exchanger SDK, or require STEP export). In all cases the DFM warning rules + thresholds (material/machine-specific, see § Geometry & DFM) are built in-house on top.

Suggested Build Phases

v2.15: the phase ordering below remains valid, but the authoritative schedule is now the Milestone Plan (M0–M6, solo + Claude Code, pilot in 3–6 months) with per-milestone exit criteria bound to /fixtures.
Foundations. App shell + nav, auth/roles, native Account/Contact CRUD (BF is system of record), Quote + LineItem CRUD, file upload/storage, quotes list + saved-view engine, dashboard skeleton. (CRM two-way sync adapter layers on top — lightweight account/contact module, not a full CRM.)
Quote builder core. Part estimating view, quantity breaks, manual Materials/Operations, the Costing & Pricing engine (categories, layered margin/markup, per-qty), Add-Ons, Lead Times, Expedite, Notes.
Files & viewers. PDF/drawing viewer (View/Annotate/Measure/Redact), 3D CAD viewer with face picking + selection data, TEAM/EXTERNAL chat + annotations + tasks.
Intelligence. Email ingest → auto-quote + Bulk Create, document extraction → "Found in Files", Rules engine + Review Items + Create Rule, Part Library matching.
Geometry & manufacturing. Geometry extraction, sheet-metal interrogation, feature recognition + DFM warnings (all six process families), BOM Builder, Assembly BOM + Bulk Edit, Nesting module. Autodesk Fusion connector (v1) via APS free-tier APIs (OAuth + Data Management browse/pull + Webhooks), behind the CadConnector interface and disable-able per account.
Orders & integrations. Quote→Order build flow, Shipping & Payment, Orders tab, ERP push stub + supplier link-outs, Configure/admin, Analytics stub.

Acceptance Criteria (definition of done)

The build is functionally complete when both demo narratives can be replayed end-to-end:

Demo A — Precision Cut + ERP

  1. Upload a multi-page assembly PDF → BOM Builder parses a hierarchical BOM (Item No., Part #, Rev, Type, files); "CHECK BOM" flags gaps; "47/1000 unique parts" counter works.
  2. Sheet-metal parts show Interrogation Results (thickness, flat area, bounding, bends); eligible parts open the Nesting module.
  3. Nesting across ≥2 line items with quantity-break stock (96×48, ERP code, $/sheet) produces # sheets + allocated material cost per qty.
  4. Costing rolls up Material/Inside/Outside/Purchased; Pricing applies DTMA markup + inside/outside margins → Total/Unit price, Markup%, Margin% per qty.
  5. Bulk Edit Components reassigns process/material/finish across selected components with regeneration warning.
  6. Quote General + 4-stage Workflow tracker reflect state; "Outstanding Work" counts incomplete items.

Demo B — Arch Medial

  1. Forwarded RFQ email auto-creates a draft quote with the .eml as ORIGINAL RFQ + extracted supporting files; dashboard notification fires.
  2. Bulk Create Line Items is pre-filled from the email (7 parts, multi-qty); creating them distributes files to parts.
  3. Review Items auto-populate across parts (Anodize, Deburring, MIL-SPEC, Pack Individually, True Position, Gold Callout, Material Review, AMS-4016) with suggested actions + SET ALL.
  4. Part Library shows match buckets (Exact File/Geometric/Name/Part#/Similar) with historical quote counts.
  5. CAD viewer: open a STEP, recognize milling features + 79-style DFM warnings, highlight features, pick a face → selection data, post a face annotation tagging a teammate.
  6. Create Rule from a GD&T signal (datum count > 2) with an "add operations" action; the rule generates a Review Item.
  7. CNC operations cost per qty (1/5/20); pricing layers (account markup, material/general margins) → unit price + margin.
  8. Add FAI add-on; lead times per qty + expedite; convert to Order via Build Order → Shipping & Payment.

Demo C — Requirements Review

  1. Open a print in the viewer → GD&T & callouts extract and overlay (datums, control frames, holes, countersinks, counterbores, dimensions, tolerances); "Found in files [n]" badge counts them.
  2. Click a callout → popover with plain-language description + source page; "Mark as inaccurate" removes it; "Create rule" opens the dialog pre-seeded with that entity.
  3. Whiteout clears clutter; spotlight locates a single callout while whited-out; multi-sheet page nav works.
  4. Pencil → region → Add Missing Extraction modal adds a dimension and records a training label.
  5. Units auto-detected from the print; in/mm toggle re-renders values.
  6. Part Setup panel click-to-fill populates Part Number/Rev/Description (NUM/REV toggle), Processes, Specifications, Likely Global Material from extractions.
  7. Create Rule: build a signal (path + filter ≤ 0.005in, or multi-keyword text, or Part max-dim ≥ value AND print length ≥ value, or Files has-print=false), stack resolutions (No-quote / Outside processing / Add operation / Set process / Assign estimator), set an assignee; live Preview updates; CREATE RULE enables when valid.
  8. New rule auto-flags this and every subsequent matching part; line-item Review panel shows one-click resolution buttons + Discuss this.
  9. Quote-level summary groups findings by rule with SET ALL bulk resolution; no-quote all matching line items in one click.
  10. Set Machining→Milling→5-axis process; apply "Add operation" resolutions to the router with duplicate prevention + reorder; per-qty (1/50/500) operation pricing computes.

Demo D — BOM Builder

  1. Upload a multi-page assembly PDF; the file ACTIONS menu offers Split PDF / Edit ITAR status / Duplicate / etc.
  2. Split PDF produces N single-page files (progress + success toasts); the original is retained as "(Original)".
  3. Lens stamps purple BOM badges on pages with BOM tables; hover shows "N BOM tables found in file".
  4. Assign the parent file → root line item created; open BOM Builder modal.
  5. Lens pre-populates the root BOM (item no., part #, rev, qty, description) from the table + title block.
  6. Add Files matches split pages to line items by title-block part number ("52 quote files match 10 parts") and attaches them as supporting files; Supporting Files count drops to 0 as they're assigned.
  7. "Child BOM suggestion available" prompts expand Manufactured rows into Subassemblies with nested decimal children; unique-parts counter climbs (10→25→43).
  8. Purchased parts auto-match to the Components Library (gear + green dot).
  9. Drag-reorder rows; undo/redo; draft autosaves.
  10. Check and Publish commits BomNodes, shows success toast, and leaves an "Open in BOM Builder" banner on the line item linking to the source page.

Demo E — Custom Markups & Margins

  1. Configure → Pricing: New Pricing Item modal creates an item with Calculation Type (Markup/Margin), an existing or New Cost Category (name + color).
  2. A custom category (e.g. Difficult Material) computes its cost via a Kalk formula that iterates the BOM (get_components) and sums matching cost (set_custom_cost); it appears as a colored Costing row.
  3. Adding the matching markup applies a % to only that category's cost (e.g. 10% on $539.36, not on all $674.20 material).
  4. Reproduce the six examples: difficult-material, single work center, labor-vs-overhead split, piece-price-vs-tooling split, outside-finishes-only, and complexity-driven (markup % follows a PartLevel operation; overriding L3→L2 changes 30%→20%).
  5. Pricing supports a Discounts layer ("Total excl. Discounts").
  6. Quote ACTIONS → Excel export includes per-custom-category cost + markup columns; data also reaches analytics + API.
  7. Kalk editor: CHECK validates, SAVE CHANGES persists, Versions tracks history.

Demo F — Part Viewer, Collaboration & Sourcing

  1. Parts Library lists Team/Shared/Archived parts; UPLOAD NEW PART accepts native .SLDPRT, STEP, PDF; the standalone viewer opens.
  2. Geometric analysis + manufacturing warnings render; thresholds are material/machine-specific and tunable in Configure → Interrogations.
  3. TEAM: @mention a teammate on a selected feature → they get a link that opens the viewer zoomed to that feature; Assign Task surfaces on the Dashboard.
  4. EXTERNAL: create a vendor channel, BCC multiple vendors, edit subject/body, set permissions (view/download/upload) + file scope + expiry; recipients are isolated from each other.
  5. PDF Edit → redaction tool blacks out regions and saves a stored redacted copy; one file flagged PRIMARY.
  6. Tolera Source: BOM fasteners show availability + per-unit/per-BOM/total across quantity breaks; SEND RFQ opens the distributor-selection modal and composes an email.
  7. Tolera Advisor: share the part + annotation to an MSC specialist chat with a time-boxed access window.

Demo G — Costing Templates (Processes)

  1. "Update Process and Material" modal lists the process catalog; selecting CNC Simple Milling sets the process + material.
  2. Selecting the process auto-generates the operation routing with setup/run times derived from geometry; cells are editable; ADD OPERATION works.
  3. Configure → Processes → a process: General (lead time, yield, unit-price precision 2/3/4 decimals, Smart RFQ toggle, default purchased-component toggle) saves.
  4. Edit Operations modal: search + add ops, set Assembly / Root Component Only flags, drag-reorder, remove; Save persists the ordered router.
  5. Default Finishes / Add-ons / Pricing Items (markups) / Discounts attach to the template and appear whenever it's used.
  6. Custom Operation Generation formula conditionally generates ops from interrogation (e.g. Drill Press only when drilled_hole/countersink/counterbore detected); CHECK validates, Versions tracks history.

Demo H — 2025 Enhancements

  1. Multi-select DXF files → Update Thickness modal sets thickness in bulk; costing recalculates; shared-file guardrail fires.
  2. Operation-definition editor: edit variable defaults + visibility, display units (incl. parts/hr), ERP code, outside-service flag.
  3. Operation drawer shows Calculated/Override + runtime variables + ⓘ tooltips; inline note preview works from the router list; notes can be programmatic.
  4. Edit Dimensions / Part Setup: click a print extraction to set X/Y/Z; math expression and typed-unit inputs evaluate/convert; Swap units works.
  5. Email Templates: set a DEFAULT per type (quote-send/order-shipment/order-refund); merge fields render.
  6. Quote Display Settings toggles change the digital quote (immediately) and the PDF (on next send); restyled PDF.
  7. Company Unit Preferences (imperial/metric) + Date Formatting apply everywhere except analytics/operations; "Require 2FA for external share" enforced.
  8. Requotes setting greys out "request requote" on expired digital quotes.
  9. Facilitate Order drawer: select-all, bulk Ships-On, search, per-line discounts/charges; edit an order pre-shipment (PO, address, add/remove) with history; opt-in toggle in Settings.

Demos I & J — Sheet Metal & Nesting

  1. Sheet-metal part uses the "Default Sheet Metal (Laser)" interrogation profile → sheet-metal features (Bend Relief, Cutout, Sheet Metal Bend) + warnings (Close Bends, Non-90° Bend, Small Bend Radius, W-Bending, …); checking a warning highlights it on the model.
  2. Update Process & Material modal: UPDATE regenerates the router; UPDATE AND KEEP EXISTING OPS preserves it; material is a hierarchical type-ahead.
  3. Single-component Nesting Module auto-populates sheet size/threshold/thickness/buffers; generate → Yield/Drop/Scrap/Parts-per-Sheet + layout; HAS NEST badge appears; material cost updates per quantity break.
  4. Multi-component eligibility alert when parts share material+thickness → Nesting Overview grid → select compatible → Prepare Multiple Sheet Nests (per-break stock + ERP code + cost/sheet, nest settings) → GENERATE N NESTS with shared-costing lock.
  5. Nest #1 result: total sheets/cost/used/scrap/drop/contour-length + per-sheet-type breakdown + rendered layout; associated sibling nests per break.
  6. Requirements Review flags CUI on a print; the digital quote lets the buyer pick a lead-time/price tier (with optional NRE add-on) and check out.

Demo K — CNC Machine Shops (overview)

  1. CNC part uses a milling interrogation profile ("Default Mill" / "Milling (Robodrill)") → features + warnings; a "CNC | Mill Complexity Indicator" process is selectable.
  2. Requirements Review offers a "Double Check" action alongside no-quote / assign-estimator / add-operation / not-applicable.
  3. Send-quote composer supports To/CC/BCC recipients, a template picker, editable subject/body, and Include-PDF.
  4. Customer digital quote shows an "AVAILABLE FROM $X–$Y/ea" range, View in 3D, buyer-selectable add-ons (NRE, Certificate of Conformance), and a qty×lead-time pricing grid for checkout.

Demo L — Purchased Components (real-time)

  1. Component ⋮ → Convert to purchased component opens the Smart Match modal; selecting an OEM/geometric/historical match (CONVERT enabled) tags the row purchased.
  2. Once converted, the same geometry/part# auto-tags as purchased in future quotes (no re-conversion).
  3. Purchased rows show preferred-distributor pricing + per-break inventory: green ≥200% on-hand, amber "at risk", "?" loading; a pricing timestamp shows.
  4. Changing/adding a make-quantity break re-queries and refreshes price + availability across all columns; high volume surfaces at-risk stock.
  5. Real-time Pricing Settings (BETA): switch a component between Dynamic per-piece and a fixed bulk tier; change preferred distributor; SAVE persists the mode.

Demo M — Assembly Table

  1. Turning Grouped off in CHILD BOM enables single-level drag-reorder; the left tree updates; cross-level moves are not drag-supported.
  2. Breadcrumb + sibling dropdown navigate within/up the BOM; each component has a stable shareable URL; hovering a node shows a thumbnail + qty + status.
  3. "Replace Assembly with Manufactured Component" converts an over-nested sub-assembly into one made part consuming its purchased hardware; "Convert to Purchased" fixes bought parts.
  4. Copy Pricing copies Material/Operations to a sibling and to a component in another quote; Bulk Update Components mass-assigns process/material/finish.
  5. Import/Merge historical part adds a previously-quoted sub-assembly into the BOM preserving its costing; quote-level line items reorder by drag.
  6. BOM manipulation on a >100-component assembly stays responsive (no timeouts).
Verification: seed the demo tenants/accounts (DTMA, CC's CNC Machining, Blood Orange Machine, Paperless Precision, Pistol Pete's Parts, Dini's Demos, Sultans of Solutions, GOAT Machining Co.) and the demo parts so QA can replay all twelve scripts. Cross-check computed totals against the dollar figures in the screenshots where applicable.

Resolved Decisions & Build Directives v2.15 addendum

Every build-blocking open question was resolved with the client on 2026-06-12/13. Where this section conflicts with older text elsewhere in the spec (notably the Spatial-first geometry engine wording), this section wins.

Document precedence (for Claude Code). ① This master spec (v2.15, incl. this addendum) is authoritative. ② The supporting documents are normative references for specific areas: paperless-parts-build-spec.md → DFM check formulas/thresholds, the 62-attribute sheet-metal taxonomy, the Lens extraction taxonomy (12 categories), and Kalk function details; pp-workflow-technical-spec.md → the end-to-end role choreography (salesperson → estimator → purchasing → shop floor → manager), the Task-vs-@mention distinction, the two-layer status convention, and the purple-suggestion accept pattern; paperless-parts-technical-review.md → known capability ceilings (5-axis runtime is manual-override by design; GD&T is flagged, not auto-costed; nesting is estimation-grade). ③ pp-2025-features-factorybid-analysis.md is roadmap material only, except the four features explicitly promoted into v1 in § New v1 Scope. On any conflict, the order above decides. When the spec is silent, choose the simplest interpretation consistent with the demos and log it in DECISIONS.md.

Platform & architecture (decided)

DecisionChoiceBuild notes
TenancyMulti-tenant SaaSOne deployment, many shops. org_id on every row; enforce with Postgres row-level security + an org-scoped service layer. All Configure/Settings objects are per-org. File storage keyed org/<org_id>/…. Hybrid/dedicated instances are a future sales option, not a v1 concern.
BackendPython 3.12 + FastAPIPydantic models as the single schema source; SQLAlchemy + Alembic migrations; Celery on Redis for async jobs (extraction, geometry, nesting, email, sync). Chosen over Node because geometry (pythonocc), the LLM pipeline, and the Kalk sandbox are all Python-native.
FrontendReact + TypeScript + Vite, TailwindAs specced. TanStack Query + Table. OpenAPI-generated TS client from FastAPI keeps types honest across the language boundary.
Database / infraPostgreSQL 16 + RedisJSONB for extraction findings, per-qty-break value maps, and Calculated-vs-Override pairs ({calculated, override, unit} per variable). Postgres FTS for search v1 (PDF text + notes + structured fields); OpenSearch only if FTS proves insufficient.
HostingHetzner (EU/Germany) + DockerDocker Compose for dev and first production; move to managed K8s when load demands. S3-compatible object storage (Hetzner Object Storage; MinIO locally). German hosting is part of the GDPR/DACH sales story.
AuthClerkEmail-first login, passkeys, Google/Microsoft IdPs out of the box; enterprise SAML/OIDC via Clerk when needed. Clerk is a US processor → goes on the subprocessor list with a DPA; only identity data (name/email) lives there, never quote/CAD data. Map Clerk orgs → BF orgs; roles per § Authorization stay in BF's DB. Localize the Clerk UI (en/de).
Product nameTBD"Bid Factory" is a working title. Parameterize all branding: a BRAND config (name, logo, domain, email domain) consumed by UI, email templates, and PDF output. No hard-coded name strings.
Secrets managementInfisical (self-hosted on Hetzner)Open source, EU-only, no third-party subprocessor. Run as a Docker container on a Hetzner CX22. Set up before any other service is configured. All infrastructure credentials (Anthropic, Mailgun, Clerk, Hetzner Object Storage) loaded from Infisical via CLI at deploy time and in GitHub Actions. Never hardcode secrets or commit .env files.

Email Connectivity — decided

Quotes are sent from the estimator's own email address, not a platform address. Users connect Gmail or Outlook via OAuth (one-click) or configure a generic SMTP/IMAP account. The platform (Mailgun EU) is retained only for inbound RFQ forwarding ({org-slug}@rfq.tolera.eu → webhook → auto-quote). All quote sends, reply threading, and email notifications flow through the user's connected account.

Why: The customer receives the quote from the person they know (jan@acme-machining.de), not a SaaS platform address. Trust is higher, deliverability is better (DKIM/SPF handled by Google/Microsoft), and replies come back into BF automatically via the same OAuth connection.

Connection types (v1)

TypeSend mechanismReceive mechanismOAuth
GmailGmail API messages.sendGmail API history.list (incremental from stored historyId) — Celery task every 5 minGoogle OAuth2, scopes: gmail.send + gmail.readonly
Outlook / Microsoft 365Microsoft Graph API sendMailGraph API messages/delta (delta token stored per connection) — Celery task every 5 minMicrosoft OAuth2 (MSAL), scopes: Mail.Send + Mail.ReadWrite
Generic SMTP + IMAPSMTP with TLS (app passwords / credentials)IMAP polling every 5 min (imaplib); UNSEEN flag filterNone — username + password (encrypted at rest)

Data model (M0 — required from day one)

Table / fieldTypeNotes
user_email_connections.connection_typeENUM(gmail | outlook | smtp_imap)One active connection per user (can have multiple, one marked primary)
user_email_connections.from_addressTEXTThe address quotes are sent from — shown in the send-quote composer
user_email_connections.from_nameTEXTDisplay name on outbound emails
user_email_connections.encrypted_credentialsBYTEAEncrypted OAuth refresh token (Gmail/Outlook) or SMTP credentials. AES-256-GCM, key from Infisical. Never logged.
user_email_connections.gmail_history_idTEXT nullableStored after each Gmail sync — used for incremental history.list queries
user_email_connections.outlook_delta_linkTEXT nullableStored after each Outlook delta query — used for next incremental fetch
user_email_connections.last_synced_atTIMESTAMPTZUpdated after every successful sync run
quote_email_threads.quote_idFK → quotesOne thread record per quote that has been sent by email
quote_email_threads.sent_message_idTEXTThe RFC 2822 Message-ID of the first outbound email — used for In-Reply-To matching
quote_email_threads.provider_thread_idTEXT nullableGmail threadId or Outlook conversationId — used for efficient thread sync
email_messages.directionENUM(outbound | inbound)All messages in a quote thread, regardless of direction
email_messages.body_html / body_textTEXTStored for display in the quote communication timeline. Attachments saved to Object Storage and linked.

Threading mechanism

On send: BF captures the Message-ID returned by the Gmail/Outlook API and stores it on quote_email_threads.sent_message_id. Also stores the provider's threadId / conversationId.

On sync (Celery task every 5 minutes per connected user): fetch new messages since last historyId / delta_link. For each new message, check if In-Reply-To or References header matches a stored sent_message_id — or if the threadId / conversationId matches a stored thread. On match: create an email_message row, save attachments to Object Storage, emit a real-time notification to the estimator.

Fallback — no email connection configured

If an estimator has not connected an email account, the send-quote composer shows a banner: "Connect your email to send quotes from your own address." They can still send via the platform address (quotes@{domain} via Mailgun EU) — this is the fallback path, not the primary flow. The banner persists until an account is connected.

Settings page

Settings → User Profile → Email Connection: one-click "Connect Gmail" (Google OAuth popup) and "Connect Outlook" (Microsoft OAuth popup) buttons, plus a "Manual SMTP/IMAP" form. Shows current connection status, from_address, last synced timestamp. "Test connection" button sends a test email to the user's own address.

Build notes: Add OAuth app registrations to the pre-build infrastructure checklist — both Google and Microsoft require app registration and domain verification before OAuth flows work. Google requires gmail.readonly scope which triggers a security review for production apps (allow 2–3 weeks for approval — start this on day one). Store OAuth client ID/secret in Infisical. Encrypt all refresh tokens with AES-256-GCM, keys rotated quarterly. The 5-minute Celery sync task runs on the email queue; concurrency should be capped so N active users don't flood the external APIs.

Billing — Paddle (decided)

Self-service billing via Paddle (Merchant of Record). Paddle handles all EU VAT collection and remittance — critical for DACH B2B SaaS where customers have Umsatzsteuer-IDs and expect correct reverse-charge invoicing. Fees: ~5% + $0.50/transaction, no VAT compliance work on the product side.

v1 scope: build the data model and feature gates. Do not build the full Paddle checkout flow in v1. The pilot customer is on a free beta — no billing needed yet. But the data model must be correct from M0 so that wiring up Paddle later is webhook work, not a database migration across a live product.

v1 data model (required in M0)

FieldTypeNotes
org_billing.org_idFK → orgsOne row per org
org_billing.planENUM(trial | starter | growth | enterprise)Determines feature access. Set manually via admin CLI for pilot orgs.
org_billing.subscription_statusENUM(trialing | active | past_due | cancelled | unpaid | paused)Checked by feature gates. trialing = full access; past_due / unpaid = read-only mode after grace period.
org_billing.trial_ends_atTIMESTAMPTZNull = no trial expiry (for manually-managed pilot orgs)
org_billing.paddle_customer_idTEXT nullablePopulated when Paddle checkout is wired up (post-pilot)
org_billing.paddle_subscription_idTEXT nullablePopulated on first successful Paddle subscription
org_billing.current_period_ends_atTIMESTAMPTZ nullableFrom Paddle webhook; used for grace period calculation on past_due

Feature gating pattern (all feature-gated endpoints use this)

def require_active_subscription(org: Org = Depends(get_current_org)):
    billing = org.billing
    if billing.subscription_status in ("cancelled", "unpaid") and billing.grace_period_expired:
        raise HTTPException(402, "Subscription required")
    return org

For v1 pilot orgs, set plan = trial, subscription_status = trialing, trial_ends_at = NULL — they pass all gates indefinitely until Paddle is wired up.

Post-pilot (v1.x) — what Paddle wiring adds

Public signup → Paddle Checkout overlay → subscription.activated webhook → create Clerk org + BF org + org_billing row atomically → redirect to onboarding. Customer portal link for self-serve plan changes and invoice downloads. Dunning handled by Paddle. Failed payment → Paddle retries → on final failure, webhook sets subscription_status = unpaid → grace period (7 days) → read-only mode. This entire flow is post-pilot; the data model above means it requires no migrations when it ships.

Build note: Create an /admin/orgs/{org_id}/billing endpoint (Admin role only, internal) that allows manually setting plan and subscription_status for pilot orgs. This is the only billing UI needed for v1. Stub a POST /webhooks/paddle endpoint that logs the payload and returns 200 — proper processing ships in v1.x.

Geometry engine — v1 path (supersedes the Spatial-first wording)

Decided: OCCT now, Spatial later. No commercial license exists yet and procurement must not block the build. v1 is built entirely on open source behind the GeometryService interface; Spatial (or HOOPS) swaps in when licensed, lighting up native .SLDPRT and the deferred feature families.

Componentv1 implementation
Solid import + B-rep queriesOCCT via pythonocc-core: STEP/STP import, healing, bounding box, volume, surface area, mass props (density from material), face/edge topology
2D / meshezdxf for DXF (contours, pierce count, contour length, tight-corner angles); trimesh for STL (bbox, volume, area, orientation)
Web viewerthree.js + occt-import-js (WASM) for client-side STEP tessellation; face picking, selection data overlay, feature highlighting by face-id lists from the interrogation, section/measure tools
Feature recognitionWritten in-house on OCCT topology (this is the hard part — budget accordingly). Scope below.
Geometry signatureIn-house: normalized fingerprint = quantized (volume, area, bbox-sorted dims, face/edge type histogram, per-type areas) → SHA-256; plus exact file hash. Powers Part Library match buckets + purchased-component memory. Document the algorithm in GEOMETRY.md.

v1 process-family scope ("Core 4")

Familyv1 depthNotes
CNC MillingFull: holes (simple/drilled, countersink, counterbore via conical/cylindrical face patterns), pockets, machine directions (heuristic), + warnings: deep hole (ratio per material), tight corner, thin wall, small internal radius, deep radiused cutCounts feed costing formulas + rules
Sheet MetalFull: thickness detection, bend recognition (radius/length/angle/K-factor), unfold → flat pattern (flat area, bounding dims, contour length), nesting eligibility, bend relief + cutouts; warnings: close bends, non-90° bend, short flange, small bend radius, shallow/thin relief, different bend directions, W-bendingThe DACH fabricator wedge — highest quality bar
Waterjet/Laser flat (DXF)Full: flat features, per-pierce list, contour length, per-corner tight-corner warningsEasiest family; ship first
Additive (STL)Full (small surface): build-plate view, ±X/Y/Z reorientation, bbox/volume/areatrimesh covers it
Lathe / TurningAttributes only (dims/volume/area/weight) — no feature treeFeature trees arrive with Spatial
Wire EDMAttributes only — no feature treeSame
File formats v1: STEP/STP + DXF + STL. Native .SLDPRT is NOT supported in v1 (Parasolid is proprietary): uploading one stores the file, shows a friendly "please also export as STEP" prompt on the line item, and creates a review flag. Native SLDPRT lights up with the Spatial license (or a CAD Exchanger component if licensed sooner). DFM thresholds: compile the seed table (warning → formula → default threshold → per-material/machine overrides) from paperless-parts-build-spec.md (short flange <2× thickness, close bends 3–4×, deep hole 8–10:1 by material, thin wall <1.0 mm, small internal radius <0.5 mm, W-bending 0.8–1.0, etc.) into Configure → Interrogations defaults; metric-first values for DACH.

AI extraction ("Lens") — implementation

DecisionChoice
EngineClaude API (vision) for all v1 extraction: RFQ email parsing (parts list, qtys, dates), attachment classification, BOM-table detection + extraction, title-block reading, GD&T/callout/dimension extraction with bounding boxes, plain-language descriptions, units detection. No custom-trained model in v1.
Privacy / CUIPer-org "AI processing" toggle + automatic skip for CUI/ITAR/export-flagged files. Disabled ⇒ no file content leaves the environment; extraction fields fall back to manual entry (the UI already supports it). Use the API with a zero-data-retention agreement; list Anthropic as a subprocessor in the DPA.
PipelineTwo-stage Celery pipeline per file: Stage 1 — Extract: Claude vision API extracts all findings as structured JSON, each with a confidence: "high" | "medium" | "low" self-assessment and a reasoning field explaining why. Store as ExtractionFinding rows (category, value, parsed JSON, page, bbox, units, confidence 0–1 mapped from high→0.9 / medium→0.7 / low→0.4). Stage 2 — Verify (accuracy gate): for every finding with confidence < 0.7, immediately dispatch a focused verification call — crop the page to the finding's bbox + small margin, send to Claude with a binary prompt ("Does this callout clearly show X? Answer yes or no with one-sentence reasoning"). Verified-yes raises confidence to 0.85; verified-no removes the finding. This two-stage approach maximises accuracy regardless of cost (accuracy > cost, per product decision). Findings at ≥ 0.7 after stage 2 auto-fill; findings below render as purple suggestions (click-to-accept).
Feedback loop"Mark as inaccurate" / "Add missing extraction" persist as per-tenant labeled corrections (accuracy_state + source region + correct value). v1 uses them for regression evals of the prompt pipeline, not fine-tuning. Prompts + eval set live in the repo (extraction/prompts/, extraction/evals/).
TaxonomyImplement the master spec's ExtractionFinding categories first; extend toward the 12-category/100-field taxonomy in paperless-parts-build-spec.md as rules demand.

Kalk execution (all three formula contexts)

Restricted Python via AST validation, evaluated in Celery workers: parse with ast, whitelist node types (no import, no attribute access outside the exposed object model, no dunders, no exec/eval/open), inject only the documented API (get_components, set_custom_cost, generate_operation, get_features, analyze_sheet_metal, table_var, set_notes/set_notes_from_list, set_operation_name, set_workpiece_value/get_workpiece_value, is_close, drop-down variables, …), enforce wall-clock + instruction limits. Operation-formula evaluations for a given part run in router order, not in parallel — this is required so that set_workpiece_value writes from an earlier operation are visible to later operations via get_workpiece_value; parallelising them would break the shared workpiece dictionary contract. CHECK = parse + whitelist + dry-run against the current part; SAVE = new immutable version. Compile the consolidated Kalk Language Reference (one appendix merging every function across master + supporting docs, per context) before implementing — it is the contract.

Email infrastructure

AreaDecision
ProviderMailgun, EU region for inbound + outbound. Inbound routes POST parsed MIME (+ attachments) to the ingest webhook; outbound with per-org sending identity, DKIM/SPF on the platform domain.
Addressing<org-slug>@rfq.<BRAND-domain> per org (multi-tenant). Slug shown in Settings → Email Forwarding.
Ingest pipelinewebhook → store raw .eml (ORIGINAL RFQ) → extract attachments (PDF/STEP/ZIP/SLDPRT; recurse ZIPs; size cap 200 MB/file) → sender→Account/Contact match (against native + CRM-synced records) → draft Quote → Lens email parse → Bulk-Create prefill → notification.

Payments, VAT & GDPR (DACH reality)

  • Checkout v1 = Purchase Order only (+ Local Pickup toggle). Credit cards deferred to v1.x (Stripe targeted); the Accounting settings group ships with PO/payment-terms config and hides card config until then.
  • VAT in v1 (replaces the US-style "Default Tax Rate"): per-org VAT registration + default rate (DE 19% / reduced 7%); per-account override; intra-EU B2B reverse charge (zero-rate + "Steuerschuldnerschaft des Leistungsempfängers" note) driven by buyer VAT-ID with VIES validation (cached, with manual override); VAT lines on digital quote, quote PDF, and order; quotes display net + VAT + gross.
  • Deferred but data-model-ready: ZUGFeRD/XRechnung e-invoicing, GoBD-grade archival (store immutable copies of every issued quote PDF / order confirmation with hash + timestamp now — that part is cheap).
  • GDPR compliance baseline in v1: EU-only data residency (Hetzner); DPA template + subprocessor list (Mailgun, Anthropic, Clerk, Würth, HubSpot); per-org data export (JSON/CSV); contact/personal-data erasure workflow (anonymize DB fields only — Contact.name, Contact.email, Quote.contact_name/address replaced with anonymized placeholders; archived quote PDFs are not modified or deleted — they are legal commercial records, the original exists on the customer's email server, and GoBD requires 10-year retention of the original document); audit log; configurable retention for quotes/files; no third-party trackers in-product. AVV/TOMs documents drafted alongside (German-market paperwork).

AI Architecture & Feature Roadmap v2.15

Bid Factory is AI-native by design. Every AI touch point follows a single governing principle: AI suggests and surfaces; the estimator decides and confirms. Estimating is art — pricing strategy, customer relationships, and risk tolerance belong to the estimator. AI handles the legwork (extraction, pattern matching, history retrieval, anomaly detection) so the estimator's judgement lands on a better-prepared canvas.

AI processing toggle applies to all features below: When disabled per-org, Lens fields become manual entry, triage briefs are skipped, the assistant is unavailable. No feature silently fails — each shows a clear "AI processing disabled" state. CUI/ITAR-flagged files are always skipped regardless of the toggle.

Three-tier architecture

TierMechanismCharacterHuman gate
T1 — Embedded AIDirect Claude API calls from Celery workers or FastAPI handlers; results cached on model entitiesInvisible — runs in the background; surfaces as purple suggestion chips, triage cards, coaching flagsClick-to-accept; never auto-applies without an explicit estimator action
T2 — In-App AssistantClaude API chat session with server-injected quote/part context (read-only snapshot; assistant cannot write to the quote directly)Conversational — estimator asks, assistant answers or drafts; collapsible side panel on any quote or part viewAll answers are advisory; any write action (applying a suggestion, importing a router) requires an explicit estimator confirm in the main UI
T3 — MCP ServerFastAPI routes exposed as Model Context Protocol tools at /v1/mcp/; tool schemas versioned alongside the APIExternal-agent-facing — allows Claude Desktop, shop automations, and future AI surfaces to call into Bid Factory without a custom integrationRead + suggest tools freely callable; confirm tools require human_confirmed: true in the request — return {status: "pending_confirmation"} without it

Tier 3 — MCP Server detail

Building an MCP server on top of existing FastAPI routes makes Bid Factory composable with any MCP-compatible agent at minimal marginal cost — an MCP tool is a typed schema on an endpoint you are already building. Ship in v1 as infrastructure even if no external surfaces use it immediately; the future surfaces it unlocks (Claude Desktop plugin for estimators, shop-level automation agents, ERP/CRM agents that pull quote status) require no rewrite once it exists.

CategoryExample toolsBehaviour
Readget_open_quotes, get_quote_detail, get_review_items, get_part_matches, get_vendor_quotes, get_triage_briefReturn structured JSON; no side effects; freely callable by any authorised agent
Suggestsuggest_vendors(line_item_id), draft_quote_from_history(part_id), get_requote_diff(part_id_a, part_id_b)Return a proposed action + rationale JSON; no side effects; agent must present to a human before acting
Confirmapply_vendor_quote, send_quote, accept_draft_routerReturn {"status":"pending_confirmation","summary":"…"} unless called with human_confirmed: true; calling agent must present the summary and receive explicit human approval before re-calling
Build notes: All MCP responses are model-context-friendly JSON (flat, no HTML, all IDs included for follow-up calls). Disable per org via the existing AI processing toggle. Version the schema at /v1/mcp/. Ship the server in M6 alongside NL Search — low incremental cost if routes are designed with MCP schemas in mind from M1 onwards.

AI Feature 1 — RFQ Triage Brief

Governing question: "Should I work on this RFQ now, and am I the right person?"

Trigger: Automatic — chains after the Lens email-parse Celery job when a new draft Quote is created via email ingest or the Smart RFQ form. Runs once per quote; re-runs if new files are added before any estimator opens it. Result cached as triage_brief JSONB on the Quote.

UI placement: Dashboard notification card for the new quote — replaces the plain "New quote from email" notification with a structured card. Also accessible as a collapsible "Triage" chip in the Quote header.

SignalExampleWhy it's here
Parts + file summary7 parts · 5 STEP + 3 PDFScope at a glance — 1-part job or a 30-part programme?
Missing files ⚠⚠ No file received: PP-2244, PP-5531Hard blocker — estimator must go back to the customer before costing starts
Detected processesCNC milling (likely) · Anodize (likely)From material keywords + file types in the email-parse output; routes to the right estimator
Estimated time to quoteEst. 2–3 hrsDeterministic formula (part count × process type × per-org historical avg), not AI. Feeds workload planning.
Customer (one line)Existing · Arch Medial · 8 quotes · 63% win rateNew vs known. Full intelligence brief fires at send-time, not here.
Compliance flags ⚠⚠ ITAR text detected in email bodyChanges what the estimator can do — always surfaced, never suppressible
Need-by + urgencyNeed-by: 14.08.2026 · 6 daysFeeds the dashboard urgency score; natural deadline visible at triage

What is NOT in the triage brief: full GD&T extraction (not run yet at this stage); detailed material specs (process family is enough); price or cost estimates (no costing data exists yet — early anchoring is harmful); detailed tolerance analysis (a "tight tolerances detected" flag suffices); email body summary (estimator reads the email themselves).

AI job: Single Claude call over the Lens email-parse output (parts list, file classifications, sender→Account match result, compliance keyword scan). Claude produces the structured brief JSON and infers process hints from material keywords and free text. The time-to-quote figure is deterministic (no AI).

Human gate: Informational. Suggested assignee role is a one-click chip. Estimator acts or dismisses.

Build notes: Add triage_brief JSONB to Quote. Celery task generate_triage_brief chains after wingman_email_parse. Dashboard notification component renders the brief card when present. Re-run on new file upload before the quote is opened by any estimator.

AI Feature 2 — Rule Auto-Suggestion

Governing question: "Is there tribal knowledge I'm applying manually that should be a rule?"

Trigger: When an estimator manually adds an operation to a line item's router (not via rule resolution), the system checks: has this same operation been manually added to 3+ parts in the same process family + material class in the last 90 days? If yes, surface a suggestion.

UI placement: Non-blocking suggestion chip in the operation drawer immediately after the manual add. Also surfaces on the Dashboard suggested-actions strip via a nightly batch job. One-click opens the Create Rule dialog pre-seeded with the detected pattern (signal name, operation name, likely condition). The human designs and saves the final rule.

AI job: Pattern detection is a DB aggregate query (OperationInstance.added_manually = true count). Claude only translates the detected pattern into a human-readable suggestion sentence and pre-seeds the rule dialog with a condition draft. No rule is created by AI.

Human gate: Estimator opens Create Rule (pre-seeded) or dismisses. No rule is created without human authoring and clicking CREATE RULE.

Build notes: Add added_manually BOOL to OperationInstance — set when an operation is added directly by the estimator, cleared if resolved by a rule. Nightly Celery job scans for patterns per org and surfaces suggestions via the existing SuggestedAction mechanism.

AI Feature 3 — Requote Diff Assistant

Governing question: "What changed between this revision and the last time I quoted this part?"

Trigger: (a) Estimator clicks "Create Revision" on an existing quote and a Part Library match is found; (b) a requote request arrives via the buyer portal with matching files detected; (c) manually triggered from the Part Library match panel.

UI placement: Banner on the Part Estimating View when a previous quote is found: "Previous quote found (Rev A, Quote #1234, 3 months ago) — see what changed ▾". Clicking expands a diff panel. Visible until dismissed or the estimator starts editing.

TypeSourceExample
Geometry deltaOCCT stats subtraction (volume, bounding box, feature counts)Volume: −2.3% · Holes: same 12 · Pockets: +1
Drawing changesExtractionFinding set diff (added, removed, changed)New callout: "FIRST ARTICLE INSPECTION REQUIRED" · Bore 3 tolerance: ±0.010 → ±0.005
AI synthesisClaude over the structured diff JSON"Minor change — tighter bore tolerance and an FAI requirement added. The previous router is likely still valid; review the Inspection operation and consider adding an FAI add-on."

AI job: Claude receives the structured diff (OCCT delta + extraction diff as clean JSON — not raw files). It identifies which changes are material (require router/pricing changes) vs cosmetic and writes one synthesis paragraph.

Human gate: Three explicit choices: Import router from Rev A (carries forward the previous router; all values marked "imported from Quote #X") / Review diff field-by-field / Start fresh. Nothing is copied without an explicit click.

Build notes: Expose diff_parts(part_id_a, part_id_b) → DiffResult in the geometry service. DiffResult: {geometry_delta, finding_diff: {added[], removed[], changed[]}}. Cache as requote_diff JSONB on the new Quote. Celery computes on quote creation when a Part Library Exact Geometric or Exact File match exists.

AI Feature 4 — Agentic Quote Assembly

Governing question: "This is a repeat part I know well — can the system do the mechanical work so I just verify?"

Trigger: When a line item is created and a Part Library match at Exact File or Exact Geometric confidence is found, a banner appears: "This part was quoted [N] times — want me to draft from the most recent quote?"

UI placement: Banner on Part Estimating View (same banner location as Requote Diff — combined into one panel when both are active on the same line item).

Flow:

  1. Claude generates a one-paragraph review comment — what's the same, what changed (drawing or geometry delta if any), what to watch out for. Estimator reads this before deciding to accept.
  2. Two choices are presented:
    • Review — imports the router + pricing; all imported values are individually highlighted as "AI-drafted" (purple/yellow chips); the quote is not ready-to-send until the estimator explicitly reviews each section.
    • Accept All — for estimators who know the part well: imports the full router + pricing in a single click. All values are marked "imported from Quote #N — [date]" in the audit trail (not individually flagged). A 60-second undo chip appears immediately after. Accept All is only available when the diff shows no material changes (no significant geometry delta and no new drawing callouts) — if material changes are detected, only Review is offered and this is clearly noted.
  3. In both paths, all imported values carry source = imported visible in the operation variable drawer and the quote's audit log.

AI job: Primarily deterministic (copy the router from the historical quote — already specced as the Part Library "select a historical match" behaviour). Claude adds the review comment. When Requote Diff and Agentic Assembly are triggered on the same line item, Claude writes a single combined brief covering both.

Human gate: Review path: estimator reviews each section individually. Accept All path: estimator reads Claude's review comment, then clicks Accept All. Sixty-second undo provides a safety net. Accept All is suppressed server-side when diff.geometry_delta.significant = true OR diff.finding_diff.material_changes ≠ [].

Build notes: Add source ENUM(manual | imported | ai_drafted) and source_quote_id to OperationInstance and pricing variable overrides. Accept All calls a single atomic endpoint that copies the entire router + pricing in one transaction. Undo endpoint reverts to a blank line item within 60s via a Redis key (undo:quote_assembly:{line_item_id}, TTL 60s). accept_all_eligible: bool is computed server-side and returned in the PartMatch response.

AI Feature 5 — Customer Intelligence Brief

Governing question: "Before I send this quote, what do I know about this customer that should change my approach?"

Trigger: Lazy — fires when the send-quote composer is opened, if the Account has ≥ 3 prior quotes. Not stored; generated on demand.

UI placement: Collapsible card at the top of the send-quote side panel (above To/CC/BCC), labelled "About [Account Name]". Dismissible per session.

Content — 2–3 bullets maximum: Claude picks the most decision-relevant signals (not a data dump). Examples: win rate + trend (last 12 months); whether this quote value is outside their historical range; revision pattern (first-accept vs always negotiates); estimator-authored Account Notes with pricing-relevant text.

Example output: Arch Medial Solutions · 8 quotes · 5 won (63%) — this quote is €31k, their largest order with you (previous max: €12k). They've never accepted without at least one revision in the last 12 months.

AI job: DB aggregate query per Account → Claude writes 2–3 bullets, weighting what's unusual or decision-relevant. The model's job is editorial (select and phrase), not report everything.

Human gate: Pure information. Estimator reads, applies their own judgement, sends.

Build notes: get_account_intelligence(account_id, quote_id) → AccountBrief service call. Triggered lazily on composer mount. Not persisted. Graceful degradation: brief omitted entirely (no empty state) when insufficient data.

AI Feature 6 — Pre-send Quote Review

Governing question: "Is there anything I've missed before I send this?"

Trigger: Auto-runs when the send-quote composer opens (same trigger as Customer Intelligence Brief — both appear in the same side panel, under sections "About this customer" and "Quote check"). Also manually triggerable from the Quote header.

Checklist (deterministic — Claude only narrates anomalies): unresolved Review Items count + types; line items with no lead time set; quote expiration date not set; line items still in "Awaiting vendor quote" state; margin outside the org's historical range for this process family (flagged, not blocked); missing contact (already a hard send precondition — surfaced early here as a warning).

AI job: Checklist is deterministic DB checks. If any check is warning or anomaly, one Claude call writes a plain-language sentence per item. If all checks pass: "Looks good — no issues detected." No Claude call on a clean pass (fast deterministic path).

Human gate: Non-blocking. Estimator can send over any warning. Hard send preconditions remain as already specced (contact assigned + ≥1 priced line item).

Build notes: pre_send_review(quote_id) → ReviewResult service call returning [{check, status, detail}]. Claude call fires only if any status ∈ {warning, anomaly}. Rendered inline in the send composer — no modal.

AI Feature 7 — Estimation Coaching (opt-in)

Governing question: "Is my cycle time estimate in line with what we've done on similar parts?"

Trigger: When an estimator saves a manual override on a cycle time or setup time field, the system queries historical similar parts (pgvector ANN + same operation name). If the value deviates >40% from the median and N ≥ 5 historical matches exist, a non-blocking yellow info chip appears on the overridden field.

Example chip: ⚠ Similar parts — MILL-03 median: 2.8 hrs (range 2.2–3.5). Your estimate: 1.8 hrs.

AI job: DB query (pgvector ANN top-20 + operation filter) → compute median + range → Claude writes one sentence. Almost entirely deterministic. No Claude call if fewer than 5 historical matches are found.

Human gate: Pure advisory. Estimator dismisses or adjusts. No block.

Opt-in: Settings → Company Settings → "Enable Estimation Coaching" toggle (default: off). Recommended for shops onboarding junior estimators; experienced single-estimator shops will find it unnecessary.

Build notes: Fires on OperationInstance save when is_overridden = true. Cache per part + operation pair for the session. The toggle check is the first thing evaluated — no DB query runs if coaching is disabled.

AI Feature 8 — Margin Coach

Governing question: "Is my margin competitive for this type of work?"

Activation: The data layer (win/loss + pricing per quote) is built in v1 as part of existing functionality — no extra collection needed. The Margin Coach UI panel activates automatically per-org once they reach ≥ 50 won/lost quotes. Shows nothing (not even an empty state) until that threshold is crossed. Expected to activate 3–6 months post-pilot for early adopters.

UI placement: Collapsible strip at the bottom of the Pricing section in the Part Estimating View. Shown only when ≥ 5 similar historical quotes are found for this part class.

Content: For this part class (process family + material class + size band), the median margin achieved and win rate correlation across margin bands. 2–3 stat chips. This is a reference point — not a recommendation. The estimator sets their own margin.

AI job: DB aggregate → Claude writes 1–2 sentences. No AI call if insufficient data.

Human gate: Pure information.

Build notes: get_margin_benchmark(part_id, process, material) → MarginBenchmark | null service. Returns null if data threshold unmet; front-end renders nothing. Do not prompt the estimator to "add more quotes" — the panel simply appears when threshold is crossed.

AI Feature 9 — Natural Language Search (beta)

Governing question: "How do I find quotes without knowing the exact filter combination?"

Trigger: Estimator types a natural language query in the global search bar. A "✦ Ask" mode toggle (or smart auto-detection of natural language vs keyword) routes to the NL pipeline.

Example queries: "lost quotes on titanium 5-axis parts over €10k last year" · "quotes sent to Bosch with open review items" · "won CNC jobs where margin was above 35% this year".

AI job: text-to-filter-set, not text-to-SQL. Claude translates the NL query into a structured FilterSet (process, material, status, date range, account, outcome, margin band, etc.) executed against existing list endpoints. Returns: interpreted_filters (shown as editable chips so the estimator can see and correct Claude's interpretation) + result_count. A query→filter example library (20–30 patterns) is embedded in the system prompt to improve edge-case reliability.

Human gate: Read-only results. Estimator sees and can edit the filter chips before acting on results.

Beta framing: Ships behind a "✦ Ask (Beta)" label with an inline "what can I search for?" helper prompt. No results are actioned — search only.

Build notes: POST /search/nl endpoint. Claude receives: query string + schema summary (field names, enum values, date ranges) + example query→filter mappings. Returns {filters: FilterSet, interpretation: string, result_count: int}. Executes the filter against existing quotes/parts list endpoints — no new query surface. Ship in M6.

New v1 Scope — beyond the reference clone v2.15

Four differentiators promoted from the roadmap into v1. Everything else in pp-2025-features-factorybid-analysis.md (drag-drop PDF layout builder, what-if BOM editing, geometry-vector similarity, RFQ intelligence scoring) stays post-v1.

1 · Margin-target mode (pricing engine)

Any pricing item gets a third calculation mode beside Markup/Margin %: Target Margin — the user types the margin % they want the line item to net, and the system back-calculates the required markup amount on the item's target category given everything else in the stack. Live preview shows resulting Total/Unit/Margin. Implementation: solve amount_i such that Total Profit / Total = target, holding other pricing items fixed; show "unreachable" state if other items already exceed the target.

2 · Dashboard — first-principles redesign

Replaces the reference three-panel dashboard (the classic Workflows table remains available as a saved view). Organizing principle: "What needs me now."

  • Work queue (primary): one merged, role-aware, prioritized list across quotes needing my action, tasks assigned to me, review items assigned to me, vendor RFQs awaiting send/ingest, and @mentions. Each row: entity, why it surfaced (reason chip: "due in 2d", "3 unresolved review items", "vendor quote received"), urgency score, deep link to the exact spot (viewer page, review panel, vendor RFQ).
  • Urgency score: w1·days_to_due⁻¹ + w2·quote_value_band + w3·unresolved_count + w4·flags(expedite/VIP/export) — weights org-configurable in Settings, sensible defaults shipped. Deterministic and explainable (show the contributing factors on hover).
  • Recently opened: a strip of the user's last 8 quotes/parts (thumbnail + status) for instant resume.
  • Retained from reference: global search, notifications feed, "View all tasks / review items" links. KPI glance row (open quotes, due this week, win rate 30d) for managers.

3 · Two-way email threading

Quote-scoped email where quotes are sent from the estimator's own email address (Gmail or Outlook via OAuth, or generic SMTP/IMAP). The customer sees the email from jan@acme-machining.de — not a platform address. Outbound: send via Gmail API / Outlook Graph API / SMTP from the user's connected account; store the Message-ID + provider threadId. Inbound: a Celery sync task runs every 5 minutes per connected user, fetching new messages via Gmail history.list or Outlook messages/delta; replies are matched to quotes via In-Reply-To header or provider thread ID and appended to the quote's communication timeline. Attachments on replies land in Quote Files with the file→line-item matching banner. The EXTERNAL chat channels and email threads display in one unified communication view per quote. Fallback: if no email account is connected, outbound uses the platform address via Mailgun EU with a "Connect your email" prompt. See § Email Connectivity decision for full data model and sync architecture.

4 · Vendor RFQ Portal (outside-process sourcing)

The competitive wedge — the reference product has nothing equivalent. Scope as agreed:

  1. Vendor library: at onboarding (and any time in Configure → Vendors) the org uploads/maintains a vendor list (CSV or manual: name, contact email, process types served — anodize, plating, heat treat, paint, laser OSV, …).
  2. Queue: any part/operation marked external / outside service automatically appears on the Vendor RFQ page (nav under Quotes), grouped by process type, with quote context.
  3. Compose: select one or more vendors (filtered to matching process types) → the RFQ auto-populates: part info (number/rev/material/spec/finish callouts pulled from extractions), quantities + need-by date, and the relevant files (redacted versions selectable). Editable subject/body.
  4. Send: multi-vendor blind send (recipients isolated) — reuses the ExternalShare infrastructure (scoped expiring links, per-recipient permissions, access logging).
  5. Ingest: vendor responds either via the portal form (no account required: price per qty break, lead time, notes, file upload) or by email reply (threaded ingest parses/attaches it). Responses appear on the originating operation with one-click apply to outside-service cost.
  6. History: every response persists per vendor + process type → "typical anodize cost X–Y €" hints once data accumulates (display-only in v1).
The structural win: vendor quotes stop living in inboxes. Instrument usage from day one — portal open rate, submit rate, apply rate.

Each milestone ends with a verifiable acceptance subset bound to /fixtures; do not start the next until the previous one's checks pass.
