Development Workflow — Git, PRs & Branches v2.15

Prescribed for Claude Code. Follow this structure from day one — do not deviate without updating this section and CLAUDE.md.

Branch strategy

BranchPurposeBranches fromMerges intoDeploys to
mainProduction-ready code only. Never commit directly. Tagged releases trigger prod deploy via Kamal.——Production (on tag)
developIntegration branch. All completed feature + fix work lands here first. Must be green (CI passing) at all times.main (at project init)main (via release PR)Staging (on merge)
feature/{description}All new feature work. One branch per logical unit of work (not per file). Example: feature/wingman-extraction-pipelinedevelopdevelop (via PR)—
fix/{description}Bug fixes found during development (non-production). Example: fix/quote-total-rounding-errordevelopdevelop (via PR)—
hotfix/{description}Critical production bugs that cannot wait for the next release. Example: hotfix/email-ingest-crash-on-empty-subjectmainmain + develop (both, via separate PRs)Production (immediately after merge to main)
milestone/M1, milestone/M2 …Optional: group long-running milestone work before integrating into develop. Use when a milestone has multiple feature branches that depend on each other and aren't individually releasable.developdevelop (via PR at milestone completion)—

Pull request rules

RuleDetail
No direct commits to main or developAll changes arrive via PR. Even solo — the PR is the audit trail and the CI gate.
CI must pass before mergeGitHub Actions runs pytest, type checks (mypy), and the extraction eval suite on every PR. A failing PR cannot be merged.
PR description templateEvery PR must include: (1) What changed and why; (2) Milestone association (e.g. M3); (3) How to test it manually; (4) Any follow-up issues created. Claude Code should populate this automatically.
Squash merge into developFeature and fix PRs are squash-merged to keep the develop history clean. Hotfix PRs are merge-committed (to preserve the hotfix context on both main and develop).
Release PRs (develop → main)Created at milestone completion. Title: Release: M{N} — {milestone name}. Include a summary of all features + fixes in the milestone. Tag with v0.{N}.0 after merge to trigger prod deploy.

Issue / bug tracking

TypeLabelBranch prefixPriority rule
New featurefeaturefeature/Scheduled to a milestone
Bug found in development (non-production)bugfix/Block current milestone if it breaks a golden test or exit criterion; otherwise schedule to next milestone
Production bug (pilot customer impacted)bug + productionhotfix/Fix immediately; deploy via hotfix flow above
Tech debt / refactorchorefix/Only in milestone gaps; never block feature work
Spec ambiguity / decision neededquestion—Open a DECISIONS.md entry; do not guess — block and ask

CLAUDE.md standing instructions (seed content)

The following must be in CLAUDE.md from M0. Claude Code reads this file at the start of every session. Add to it as conventions are established.

# Bid Factory — CLAUDE.md

## Branch rules (mandatory)
- Never commit directly to `main` or `develop`
- Feature work: branch from `develop` as `feature/{description}`
- Bug fixes: branch from `develop` as `fix/{description}`
- Production hotfixes: branch from `main` as `hotfix/{description}`, merge to BOTH main and develop
- All merges via PR with CI passing

## Spec authority
- Build spec: `Bid-Factory-Build-Spec.html` — source of truth for all features
- Where spec conflicts with older text, the v2.15 addendum wins (stated in spec)
- Ambiguities: add to `DECISIONS.md` with date + question; do not guess

## Run commands
- Start local stack: `docker compose up`
- Seed demo data: `docker compose exec app python -m scripts.seed_demo`
- Run tests: `docker compose exec app pytest`
- Type check: `docker compose exec app mypy app/`
- Deploy to staging: `kamal deploy --destination staging`
- Deploy to prod: `kamal deploy` (only from main, only on tagged release)

## Golden tests (M1 exit criterion)
- Difficult-Material case must reproduce $2,160.84 exactly
- All Demo E pricing examples must match verified figures
- Never break these — they are the pricing engine's source of truth

## Code conventions
- Python 3.12 + FastAPI; all endpoints use Pydantic v2 schemas
- SQLAlchemy 2.0 (async); Alembic for all migrations
- Celery tasks: always idempotent; always handle retry gracefully
- org_id on every DB row; Postgres RLS enforced at DB level
- All file access via pre-signed URLs — never expose raw storage paths
- AI calls: tag every Anthropic request with org_id for cost tracking
- Secrets: never hardcode; load from environment via Infisical (self-hosted on Hetzner)
DECISIONS.md: A companion file in the repo root. When Claude Code encounters a genuine ambiguity not resolved by the spec, it must add an entry: date, question, options considered, and block (do not guess). Benjamin reviews and resolves entries before the next build session. This prevents silent wrong assumptions from compounding across sessions.

Open Questions for the Client

  1. Pricing composition order. ✓ RESOLVED. Confirmed by the client + verified against demo numbers (see Costing & Pricing → Calculation model): pricing is per line item, not per quote; each pricing item is computed independently off its target category's cost and summed (no compounding on a running price); custom categories are re-slices not added to Total Estimated Cost, so overlapping markups stack additively without double-counting cost. Remaining sub-question: the exact contribution formula for Margin-type items when mixed with markups (markups are fully verified; margin math is documented as cost × pct/(1−pct) but not seen in a demo).
  2. Geometry engine. ✓ DECIDED — lead vendor selected. Hybrid — license on-prem/self-hostable CAD toolkits for import + viewer + feature-recognition behind a swappable GeometryService; build DFM rules + sheet-metal interrogation on top. v1 = full auto interrogation across all six process families; formats STEP/STP + native SLDPRT + STL + DXF; self-hosted/EU, files never leave (Autodesk APS ruled out as cloud-only). Lead vendor = Spatial (Dassault) — verified to cover native SOLIDWORKS import + feature recognition + sheet-metal unfold + semantic PMI + HOOPS web viewer (.SLDPRT support question now resolved). Alternatives: HOOPS, or an open-source-first OCCT/three.js/trimesh/ezdxf fallback. See Geometry engine. v2.15 update: v1 builds on the open-source path — OCCT (pythonocc) + three.js/occt-import-js + trimesh + ezdxf behind GeometryService; Core-4 families full (milling, sheet metal, waterjet/laser-flat, additive), lathe/wire-EDM attributes-only, STEP-only solids (no native .SLDPRT until the Spatial swap-in). Spatial licensing proceeds in parallel and is no longer a build blocker. See Resolved Decisions → Geometry.
  3. Likelihood / win-probability\. ✓ RESOLVED (v2.15). Manual % entry per quantity break in v1; values accumulate as training data for a future model-computed version.
  4. Custom Tables & cost formulas\. ✓ RESOLVED (v2.15). Full Kalk ships in MVP — all three formula contexts (pricing, operation-generation, operation-formula) as an AST-sandboxed restricted-Python subset, plus CSV-backed Custom Tables with quote-time overrides. Fixed formulas alone cannot reproduce the demos. See Resolved Decisions → Kalk.
  5. ERP scope\. ✓ RESOLVED (v2.15). v1 = a stub push adapter with a documented JSON payload schema (ERP_PAYLOAD.md), push-only. Real ERP adapters (incl. DACH ERPs) post-pilot, driven by customer demand.
  6. Customer portal\. ✓ RESOLVED (v2.15). Customer-facing in v1: the buyer digital quote + self-serve checkout (Purchase Order only; cards v1.x) AND external vendor sharing. Plus the new Vendor RFQ Portal.
  7. CAD connectors. ✓ DECIDED. Pluggable CadConnector adapters, opt-in/power-user, separate from the no-license native engine and the self-hosted core. Fusion is in v1 (APS free-tier: OAuth + Data Management + Webhooks; no token-metered APIs needed; cloud-only so disable-able per account). Fast-follows: Onshape (cloud OAuth/REST) → SOLIDWORKS full round-trip → Creo/NX via PDM. See § CAD connectors. ✓ RESOLVED (2026-06-21): SOLIDWORKS connector deferred post-pilot; default target = the desktop add-in (most common for SME DACH shops); revisit PDM/3DEXPERIENCE only on customer demand. See DECISIONS.md.
  8. Quote lifecycle / state machine. ✓ DECIDED. Statuses Draft · On-Hold · Sent · Won · Lost · Expired (soft) · Cancelled · No-Quote; Sent is locked (edit → Create Revision, Rev A→B supersede); closed quotes reopen in place to Draft; win/loss quote-level with partial-order conversion; orders built internally only; Lost reason optional. Full machine in § Quote status lifecycle. v2.15: all confirmed — On-Hold reachable from Draft and Sent (returns to prior status); revisions = Rev A/B/C; send preconditions = contact assigned + ≥1 priced line item; margin math cost × pct/(1−pct) is authoritative.
  9. Multi-tenancy\. ✓ RESOLVED (v2.15). Multi-tenant SaaS. org_id on every row with Postgres RLS; all Configure/Settings per-org; email ingest = <org-slug>@rfq.<domain>; file storage keyed per org.
  10. Rule application mode\. ✓ RESOLVED (v2.15). Suggest-only in v1 — resolutions are always one-click human actions (matches the demos). An auto_apply flag is reserved in the Rule model for later.
  11. Extraction/training pipeline\. ✓ RESOLVED (v2.15). Buy/API: Claude vision API powers all v1 extraction (no custom-trained model), with a per-org AI-processing toggle + automatic skip for CUI/ITAR-flagged files (manual-entry fallback). Corrections ("Mark as inaccurate" / "Add missing") are stored per-tenant as labeled data and drive prompt-pipeline regression evals, not fine-tuning. Default confidence threshold 0.7 — below it, findings render as click-to-accept suggestions. See Resolved Decisions → AI extraction.
  12. Configurable workflow steps\. ✓ RESOLVED (v2.15). Default set for new orgs = the estimator-centric Demo C set (Estimator · Material · DFM · Cycle Times · OSV · Purchased Components); orgs freely rename/reorder/add ("NEW STEP"). Rules reference steps by stable id, not name. Demo tenants are seeded with their demo-correct sets so acceptance scripts replay.
  13. CRM integration. ✓ DECIDED. Bid Factory is the system of record for all data (accounts, contacts, parts, quotes, pricing); it integrates two-way with the customer's CRM (HubSpot, Salesforce, SAP + generic adapter for German-manufacturing CRMs), pulling accounts/contacts in and pushing full quote + pricing + status + win/loss on send via webhooks. On conflict, BF wins. See CRM integration. v2.15: HubSpot is the first (and only v1) real adapter; Salesforce/SAP/generic stay mocked behind CrmAdapter. German-CRM list + conflict granularity still open (field-level recommended).
  14. Authentication & authorization. ✓ DECIDED. Login: email-first + SSO (passkey + identity providers) — see Authentication & SSO. Permissions: fixed-role RBAC, multi-role union, org-wide visibility, all see cost/margin, status-only workflow (no approval gate), export-controlled = flag+audit — full matrix in Authorization & Permissions. v2.15: Clerk — email-first + passkeys + Google/Microsoft IdPs, enterprise SAML/OIDC via Clerk when needed; only identity data at Clerk (DPA + subprocessor list); roles/permissions stay in the BF database. "View + annotate" scope and delete-by-owner confirmed as specced.
  15. Localization / currency. ✓ DECIDED. EUR only — no multi-currency. Multi-language roadmap: Phase 1 = English + German (en/de); later Italian, Spanish, Danish, Dutch, Austrian. Build locale-based i18n from the start. v2.15: GDPR = compliance-baseline package in v1 (EU residency, DPA + subprocessors, per-org export, erasure workflow, audit log, retention) — see Resolved Decisions. German strings Claude-generated against a manufacturing glossary (native review pre-pilot). Belgian locale ✓ RESOLVED (2026-06-21): dropped from the roadmap — DACH (DE/AT/CH) only.
